# Review findings for kiwi-server 2.1.0

Extracted from two background workflows run on 2026-10-05 against commit 48a724f.
Many verification agents never ran (API outage, then the session's usage limit), so a
finding marked *unverified* is a reviewer's claim that nobody tried to refute — read it
as a lead, not a verdict. Secrets were not copied; the examples use placeholder values.

### R1. kiwi-stack start deadlocks with host units ordered After=kiwi-stack.service (node-gw first boot hangs forever)

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:263
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: m1 (node-gw) first boot: ks_stack_apply runs `systemctl enable --now kiwi-stack.service` (lib.sh:358). Its ExecStart is `kiwi-stack start`, which after `compose up` runs `host_units restart` = `systemctl restart kn-gateway.service` (blocking, no --no-block). kn-gateway.service (modules/gateway/kn-gateway.service.j2:4) has `After=kiwi-stack.service`, so systemd will not run its start job while kiwi-stack.service's start job is still running — and that job is running precisely because its ExecStart is waiting on this systemctl call. With TimeoutStartSec=0 nothing ever times out: kiwi-stack.service never becomes active, kiwi-role.service never finishes, role.done is never written, kn-gateway never starts and multi-user.target (which gains After= on its WantedBy units) never reaches. The same deadlock recurs on every subsequent boot when kiwi-stack.service starts from multi-user.target. Verified by reading the unit template, the helper and systemd's job ordering (a start job waits for running jobs of units it is After=).
- **Proposed fix**: Do not restart host units from inside `start`: delete `host_units restart` from the `start)` branch (the units already have Wants=/After=kiwi-stack.service and are enabled by ks_stack_apply, so systemd starts them after the stack by itself), keep it only in `update)` where kiwi-stack.service is already active. If a restart from `start` is wanted, make it `systemctl --no-block restart "$u"`.

### R2. usermod -aG docker aborts the role on Fedora CoreOS (docker group lives only in /usr/lib/group)

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:88
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: A module role on target coreos (moby-engine ships `docker`, so ks_ensure_docker passes) reaches ks_stack_apply -> ks_add_to_group user docker / core docker. `getent group docker` succeeds because nss-altfiles reads /usr/lib/group, but shadow-utils `usermod` only edits /etc/group and fails with "usermod: group 'docker' does not exist" (documented FCOS behaviour: https://discussion.fedoraproject.org/t/installed-moby-engine-cannot-add-user-to-docker-group/37885). Under `set -e` the script dies before unpacking the stack; kiwi-role.service fails and is retried on every boot with the same result. Ignition's `groups: [docker]` for core works because Ignition handles altfiles, but the role script re-does it for core and additionally for the service user. uCore images built with docker-ce layered may carry the group in /etc/group; FCOS does not.
- **Proposed fix**: In ks_add_to_group, when the group is absent from /etc/group but present in /usr/lib/group, copy it first: `grep -q "^$2:" /etc/group || grep "^$2:" /usr/lib/group >> /etc/group 2>/dev/null || true`, then `usermod -aG "$2" "$1" || ks_warn "could not add $1 to $2"`. Skip the call for $KS_ADMIN_USER on ostree where Ignition already did it.

### R3. kiwi-server script writes ':: creating the fleet CA' / ':: issuing a certificate' to stdout before the script - the saved script is not executable

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:438
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Fresh checkout, secrets/ca does not exist yet (or the host cert is expired/renewed). `kiwi-server script fleet.yaml sh3 > sh3.sh` as the README recommends: cmd_script -> Renderer.script -> bundle -> tls_for -> certs.ensure_host_cert -> ensure_ca, which call util.say() (print to stdout, certs.py lines 40 and 74) BEFORE sys.stdout.write(script). Verified on a copy of /tmp/ks-review with secrets/ca removed: stdout begins with ':: creating the fleet CA in .../secrets/ca (keep kiwiCA.key private, import kiwiCA.pem on your devices)' and ':: issuing a certificate for sh3.kiwi and *.sh3.kiwi', the shebang is line 3. `sudo bash sh3.sh` then dies with a bash syntax error on line 1 ('(' inside a simple command) - the no-ISO first boot fails. The GUI's Show-script dialog (gui line 1073-1077) displays the same two lines as the first lines of 'the role script'. Second run is clean because the certificate is reused.
- **Proposed fix**: In cmd_script set util.QUIET = True (or route util.say to stderr when sys.stdout is not a tty / when the command's product is stdout): e.g. at the top of cmd_script: `util.QUIET = True`; better, make util.say print to sys.stderr for all commands so porcelain/script output is never mixed with progress lines. In the GUI, pass '--quiet' in show_script as a belt-and-braces measure.

### R4. Container addresses are built from the subnet's first three octets, so any non-/24-aligned docker_subnet yields addresses outside the network

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:231
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `_subnet_parts` (line 207-210) returns `base = network_address.rsplit('.',1)[0]` and `base_context` builds every address as `base.N`. Setting `node-cloud: { docker_subnet: 10.10.5.128/25 }` on sh3 renders ipv4_address 10.10.5.2/.5/.6/.7/.250 while the network is `10.10.5.128/25` (verified: rendered compose lines 20-138). `docker compose config -q` accepts it (rc=0, verified), so the error only appears on first boot when `compose up` refuses the fixed addresses. Any /25..../30 subnet, or one whose host part is not 0, hits this; .250/.251 cannot exist at all in a /25.
- **Proposed fix**: Compute addresses with ipaddress: `net = ipaddress.ip_network(spec.docker_subnet, strict=False); ip = net.network_address + IP_PLAN[name]`, then raise KiwiError("%s: docker_subnet %s is too small for the fixed addresses (.2-.10, .250, .251): use a /24" ...) when `ip not in net` or ip equals the broadcast/gateway. Apply the same `ip in net` check to every `container_ip` override.

### R5. tls.auto: false without tls_fullchain/tls_privkey renders a reverse proxy that mounts two files the bundle does not contain

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:147
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `tls_for` returns immediately when `tls.auto` is false and nothing else checks that a reverse-proxy host has certificates. With `defaults.tls.auto: false` and no reverse-proxy block, sh3 renders successfully (verified): the bundle holds only docker-compose.yml, nginx.conf, post-rules.txt, wg0.conf, while docker-compose.yml lines 49-50 bind-mount `/home/user/docker/kn-nginx/fullchain.pem` and `privkey.pem`. On first boot docker creates directories at those paths and nginx cannot load its certificate, so every web service (Nextcloud, Vaultwarden, Portainer) is unreachable with no error at render time.
- **Proposed fix**: In `tls_for`, after the `auto` check, if 'reverse-proxy' is in host.modules and not both files are in host.module_files, raise KiwiError("%s: reverse-proxy.tls_fullchain and tls_privkey are required when tls.auto is false" % host.name). Alternatively do the check in `Renderer.render` of modules.py: a file-type setting the compose fragment mounts must be present.

### R6. Isolated-client subnet rules are dead: appended after the wg0 accept-all

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/vpn-server/start.sh.j2`:19
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Master with the default isolated_subnet 10.8.1.0/24. start.sh appends `iptables -A FORWARD -i wg0 -j ACCEPT` (line 19, the stock wg-easy post-up) and only afterwards the ICMP drops, per-node ACCEPTs and `-s 10.8.1.0/24 -d 10.8.0.0/24 -j DROP` / `-d 10.8.1.0/24 -j DROP` (lines 25-32). Every packet from an isolated client arrives on wg0, matches rule 19 first and is accepted; the isolation rules are never evaluated. Rendered output/gate/gate.stack/km-vpn-server/start.sh:17 vs 22-28 shows the same order. An 'isolated' guest can ping and connect to every other client in 10.8.0.0/24 and 10.8.1.0/24, which the README and role.yaml say is prevented. (The live v1 start.sh has the same ordering bug.)
- **Proposed fix**: Emit the isolated-subnet block before the stock post-up (move lines 21-33 above line 15), or insert them with `-I` after the docker-subnet DROPs so they precede `-i wg0 -j ACCEPT`. Keep the docker-subnet DROPs (lines 10-13) where they are; they already precede the accept.

### R7. wg-easy admin UI (51821) and Pi-hole admin (80) reachable from the whole mesh, including isolated clients

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/vpn-server/start.sh.j2`:17
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Inside the wg-easy container the INPUT policy is docker's default ACCEPT; start.sh only adds ACCEPT rules (lines 17-18) and never a deny. wg-easy listens on 0.0.0.0:51821, Pi-hole (network_mode: service:vpn-server) on 0.0.0.0:80. The `127.0.0.1:51821:51821` port mapping in docker-compose.yml.j2:24 only limits the host side. Any mesh client reaches http://10.8.0.1:51821 (plain HTTP, password only, no lockout) and can create/download new peer configs; isolated clients are explicitly allowed to 10.8.0.1 (start.sh line 29), so a guest can mint itself a non-isolated 10.8.0.x peer. module.yaml:51 ('The web UI listens on 127.0.0.1:51821 of the master only') and apply.sh's message are false.
- **Proposed fix**: In start.sh.j2 add, before the ACCEPT rules: `iptables -A INPUT -i wg0 -p tcp --dport 51821 -j DROP` (and decide explicitly whether 10.8.0.1:80 Pi-hole admin should be mesh-reachable; if not, drop 80 on wg0 and keep 53/9050). Alternatively set WEBUI_HOST={{ container_ip }} if the image supports it. Fix the help text.

### R8. Master Pi-hole sets hostname together with network_mode service: — dockerd refuses to create the container

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/dns/docker-compose.yml.j2`:7
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Render the master role (dns with module_defaults network_mode: vpn-server, as roles/master/role.yaml sets). The template emits `hostname: pihole` unconditionally, so output/gate/gate.stack/docker-compose.yml has km-pihole with both `hostname: pihole` (line 70) and `network_mode: service:km-vpn-server` (line 77). On first boot `kiwi-stack start` -> `docker compose up` fails for km-pihole with the daemon error "conflicting options: hostname and the network mode" (the string is present in /usr/bin/dockerd; a container sharing another container's network namespace may not set its own hostname). `docker compose config` exits 0 on this file, so the CI check does not catch it. The master comes up with no DNS for the whole mesh (WG_DEFAULT_DNS=10.8.0.1 points at this container). The live kiwi-master compose had no `hostname:` on kmpihole; only the node-gw one (own network) had it.
- **Proposed fix**: Guard the line: `{% if not network_mode %}    hostname: pihole{% endif %}` (same for any other template that may be rendered with a shared namespace). Add a unit test that fails when a service has both `hostname` and `network_mode: service:`/`container:`.

### R9. nginx on a cloud node cannot start on first boot: static proxy_pass to nextcloud-aio-apache (live fix with resolver + variable dropped)

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/cloud/module.yaml`:39
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The cloud module announces `proxy_pass: http://nextcloud-aio-apache:11000` and the aggregator (modules/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2 line 88) writes it as a literal upstream name. nginx resolves literal proxy_pass hostnames at startup; `nextcloud-aio-apache` only exists after the operator finishes the AIO setup through nc-admin.<host>, so on a fresh sh3 kn-nginx exits with `host not found in upstream "nextcloud-aio-apache"` and restart-loops, taking nc-admin.sh3.kiwi, vault.sh3.kiwi and portainer.sh3.kiwi down with it. After AIO updates recreate the apache container nginx also keeps the stale IP. The live kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf (not the .example) carries exactly the fix: `resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=10s; resolver_timeout 5s; set $nc_aio_apache_hn nextcloud-aio-apache; proxy_pass http://$nc_aio_apache_hn:11000;` — the generated template regresses it. The live compose also had `depends_on: [knvpn-client, nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer, knvault]` on nginx; the generated kn-nginx has no depends_on at all.
- **Proposed fix**: Add a `resolve: true` key to the nginx server schema; when set, the aggregator emits inside the server block `resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=10s ipv6=off; resolver_timeout 5s;` and in the location `set $upstream_<n> <host>; proxy_pass http://$upstream_<n>:<port>;` instead of the literal name. Set it on the cloud module's apache block (and optionally the mastercontainer block). Also let the reverse-proxy fragment add `depends_on` for the compose services it proxies to.

### R10. Nextcloud AIO and Portainer mount docker.sock without label:disable — unusable on SELinux (ucore/coreos) hosts

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/cloud/docker-compose.yml.j2`:29
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: docs/modules.md states Fedora CoreOS/uCore run docker with SELinux enabled (hence the :z labels). The cloud fragment mounts `/var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock:ro` and the portainer fragment (modules/portainer/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 10) mounts it rw, neither with `security_opt: ["label:disable"]`. Under an enforcing container-selinux policy a container_t process is denied connecting to the socket, so on sh3 (ucore-hci in the example fleet) nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer cannot create the Nextcloud containers and Portainer shows no environment. Nextcloud AIO's own compose, kept verbatim in the live kiwi-cloud file at line 238, says `# security_opt: ["label:disable"] # Is needed when using SELinux`; the live hosts evidently were not SELinux hosts, the generated targets are.
- **Proposed fix**: Add `security_opt: ["label:disable"]` to the nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer and portainer services (harmless on Debian), or expose `target`/`selinux` in the template context and emit it for coreos/ucore. Mention in docs/modules.md that a docker.sock mount needs it.

### R11. kiwi-stack.service deadlocks on node-gw: `kiwi-stack start` restarts a host unit ordered After= itself from inside its own ExecStart, so first boot never finishes

- **Severity**: high · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:263
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Any node-gw host (m1 on every target): ks_stack_apply runs `systemctl enable --now kiwi-stack.service`. Its ExecStart is `kiwi-stack start`, which after `compose up` does `host_units restart` -> `systemctl restart kn-gateway.service` (no --no-block, so it waits for the job). kn-gateway.service (modules/gateway/kn-gateway.service.j2 line 4) has `After=docker.service kiwi-stack.service` + `Wants=kiwi-stack.service`, so its start job is queued behind the still-activating kiwi-stack.service start job, which cannot complete because its ExecStart is the `systemctl restart` that is waiting. TimeoutStartSec=0 on kiwi-stack.service means it never times out: kiwi-stack.service stays 'activating' forever, `systemctl enable --now` in the role script never returns, kiwi-role.service never writes role.done, and the same hang repeats on every subsequent boot (kiwi-stack.service is enabled). gate and sh3 are unaffected only because KS_STACK_UNITS is empty there.
- **Proposed fix**: Do not drive host units from inside kiwi-stack.service. Either (a) drop `host_units restart` from `start`/`update` and `host_units stop` from `stop`, and let the units' own ordering do it (they are already `After=kiwi-stack.service`, `WantedBy=multi-user.target`, enabled by the role; for `update` use `systemctl --no-block restart`), or (b) remove `After=kiwi-stack.service`/`Wants=kiwi-stack.service` from kn-gateway.service.j2 (gw.sh already waits up to 120 s for the VPN container), or at minimum call `systemctl --no-block "$1" "$u"` in host_units. Add a test that asserts no host unit is ordered After=kiwi-stack.service while kiwi-stack restarts it synchronously.

### R12. install.sh never installs the GUI on Debian/Ubuntu: GTK4 typelib probe misses the multiarch path

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/install.sh`:28
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: want_gui() only tests /usr/lib64/girepository-1.0/Gtk-4.0.typelib and /usr/lib/girepository-1.0/Gtk-4.0.typelib. On Debian and Ubuntu (verified on this Ubuntu 24.04 container: dpkg -L gir1.2-glib-2.0 puts typelibs under /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/girepository-1.0/) neither path exists, so ./install.sh install prints 'no GTK4 stack (or KIWI_GUI=0) — skipping the GUI' and kiwi-server-gui, the .desktop entry and the icon are never installed, although README.md line 56 ('~/.local/bin/kiwi-server, kiwi-server-gui, the library') and the manifest (COMPONENTS=cli gui config) say they are. A Debian desktop user with gir1.2-gtk-4.0 installed gets no app-grid entry unless they know to export KIWI_GUI=1.
- **Proposed fix**: Probe with python itself, which is what the GUI needs anyway: `python3 -c 'import gi; gi.require_version("Gtk","4.0"); gi.require_version("Adw","1")' 2>/dev/null`, or at least add a glob over the multiarch dir: `compgen -G '/usr/lib/*/girepository-1.0/Gtk-4.0.typelib' >/dev/null`.

### R13. updates.days: [] on CoreOS renders Zincati strategy=immediate, not 'any day inside the window'

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/targets/coreos.py`:51
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: README.md line 192 says '`updates.days: []` means any day' and the Updates table says CoreOS reboots 'only inside the window'. zincati_config() returns `strategy = "immediate"` when days is empty, which lets Zincati finalize and reboot at any time of day, ignoring updates.time and length_minutes entirely. The other two targets honour the doc: on_calendar([], time) in targets/__init__.py yields `*-*-* HH:MM:00` (daily at that time) for the uCore and Debian timers. A coreos host with `updates: { days: [], time: "03:30" }` therefore reboots whenever an update lands, e.g. mid-day.
- **Proposed fix**: When days is empty, emit a periodic window over all seven days: `days = [ "Mon", "Tue", "Wed", "Thu", "Fri", "Sat", "Sun" ]` with the configured start_time/length_minutes (Zincati accepts a full-week list), and reserve `immediate` for nothing or for an explicit opt-in. Add a TestCoreos case for days: [] asserting 'periodic' and no 'immediate'.

### R14. CA trust depends on render order: ca_cert() only embeds an existing kiwiCA.pem

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:165
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: README.md line 34 and CHANGELOG 2.1.0 promise the fleet CA 'is trusted on every machine built'. Renderer.ca_cert() returns None when secrets/ca/kiwiCA.pem does not exist yet; the CA is only created by tls_for() for hosts that run the reverse-proxy module, or by `kiwi-server ca`. On a fresh fleet, `kiwi-server build fleet.yaml lab1` (a bare host) or a fleet file that lists the bare/master hosts before the first node-cloud/node-gw host renders a role script without KS_FILES[ca_cert]; that machine is built and installed without the CA, and never gets it unless re-rendered after the CA exists. tests/cli.sh only passes its `grep KS_FILES[ca_cert] output/lab1/lab1.role.sh` check because lab1 is the last host in examples/fleet.yaml.
- **Proposed fix**: In ca_cert(), when tls.auto is true call certs.ensure_ca(ca_dir, days) (as tls_for does) and read the resulting pem, so the CA exists for every host regardless of order; alternatively create the CA once in Renderer.__init__ when any host has tls.auto. Add a unit test rendering a bare host alone from a fresh fleet and asserting KS_FILES[ca_cert] is present.

### R15. post_script is indented four spaces, which breaks any heredoc and leaves ks_main unreached

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/roles.py`:288
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: A host with `post_script: |` containing `cat > /etc/motd <<EOF … EOF` (reproduced by rendering a scratch fleet): every line is prefixed with four spaces, so the terminator becomes `    EOF` and never matches. bash reports "here-document delimited by end-of-file" then "syntax error: unexpected end of file" at the function's `}`; the script exits 2 before `ks_main "$@"` runs, so nothing is applied, role.done is never written and kiwi-role.service fails on every boot. shellcheck in the tests cannot catch it because it only sees the example fleet.
- **Proposed fix**: Emit the post_script body verbatim (no re-indentation) between `ks_post_script() {` and `}`; bash does not need the indentation. Optionally run `bash -n` on the rendered script in render() and fail with a clear message pointing at post_script.

### R16. Service user is never uid 1000, while the containers and absolute bind-mount dirs assume it

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:345
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: On every target uid 1000 is already the admin user (`core` from Ignition on CoreOS/uCore, the preseed's first user on Debian), so `ks_ensure_user user 1000` falls back to `useradd -m` and `user` becomes 1001. The rendered compose keeps the v1 defaults PUID/PGID=1000 (transmission, jdownloader) and `user:…:1000` (sftp), i.e. the containers run as the admin, not as the owner of the stack. ks_stack_unpack then chowns only the relative dirs to user:user (1001) and explicitly skips absolute ones (lib.sh:234), so /mnt/data/downloads on m1 and /mnt/nvme_2tb/docker/knnc-data on sh3 stay root:root 0755 (confirmed in a sandbox run). JDownloader's /output and the SFTP home are therefore not writable by the uid the containers run as; nothing in the stack owns the download directory at all.
- **Proposed fix**: Resolve the uid at first boot instead of guessing: after ks_ensure_user, `KS_STACK_UID=$(id -u "$u")`, chown the absolute bind-mount directories to `$u:$u` as well (only when the script created them), and have the generator render puid/pgid/sftp_uid from a single stack setting (`service_uid`, default empty = the service user's uid) that ks_ensure_user enforces with `useradd -u` — failing loudly if the uid is taken rather than silently taking the next free one.

### R17. --force re-run never re-applies the stack: enable --now is a no-op on the running service and replaced bind-mounted files keep the old inode

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:358
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The operator changes a module setting (Pi-hole password, nginx blocks, a new WireGuard config), re-renders and runs `sudo bash m1.role.sh --force` as the README suggests. ks_stack_unpack rewrites docker-compose.yml and the configs, but `systemctl enable --now kiwi-stack.service` returns immediately because the unit is already active, so `compose up` is never run again and the containers keep the old environment. Even a manual `kiwi-stack start` would not help for nginx.conf / wg0.conf / post-rules.txt: ks_file writes `$dest.tmp` and `mv`s it over the bind-mounted file, so the running container still sees the old inode until it is recreated.
- **Proposed fix**: After writing the units, if kiwi-stack.service is already active run `/usr/local/bin/kiwi-stack restart` (down + up recreates the containers and re-binds the files) instead of relying on `enable --now`; e.g. `if systemctl is-active -q kiwi-stack.service; then kiwi-stack restart; else systemctl enable --now kiwi-stack.service; fi`. Alternatively write bind-mounted files in place (`cat > "$dest"`) rather than by rename.

### R18. Modules entry rebuilds (and destroys) itself on every keystroke, so a module list cannot be typed

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:307
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Host sh3 (node-cloud) selected; the user clicks the 'Modules' Adw.EntryRow and types 'vpn-client reverse-proxy'. The row's 'changed' handler (line 361) calls Form.write -> path is (role, 'modules') -> rebuild_role_group(), which does self.page.remove(g) on the very group that holds the row being edited and creates a new row from the doc. After 'v' the focused row is gone, focus is lost and the user must click again for every character. Worse, from_text('list', 'vpn-client ') -> ['vpn-client'] and the new row shows to_text(...) = 'vpn-client' (verified: the trailing space is dropped), so after the next click the user types 'vpn-clientr' - a two-module list can never be entered from the keyboard; only pasting a complete string works. The README advertises exactly this feature ('changing the modules list changes the groups').
- **Proposed fix**: Do not tear down the row that is being edited. Keep the 'Modules' group out of self.role_group (store it in self.modules_group and only remove/rebuild the per-module groups that follow it), and only rebuild when the resolved module list actually changed: in write(), for the modules path compute new = self.effective_modules(role) and compare with the last rendered list before calling a rebuild_module_groups(). Alternatively connect to the EntryRow 'apply' signal (set_show_apply_button(True)) instead of 'changed' for this one row, so the groups are rebuilt once when the user confirms.

### R19. Build confirmation shows the disk from stale status: the 'disk it will wipe' can be the old value or '(no disk set)'

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:1055
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: User changes host m1's 'Install disk' from /dev/sda to /dev/nvme0n1 (or sets it for the first time) and clicks 'Build ISO'. confirm_build -> ensure_saved -> save() -> refresh_status() starts a background thread running `kiwi-server list --porcelain`; confirm_build then immediately reads self.status.get(n).get('disk') from the status captured before the edit and presents 'm1 -> /dev/sda' (or '(no disk set - the build will refuse)') while the build that follows wipes /dev/nvme0n1. The dialog's one purpose is to show the disk that gets wiped, and it shows the wrong one whenever the disk was edited since the last status refresh.
- **Proposed fix**: Take the disk from the document instead of the cached status: `disk = self.doc.get(n, ('disk',)) or self.doc.get('defaults', ('disk',))`; or make refresh_status accept a callback and present the dialog from apply_status once the fresh `list` result is in (and disable the Build buttons until then).

### R20. When kiwi-server is not found the error dialog is presented inside a window that is never shown - the app hangs invisibly

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:1181
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: kiwi-server-gui started while ~/.local/bin/kiwi-server is missing (GUI installed, CLI removed, or KIWI_SERVER_CLI pointing nowhere). find_cli() returns None; do_activate creates `tmp = Adw.ApplicationWindow(application=self)` but never calls tmp.present(), then `dlg.present(tmp)`: Adw.Dialog.present looks up the AdwDialogHost inside tmp and attaches the dialog there, so it is inside an unmapped window and nothing appears on screen. Because tmp is registered with the application, the GApplication use-count stays held and the process keeps running with no UI; the user sees nothing and has to kill it.
- **Proposed fix**: Present the window before the dialog (`tmp.present()` before `dlg.present(tmp)`), or present the dialog standalone with `dlg.present(None)` and call self.quit() in the response handler as now; or simply print the message to stderr and return nonzero when no window can be shown.

### R21. FleetDoc.save replaces the fleet file with a world-readable copy, dropping a user's 0600 mode on a file full of secrets

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:236
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The user keeps fleet.yaml (which holds wireguard_private_key, wg_password, pihole_password, transmission_password, the admin password in plain text) at mode 0600. Any Save in the GUI writes `fleet.yaml.tmp` with open(tmp, 'w') - created with the default umask, typically 0644 - and os.replace()s it over the original. The original's mode (and ownership/ACLs) is lost: the secrets are now readable by every local user. The README's own rule ('The output holds secrets ... written 0600') is defeated for the input file by the GUI.
- **Proposed fix**: Create the temp file with restrictive permissions and carry over the old mode: `fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL, 0o600)`; after writing, `try: os.chmod(tmp, stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(self.path).st_mode)) except FileNotFoundError: pass` (keep 0600 for a new file), then os.replace.

### R22. enable_vpn_dnat is computed without vpn_ip, producing `-d  -p tcp` iptables rules when the mesh address is empty

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:404
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `base['enable_vpn_dnat'] = bool(proxy_ip) and any(needs_vpn_dnat)` ignores `spec.vpn_ip`. The stack setting `vpn_ip` is not required (roles/common/stack.yaml line 24-29). Rendering sh3 (node-cloud) without `vpn_ip` succeeds and kn-vpn-client/post-rules.txt lines 8-9 read `iptables -t nat -A PREROUTING -d  -p tcp --dport 443 -j DNAT --to-destination 172.128.0.5:443` (verified), which iptables rejects (`-p` is parsed as the host for `-d`); FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS=80,443 is still set. gluetun's post-rules step fails on the first boot and the node's mesh access never works, with no diagnostic at render time. `extra_dnat_rules` (post-rules.txt.j2 line 18) has the same hole.
- **Proposed fix**: Make vpn_ip part of the condition and fail loudly: in `render()`, if any module has `needs_vpn_dnat` (or `extra_dnat_rules` is non-empty) and `not spec.vpn_ip`, raise KiwiError("%s: vpn_ip is required — %s is reachable at the mesh address" % (spec.hostname, module)). At minimum set `enable_vpn_dnat = bool(proxy_ip) and bool(spec.vpn_ip) and any(...)` and warn.

### R23. The reverse-proxy `hsts` setting is dead: per-server hsts is coerced to bool so the template can never fall back to the module setting

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:356
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `nginx_blocks` stores `"hsts": bool(e.get("hsts", False))`, so in nginx.conf.j2 line 74 `s.hsts or (hsts and s.hsts is not sameas false)` reduces to `s.hsts`. With the default `hsts: true` the rendered sh3 nginx.conf has Strict-Transport-Security only in the Nextcloud block (module.yaml says hsts: true there) and none for Vaultwarden, Portainer (verified, lines 76 vs 125-167); with `reverse-proxy: { hsts: false }` the Nextcloud header is still emitted (verified: count 1). The user-facing setting 'Send HSTS headers' changes nothing either way.
- **Proposed fix**: Keep the tri-state: in nginx_blocks use `"hsts": e.get("hsts")` (None when the module did not say), and in nginx.conf.j2 write `{% if s.hsts is sameas true or (s.hsts is none and hsts) %}`.

### R24. Hosts rendered before the first reverse-proxy host on a fresh fleet do not get the fleet CA embedded

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:164
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `ca_cert()` only reads `secrets/ca/kiwiCA.pem` if it already exists; the CA is created lazily by `tls_for -> certs.ensure_host_cert` when a reverse-proxy host is rendered. On a checkout without secrets/ca, `kiwi-server render fleet.yaml` renders gate (master, no reverse-proxy) first: gate.role.sh has no `KS_FILES[ca_cert]=` line while m1, sh3 and lab1 have it (verified: grep counts 1 vs 2; the only match in gate is lib.sh's ks_trust_ca). The master therefore does not trust the nodes' certificates although README promises 'every machine built trusts it'; a second render fixes it silently, so the first ISO built is the wrong one.
- **Proposed fix**: Create the CA up front: in `Renderer.__init__` (or at the top of `ca_cert`) call `certs.ensure_ca(ca_dir, days)` whenever `tls.auto` is true and any host in the fleet has 'reverse-proxy' in its resolved modules (iterate `self.fleet.hosts`, `prepare(h)` is already done in `dns_records`).

### R25. A user-supplied tls_fullchain (or tls_privkey) given alone is silently replaced by the CA-issued certificate

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:150
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `tls_for` returns only when BOTH 'reverse-proxy/tls_fullchain' and 'reverse-proxy/tls_privkey' are in module_files; otherwise it overwrites both entries. With `reverse-proxy: { tls_fullchain: secrets/my-fullchain.pem }` on sh3 the render succeeds and kn-nginx/fullchain.pem in the bundle is the fleet-CA certificate, the user's file is nowhere (verified: MYOWNCERT count 0). The operator who brought a public certificate gets an internal one with no message.
- **Proposed fix**: After the both-present check add: `if ("reverse-proxy/tls_fullchain" in mf) != ("reverse-proxy/tls_privkey" in mf): raise KiwiError("%s: reverse-proxy needs both tls_fullchain and tls_privkey, or neither" % host.name)`.

### R26. A null-valued module setting (`key:` with nothing after it) bypasses the default and renders the literal string None

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/roles.py`:106
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `block.get(s.key, s.default)` returns None for a YAML null and `coerce` (schema.py line 340-341) returns None unchanged, so the setting's default is not applied and no error is raised. `cloud: { apache_port: }` on sh3 renders `APACHE_PORT=None` in docker-compose.yml and `proxy_pass http://nextcloud-aio-apache:None;` in nginx.conf (verified) — nginx refuses the config on first boot. The same happens for every int/string setting; a null `extra_records`/`countries` raises a bare template error instead.
- **Proposed fix**: In `_resolve_block`: `v = block.get(s.key); if v is None: v = s.default` before coercing (and treat None as 'required missing' only via is_empty on the result).

### R27. container_ip overrides are not validated: duplicates and out-of-subnet values are accepted and only fail on first boot

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/roles.py`:156
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `vals["container_ip"] = str(block["container_ip"])` and `base_context` (modules.py line 228-229) use the value as-is. `vault: { container_ip: 172.128.0.6 }` (cloud's address) renders two services with `ipv4_address: 172.128.0.6` (verified, compose lines 80 and 97) and README.txt lists both; `docker compose config -q` passes, `compose up` fails on the host. A non-IP string or an address outside docker_subnet is accepted the same way.
- **Proposed fix**: In `base_context`, after building `ips`, parse each with `ipaddress.ip_address`, check it is inside the subnet, and raise KiwiError("%s: container_ip %s is used by both %s and %s") on duplicates; validate the format already in `resolve_settings`.

### R28. gw.sh: LAN-to-docker-subnet DROP is shadowed by the later -I ACCEPT

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/gateway/gw.sh.j2`:56
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: All rules use `-I` (insert at head), so the final FORWARD order is the reverse of the script: `-i LAN -o knet-node -j ACCEPT` (line 58) ends up before `-i LAN -d DOCKER_SUBNET -j DROP` (line 56). Table 128 has `throw DOCKER_SUBNET`, so a LAN client sending to 172.128.0.x is routed out knet-node by the main table and accepted by rule 58 before the DROP. LAN clients reach the docker subnet directly: Portainer http://172.128.0.250:9000, gluetun's control server 172.128.0.2:8000, kn-vault:80, Transmission/JDownloader via 172.128.0.2:9091/5800, contradicting the script's own comment ('no access to the docker network'). The v1 gw.sh has the same bug.
- **Proposed fix**: Insert the DROP last (so it is first), or use `-A` with explicit ordering: put `iptables -I FORWARD -i $PUB_IFACE -d $DOCKER_SUBNET -j DROP` after the two ACCEPT inserts (and mirror that in rules_del).

### R29. Built ISOs are written world-readable although they carry every secret

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:249
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: render() chmods the role script, .bu/.ign, preseed and kiwi-server dir to 0600/0700, but build() (cli.py:249-260) and iso.py:458/597 (coreos-installer, xorriso -outdev) create <host>.iso with the default umask, i.e. 0644, inside output/<host>/ which os.makedirs creates 0755 (verified: output/* are 755). The ISO contains the role script (WireGuard private keys, wg-easy/Pi-hole/Transmission/SFTP passwords, nginx privkey.pem), the admin password hash and, for debian encrypt, the LUKS passphrase in clear. Any local user on the build machine can read it; README claims 'Files are written 0600'.
- **Proposed fix**: After isomod.coreos_iso/debian_iso return in build(), `os.chmod(o.iso, 0o600)`; also create o.dir with mode 0o700 in render().

### R30. JDownloader web UI has no authentication and is exposed to mesh and LAN through nginx

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/downloader/docker-compose.yml.j2`:25
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The jlesage/jdownloader-2 container is started without WEB_AUTHENTICATION (default off), nginx proxies jd.<host> to kn-vpn-client:5800 with no auth_basic (module.yaml:41-45), the vpn-client DNATs 10.8.0.6:443 to nginx, and the master forwards every mesh client (isolated ones included, start.sh line 29-30) to 10.8.0.6; every Pi-hole in the fleet resolves jd.m1.kiwi. Any mesh or LAN client gets the full JDownloader GUI (add downloads, browse/copy files in download_dir) with no credentials.
- **Proposed fix**: Add a `jd_password` secret setting and set `WEB_AUTHENTICATION=1`, `WEB_AUTHENTICATION_USERNAME`, `WEB_AUTHENTICATION_PASSWORD` in the compose fragment, or put `auth_basic` on the jd server block via extra_directives.

### R31. Portainer (rw docker socket) and the other admin panels are reachable from every mesh client via the DNAT'd proxy; unattended boot leaves the initial-admin page open

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/portainer/docker-compose.yml.j2`:10
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: kn-portainer mounts /var/run/docker.sock read-write (root on the host) and nginx announces portainer.<host> (module.yaml:33-40). On nodes the vpn-client DNATs 80/443 at the mesh address to nginx (post-rules.txt.j2:13-14) and the master allows isolated clients to every node (start.sh.j2:27-30), so a guest in 10.8.1.0/24 can open https://portainer.m1.kiwi. The role script starts the stack on first boot and 'walks away'; until the operator creates the Portainer admin, whoever connects first (LAN or mesh) creates it and owns the docker socket. The same DNAT exposes the Pi-hole and Transmission admin logins to isolated guests.
- **Proposed fix**: Do not publish Portainer through the reverse proxy by default (keep 127.0.0.1:9443 only, e.g. `nginx: when: portainer_public` with default false), or restrict the DNAT'd vhosts for admin panels with an nginx `allow 10.8.0.0/24; deny all;` (exclude the isolated subnet) via extra_directives. Document that the Portainer admin must be created right after first boot.

### R32. Pi-hole admin UI published on 0.0.0.0:8080 in plain HTTP on gateway nodes

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/dns/docker-compose.yml.j2`:29
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: roles/node-gw/role.yaml:17 sets expose_web_ui: true, rendering `8080:80/tcp` without a bind address (output/m1/m1.stack/docker-compose.yml:48). The host firewall list (open_ports) does not include it, but docker publishes the port regardless, so every LAN client gets the Pi-hole admin login over unencrypted HTTP while the same UI is already served over TLS at pihole.m1.kiwi. Credentials cross the LAN in clear.
- **Proposed fix**: Render `"127.0.0.1:{{ web_ui_port }}:80/tcp"` (matching the master's 127.0.0.1:8080), or drop `expose_web_ui: true` from the node-gw preset since the reverse proxy serves the UI.

### R33. Nextcloud AIO master container (holds docker.sock) published on 0.0.0.0:8080

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/cloud/docker-compose.yml.j2`:9
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `ports: - "8080:8080"` publishes the AIO admin interface on every host interface although nginx already proxies it at nc-admin.<host> (module.yaml:51-54). The container mounts /var/run/docker.sock (ro mount does not prevent socket use), so the AIO interface is a root-equivalent control panel reachable from the whole LAN on a second, unproxied port.
- **Proposed fix**: Publish `"127.0.0.1:8080:8080"` (nginx reaches the container over the docker network, not via the host port).

### R34. Fleet CA has no name constraints, issues 10-year leaf certs, and is trusted system-wide on every host including bare ones

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/certs.py`:44
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: ensure_ca creates an unconstrained CA (basicConstraints CA:TRUE, no pathlen, no nameConstraints) and ensure_host_cert signs leaves with the same 3650 days (line 86). lib.sh:367-378 installs it into /etc/pki/ca-trust or /usr/local/share/ca-certificates, and cli.ca_cert() embeds it for every host, including role bare (lab1.role.sh line 28). Whoever obtains secrets/ca/kiwiCA.key (a 0600 file beside fleet.yaml on the operator's machine) can forge any name — e.g. download.docker.com during ks_docker_ce (lib.sh:115), ghcr.io / docker.io image pulls, Debian HTTPS mirrors — on every machine the fleet built, not just *.kiwi.
- **Proposed fix**: Add `-addext "nameConstraints=critical,permitted;DNS:.<domain>"` (the fleet default domain, e.g. .kiwi) and `pathlen:0` when creating the CA; use a separate, shorter leaf lifetime (e.g. 825 days) and re-issue from cert_valid(); only embed the CA for hosts that run a stack or that the operator opts in.

### R35. Root-executed stack files are owned by the unprivileged service user, which is also put in the docker group

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:230
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: ks_stack_unpack writes every stack file as `$u:$u` (line 230) and chowns the directories to the service user (232-235). kn-gateway.service.j2:11 runs `bash /home/user/docker/kiwi/gw.sh` as root, and /usr/local/bin/kiwi-stack (root, weekly timer) runs `docker compose --file $STACK_DIR/docker-compose.yml`. Anything that gets uid `user` (a process escaping one of the containers that run with PUID=1000/1001 and write to user-owned bind mounts) can edit gw.sh or docker-compose.yml and run as root on the next timer. Line 347 additionally adds `user` to the docker group although nothing runs compose as that user anymore.
- **Proposed fix**: Install docker-compose.yml, the host unit scripts (kiwi/gw.sh) and the module config files root:root (service data directories may stay user-owned); drop `ks_add_to_group "$u" docker`.

### R36. Service user gets uid 1001 (admin takes 1000) while PUID/sftp_uid default to 1000: downloads unwritable

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: security · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:80
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The admin user created by the preseed/Ignition is the first user and gets uid 1000 on every target (core is 1000 on CoreOS, d-i gives the first user 1000). ks_ensure_user `user 1000` sees uid 1000 taken and creates `user` as 1001. ks_stack_unpack then chowns download_dir and the module dirs to user (1001) with mode 0755, while modules/downloader/module.yaml:79-84 and sftp_uid default to 1000, so Transmission/JDownloader run as 1000 and get EACCES writing /downloads and /output; SFTP uploads land as uid 1000. On the live v1 hosts `user` was 1000, so the preset silently breaks migrated and fresh machines alike.
- **Proposed fix**: Derive puid/pgid/sftp_uid from the actual service user at apply time (chown with `id -u $u`), or resolve the defaults to the service user's uid in the renderer and chown download_dir to that uid; at minimum warn when uid 1000 is not free.

### R37. Fleet DNS records written to hosts/custom.list, which Pi-hole 6 regenerates from pihole.toml — records never take effect on a fresh host

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/dns/module.yaml`:29
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: custom.list.j2 is rendered to `<prefix>-pihole/etc-pihole/hosts/custom.list` ("Pi-hole reads this as its local DNS records"). In Pi-hole 6 that path is an output file: the live kmpihole/etc-pihole/hosts/custom.list header reads "FILE AUTOMATICALLY POPULATED BY PI-HOLE — ANY CHANGES MADE TO THIS FILE WILL BE LOST WHEN THE CONFIGURATION CHANGES — change them in /etc/pihole/pihole.toml", and the live master's records actually live in pihole.toml `[dns] hosts = [...]`. A fresh host has an empty dns.hosts, so FTL's first start rewrites the file with only the header: no host in the fleet resolves gate.kiwi, cloud.sh3.kiwi, vault.sh3.kiwi, pihole.m1.kiwi …, which the README advertises as a core feature, and the master's isolated-client ACCEPT rules reference addresses clients cannot look up.
- **Proposed fix**: Emit the records as the v6 env variable in the dns compose fragment: `FTLCONF_dns_hosts={{ (fleet records + extra_records) | map('join',' ') | join(';') }}` (entries "IP name", `;`-separated), and drop or keep the file purely as a reviewable copy. Keep `legacy_env` writing /etc/pihole/custom.list for v5 images.

### R38. Daily VPN restart on nodes leaves Transmission/JDownloader in a dead network namespace

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:269
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `kiwi-stack vpn-restart` runs `docker restart $STACK_VPN_CONTAINER` only, and roles/common/stack.yaml defaults `daily_vpn_restart` to 07:00 for every module role. On node-gw, kn-transmission and kn-jdownloader run with `network_mode: service:kn-vpn-client`; when the gluetun container restarts it gets a new network namespace and the dependents keep the old, now empty one, so every morning the downloaders (and nginx's dl./jd. proxies to kn-vpn-client:9091/5800) stop working until those containers are restarted by hand. The live setups avoided exactly this: the node crons have `#01 07 * * * user docker restart knvpn-client` commented out and instead restart kiwi-gw (node-gw) or the whole stack (`kiwi-node.sh restart` on cloud); only the master, where nothing shares gluetun's namespace, restarted the client alone.
- **Proposed fix**: Have the renderer emit `KS_STACK_VPN_DEPENDENTS` (services whose network_mode is `service:<vpn>`), store it in stack.env and make vpn-restart do `compose restart <vpn> <dependents>` (or `compose up -d --force-recreate` them). Alternatively default `daily_vpn_restart` to empty for node roles.

### R39. dhcp-relay forwards to a Pi-hole whose DHCP server cannot be enabled — live node-gw was the LAN's DHCP server

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/dns/module.yaml`:51
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The live kiwi-node-gw pihole.toml has `[dhcp] active = true`, `start = 192.168.2.100`, `end = 192.168.2.199`, `router = 192.168.2.20`, `multiDNS = true`; kndhcphelper exists to relay broadcasts to it. The dns module's settings offer no DHCP options and the compose fragment emits no FTLCONF_dhcp_* variables, so a generated m1 runs kn-dhcphelper relaying to a Pi-hole with DHCP off. On a LAN where the router's DHCP was handed to the node (the live configuration), clients get no leases and the "LAN DNS (Pi-hole + DHCP relay)" role promise is empty.
- **Proposed fix**: Add dns settings `dhcp_enabled` (bool), `dhcp_start`, `dhcp_end`, `dhcp_router`, `dhcp_lease_time`, `dhcp_multi_dns` (default true) rendered as `FTLCONF_dhcp_active`, `FTLCONF_dhcp_start`, `FTLCONF_dhcp_end`, `FTLCONF_dhcp_router`, `FTLCONF_dhcp_leaseTime`, `FTLCONF_dhcp_multiDNS`; make dhcp-relay fail validation unless dns.dhcp_enabled is true.

### R40. Renamed data directories and a different cloud subnet break a machine migrated in place (wg-easy peers, Vaultwarden, Transmission data)

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: stacks-vs-live · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/vpn-server/docker-compose.yml.j2`:37
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: README/docs say the stacks are the live setups and "Addresses follow the v1 plan so a migrated machine keeps them". The bind mounts do not: vpn-server mounts `<dir>/km-vpn-server:/etc/wireguard` where the live mount is `kmvpn-server` (holds wg0.json with every client/node peer) — running the role script on the live master starts an empty wg-easy and every client loses access; likewise `km-pihole`/`kn-pihole` vs `kmpihole`/`knpihole`, `kn-vault:/data` vs `knvault/`, `kn-transmission:/config` vs `kntransmission/config`, `km-tor/var` vs `kmtor/var`. The cloud node's live plan was 172.129.0.0/24 with nginx .3, AIO master .4, vault .5, portainer .250, while node-cloud renders 172.128.0.0/24 with nginx .5, AIO .6, vault .7. Default service names also changed (dl./jd. vs the live transmission./jdownloader.).
- **Proposed fix**: Either add a `data_dir` setting per module whose default is the v1 name (kmvpn-server, kmpihole, knvault, kntransmission/config …) so a hyphenated name is opt-in, or add a migration step to ks_stack_unpack that renames the v1 directory to the new one when the old exists and the new does not, and state in docs/modules.md that node-cloud lived on 172.129.0.0/24 (set `docker_subnet` accordingly).

### R41. Daily `vpn-restart` does `docker restart` of gluetun and strands Transmission/JDownloader in the dead network namespace

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:269
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: node-gw with the default daily_vpn_restart 07:00: kiwi-stack-vpn-restart.timer runs `docker restart kn-vpn-client`. kn-transmission and kn-jdownloader use `network_mode: service:kn-vpn-client` (rendered m1 compose lines 96 and 116); when the gluetun container is restarted it gets a new network sandbox while the joined containers keep the old, torn-down namespace (no veth, no tun0). Both downloaders lose all connectivity every morning and only come back at the weekly `kiwi-stack update` (compose recreates dependents) or a manual `kiwi-stack restart`. The live v1 setups never did this: kiwi-master's cron restarted a container nothing joins, kiwi-node-gw restarted kiwi-gw, kiwi-cloud restarted the whole stack.
- **Proposed fix**: In the `vpn-restart` case recreate the VPN service through compose so its namespace sharers are recreated with it: `compose up --detach --force-recreate "$STACK_VPN_CONTAINER"` (compose v2 recreates services whose network_mode points at a recreated service), or have the generator emit STACK_VPN_DEPENDENTS (services with network_mode: service:<vpn>) into stack.env and `docker restart` them after the VPN container.

### R42. Zincati: `updates.days: []` turns into strategy=immediate, so plain CoreOS reboots at any hour instead of daily at updates.time

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/targets/coreos.py`:50
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: A coreos host with `updates: { days: [], time: "03:30", length_minutes: 90 }` (README line 192 and examples/fleet.yaml line 55 say `[]` = any day). zincati_config() emits `strategy = "immediate"`, which finalizes and reboots as soon as an update is staged, at any time of day; `time` and `length_minutes` are ignored. On ucore and debian the same setting yields `OnCalendar=*-*-* 03:30:00` (on_calendar with no days), i.e. daily inside the window as documented — the three targets disagree and the one that differs reboots a running stack in the middle of the day.
- **Proposed fix**: When `days` is empty, keep the periodic strategy and list all seven weekdays: `days = u["days"] or list(config.DAYS)` before building the `[[updates.periodic.window]]` block; reserve `immediate` for an explicit setting if you want one.

### R43. Portainer and Nextcloud AIO mount /var/run/docker.sock with no `security_opt: label:disable`; on Fedora CoreOS (moby-engine runs with --selinux-enabled) the container is denied the socket

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/modules/portainer/docker-compose.yml.j2`:10
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: node-cloud or node-gw on target coreos (also ucore if its docker daemon has selinux-enabled): Fedora's moby-engine starts dockerd with `--selinux-enabled` from /etc/sysconfig/docker, so processes in kn-portainer and nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer run as container_t and are refused connectto on the docker socket. Portainer reports it cannot reach the local Docker environment; the AIO master container cannot create the nextcloud-aio-* containers, so the cloud role comes up without Nextcloud. The live kiwi-cloud compose carries AIO's own hint for exactly this (`# security_opt: ["label:disable"] # Is needed when using SELinux`); docs/modules.md notes the socket must not be labelled but offers no alternative. Same line in modules/cloud/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 29.
- **Proposed fix**: Add `security_opt: ["label:disable"]` to the portainer and cloud service fragments (harmless on Debian, where docker ignores SELinux labels), or expose the target/os in the template context and emit it for coreos/ucore; keep the socket mount itself unlabelled.

### R44. Absolute data directories the stack creates (download_dir, sftp_dir) are root:root 0755; JDownloader (USER_ID 1000) and the SFTP user (uid 1000) cannot write to them

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:226
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: m1 (node-gw) with `download_dir: /mnt/data/downloads` on a fresh machine: ks_stack_unpack runs `install -d -m 0755 /mnt/data/downloads` (absolute paths are never chowned; the loop at line 233 skips them) so the directory is root-owned. kn-jdownloader runs as USER_ID=1000/GROUP_ID=1000 with `/mnt/data/downloads:/output` and kn-sftp creates its user with uid 1000 on `/home/user/Downloads` — every download/upload fails with permission denied. Note also that uid 1000 is the admin user (`core`) on all three targets, not the service user: ks_ensure_user hands `user` uid 1001 because core already holds 1000, so `puid`/`sftp_uid` default 1000 points at the wrong account.
- **Proposed fix**: In ks_stack_unpack, chown absolute directories that did not exist before to the stack's puid:pgid (export them from the generator as KS_STACK_DATA_UID/GID, defaulting to the downloader's puid) — e.g. `[[ -d $d ]] || { install -d -m 0775 "$d"; chown "$uid:$gid" "$d"; }` — and either default puid/sftp_uid to the service user's uid resolved at render time, or document that 1000 is the admin user.

### R45. The Debian base ISO is always `debian-cd/current`, ignoring `debian.release`; a fleet that sets another release gets a mismatched installer/suite

- **Severity**: medium · **lens**: targets-and-updates · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/iso.py`:24
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `defaults.debian.release: bookworm` (a documented knob: 'release: trixie' in the example) with no iso_url: debian_base_iso() downloads whatever `current` points at (today the trixie 13.x netinst) while preseed() writes `d-i mirror/suite string bookworm` (targets/debian.py line 63). The trixie d-i installs with bookworm's mirror suite: base-installer's kernel/udeb set does not match the suite, and the unattended install stops or produces a mixed system. Nothing validates release against the ISO actually fetched.
- **Proposed fix**: Derive the expected codename from the resolved netinst file name (debian-13.* -> trixie) and refuse to build when `debian.release` differs unless `iso_url` is given; or map known codenames to cdimage paths (current for stable, cdimage.debian.org/cdimage/archive/<ver>/ for older) and validate the suite in `validate`.

### R46. Bash completion does not know the `ca` and `modules` commands or the -v flag

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/data/bash-completion/kiwi-server`:7
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: cmds= lists 'init validate list show render build script roles targets toolchain doctor version help' and opts= lists '--output-dir --cache --toolchain --image --porcelain --quiet --verbose --help --version'. The CLI (cli.py build_parser / USAGE) also has `ca <fleet>` and `modules [-v]`, which README.md and CHANGELOG 2.1.0 advertise (`kiwi-server ca fleet.yaml`, `kiwi-server modules -v`). Typing `kiwi-server c<TAB>` or `kiwi-server mo<TAB>` completes nothing, and `kiwi-server ca <TAB>` does not offer fleet files because the per-verb case has no `ca` entry.
- **Proposed fix**: Add `ca modules` to cmds, `-v` to opts, and `ca` to the `validate|list|show|render|build|script)` file-completion case (ca takes only a fleet, so no host completion is needed).

### R47. CHANGELOG names coreos.systemd_units; the code reads coreos.units and silently ignores the documented key

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/CHANGELOG.md`:76
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The 2.0.0 entry says the per-server Butane extras '`files`, `directories`, `systemd_units`, `services`, `kernel_arguments`, `luks`, `boot_device` live on under `coreos:`'. config.DEFAULTS and lib/kiwiserver/targets/coreos.py line 195 use the key `units` (`for unit in co.get("units")`), and Host.validate() only type-checks files/directories/units, so a fleet with `coreos: { systemd_units: [ {name: foo.service, enabled: true, contents: ...} ] }` validates cleanly and the unit is simply absent from the Butane output; a 1.x user migrating by the changelog gets no error and no unit.
- **Proposed fix**: Change the CHANGELOG (and the GUI/README if desired) to `units`, and in Host.validate() reject unknown keys under coreos: (e.g. compare against DEFAULTS['coreos'] and report 'coreos.systemd_units: unknown key (did you mean units?)').

### R48. docs/modules.md documents firewall.scope host/self, but the renderer drops every rule whose scope is not vpn-client

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/docs/modules.md`:72
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The module.yaml reference says `scope: vpn-client (collected into gluetun's post-rules), host (gw.sh), self (its own container)`. modules.py vpn_rules() (line 381: `if mod.firewall_scope != "vpn-client" or name == "vpn-client": continue`) is the only consumer of firewall.rules; nothing reads rules with scope host or self, and gw.sh.j2 hard-codes its iptables lines (modules/gateway/module.yaml ships `scope: host, rules: []`). A module author who follows the doc and writes `firewall: { scope: host, rules: ["-A FORWARD ..."] }` gets a stack where those rules never appear anywhere, with no warning.
- **Proposed fix**: Either document that only `scope: vpn-client` rules are consumed (host rules belong in a module's own script/template) or implement the other scopes: feed scope=host rules into the gateway script/host unit and raise a KiwiError in Renderer.render() for non-empty rules with an unsupported scope.

### R49. Fleet-wide DNS record aggregation (cli.Renderer.dns_records) is not covered by the unit tests

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/tests/test_kiwiserver.py`:508
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: CHANGELOG 2.1.0 promises 'every Pi-hole serves every host and service name at its mesh address'. TestModules.spec() hands a hand-written list `[("10.8.0.25", "sh3.kiwi"), ...]` to stack_spec(), so the only code that builds the records from the fleet — cli.Renderer.dns_records(): hostnames of every stack host, the master's vpn_ip taken from vpn-server.server_ip, wildcard names filtered, invalid hosts skipped with a warning — is never executed under assertion. TestCli.test_validate_render_list_show renders a fleet with no dns module, so no custom.list is produced there either. A regression that, for example, dropped the master's gate.kiwi record (vpn_ip comes from server_ip, not role settings) or re-added `*.sh3.kiwi` would pass the suite and tests/cli.sh.
- **Proposed fix**: Add a TestCli case rendering a fleet with a master (dns), a node-gw and a node-cloud through cli.main(['render', ...]) and assert that output/<gw>/<gw>.stack/kn-pihole/etc-pihole/hosts/custom.list contains `10.8.0.1 gate.kiwi`, `10.8.0.25 cloud.sh3.kiwi`, `10.8.0.6 m1.kiwi` and no `*.` entry, and that the master's km-pihole list holds the node records.

### R50. KS_STACK_NO_RESOLVED_STUB and the embedded CA are set only in cli.Renderer and never asserted by a unit test

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/tests/test_kiwiserver.py`:100
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: CHANGELOG 2.1.0 promises 'systemd-resolved's stub listener turned off where Pi-hole needs port 53' and the CA 'trusted on every machine built'. bundle.no_resolved_stub is computed in cli.Renderer.bundle() (cli.py line 174) and the CA is passed as render_script(..., ca_cert=...) from cli.Renderer.script(); the test helpers full_script()/render() call modmod.Renderer.render() and rolesmod.render_script() directly, so every script the unit tests produce carries KS_STACK_NO_RESOLVED_STUB=0 and no KS_FILES[ca_cert], and no test asserts either value. Deleting line 174 or the ca_cert argument would not fail `python3 -m unittest`; only the cli.sh grep for lab1 would notice the CA, and nothing would notice the stub flag.
- **Proposed fix**: In TestCli, render a node-gw host via cli.main(['render', ...]) and assert `KS_STACK_NO_RESOLVED_STUB=1` in its role.sh, `KS_STACK_NO_RESOLVED_STUB=0` for the node-cloud host, and `KS_FILES[ca_cert]=` in both; or move the no_resolved_stub computation into modmod.Renderer.render() so the existing bundle tests can assert it.

### R51. The kiwi-stack helper inside a quoted heredoc is never linted or tested

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:252
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: CHANGELOG 2.1.0 and README list `kiwi-stack` (start|stop|restart|update|status|logs|vpn-restart) as a deliverable. It is written by ks_stack_install_cli from a `<<'KIWI_STACK'` heredoc; shellcheck treats a quoted heredoc as opaque data, so test_bash_parses_and_shellcheck_passes_every_role (line 229) and the CI shellcheck of roles/common/lib.sh never parse it, and no test extracts it, runs `bash -n` on it, or exercises its sub-commands. (Extracted by hand today it is clean — the gap is the missing guard: a future syntax error in that block would ship in every role script and only surface on a machine's first boot.)
- **Proposed fix**: Add a unit test that slices the heredoc body out of common_lib() (between `<<'KIWI_STACK'` and the terminating `KIWI_STACK` line), writes it to a file, and runs `bash -n` plus shellcheck -S warning on it; optionally also run it with a stubbed `docker` on PATH and a temporary stack.env to check each verb's usage path.

### R52. test_init_and_example_fleet_validates never validates the example fleet

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: docs-and-tests · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/tests/test_kiwiserver.py`:749
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The test's name promises that examples/fleet.yaml validates, but the body only runs `init`, checks the file exists and checks a second `init` refuses to overwrite. The README's 'full reference is the commented examples/fleet.yaml' is therefore guarded only by tests/cli.sh (which does run validate after creating ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub and secrets/*.conf). A change to the example that adds an unknown module key or a misspelt setting passes `python3 -m unittest` and is caught only by the smoke test.
- **Proposed fix**: After init, write the referenced files the example needs (an ssh key file at HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub with HOME pointed at self.tmp, and secrets/m1.kiwi.conf, secrets/sh3.kiwi.conf) and assert cli.main(['validate', dest]) returns 0; or rename the test to test_init_refuses_overwrite so the name stops claiming coverage it does not provide.

### R53. ks_file's install -d -m 0755 widens the mode of existing parent directories (stack dir 0750 becomes 0755)

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: firstboot-bash · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/roles/common/lib.sh`:46
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: ks_stack_unpack creates $KS_STACK_DIR with `install -d -m 0750`, then the first ks_file for stack/docker-compose.yml calls `install -d -m 0755 "$(dirname "$dest")"` on that same, already existing directory. GNU install -d chmods existing directories (verified: 750 -> 755), so /home/user/docker ends up world-readable/traversable, contrary to the intended 0750; the same happens to every other existing directory a ks_file target lives in (e.g. /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors). Secrets inside are still 0600, so this is robustness rather than a leak.
- **Proposed fix**: Only create the parent when it is missing: `[[ -d $(dirname "$dest") ]] || install -d -m 0755 "$(dirname "$dest")"` in ks_file and ks_write (and in ks_self_signed_cert), or use `mkdir -p` which leaves existing directories untouched.

### R54. 'Save' in the unsaved-changes dialog discards the changes even when the save failed

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:827
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The fleet file's directory became read-only (or the disk is full). User edits a host, presses Ctrl+O / closes the window, picks 'Save'. resp() calls self.save(), which catches the OSError, shows the 'Save failed' alert and returns - but resp() then unconditionally sets self.doc.dirty = False and calls then() (open_dialog or self.destroy), so the window is destroyed / a new fleet is opened and the edits are gone despite the user having chosen Save.
- **Proposed fix**: Make save() return True/False and only continue when it succeeded: `if r == 'save' and not self.save(): return` before `self.doc.dirty = False; then()`. Also do not set dirty = False on 'save' - save() already does.

### R55. Selecting a host in the collapsed (narrow) layout never shows the form

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:904
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Window narrower than 760px (the breakpoint at line 715 sets split.collapsed = True), e.g. on a small laptop or a tiled window. Adw.NavigationSplitView in collapsed mode shows either the sidebar or the content, switched by its show-content property. on_select only calls show_editor(row.scope); nothing ever calls self.split.set_show_content(True), so tapping Defaults or a host rebuilds the form behind the sidebar and the user keeps seeing the host list.
- **Proposed fix**: In on_select (or at the end of show_editor when scope is not None) add `self.split.set_show_content(True)`; libadwaita then shows the content page with a back button when collapsed and ignores the call when not.

### R56. Add-host dialog accepts names the CLI rejects, which then breaks status for the whole fleet

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:972
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: User adds a host named 'sh_3' or 'node 1' and saves. config.Fleet.__init__ raises 'host name ... must be a plain DNS label' for the entire fleet, so `kiwi-server list --porcelain` exits non-zero: refresh_status() only appends the error to the (hidden) log pane and self.status stays {} - no INVALID pill, no banner, and Render/Build fail with the same message for every host, not just the new one. The dialog's own body text says 'letters, digits, dashes' but nothing enforces it.
- **Proposed fix**: Validate before adding: `if not re.fullmatch(r'[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?', name, re.I): self.toast('Use letters, digits and dashes'); return`. Additionally, when `list` fails, show its error in the banner (or a toast) instead of only the log pane.

### R57. Output folder URI is built by string concatenation and is not escaped

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:1094
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: Fleet lives under a path with a space, '#' or '%' (e.g. ~/Kiwi Fleet/fleet.yaml -> output dir '/home/u/Kiwi Fleet/output/m1'). open_folder passes 'file:///home/u/Kiwi Fleet/output/m1' to Gio.AppInfo.launch_default_for_uri; '#' truncates the URI as a fragment and '%' sequences are decoded, so the file manager opens the wrong directory or reports an invalid URI.
- **Proposed fix**: Use `Gio.File.new_for_path(d).get_uri()` (or GLib.filename_to_uri(d, None)) instead of 'file://' + d.

### R58. Show-script runs the CLI synchronously on the GTK main thread (up to 120 s freeze)

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:1073
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: First 'Show the role script' for a node-gw/node-cloud host when secrets/ca does not exist: the CLI generates two RSA-4096 keys with openssl, renders every host for the DNS records and the stack - several seconds on a laptop. run_cli() blocks in subprocess.run on the main thread, so the window stops repainting and the compositor marks it unresponsive; with a slow machine or a missing jinja2 error path the freeze lasts until the 120 s timeout.
- **Proposed fix**: Run it like spawn()/DoctorDialog: set_busy(True, ...), run run_cli in a threading.Thread and present the TextDialog from GLib.idle_add in the callback; pass '--quiet' so progress lines are not part of the text.

### R59. Every status refresh rebuilds the editor twice and drops focus/scroll position

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: gui · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/gui/kiwi-server-gui`:1023
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: User presses Ctrl+S while typing in a module setting. save() -> refresh_status() -> apply_status(): refresh_rows() rebuilds the sidebar and re-selects the row, which fires row-selected -> on_select -> show_editor(scope) (new Form); then apply_status calls show_editor(self.current_scope) again (second new Form). The entry the user was typing in is destroyed, the caret and the scroll position are lost, and the Preferences page jumps to the top. The same happens after every Render/Build via after_spawn.
- **Proposed fix**: In apply_status only update what the status affects: rebuild the sidebar rows and the banner/subtitle (factor the banner part of show_editor into update_banner(scope)) instead of recreating the Form; and in refresh_rows block the row-selected handler while re-selecting (self.listbox.handler_block_by_func / a self._reselecting flag) so on_select does not rebuild the editor.

### R60. A missing stack vpn_ip is reported as a module firewall-rule error instead of naming the setting to set

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:289
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: The sftp rule in modules/sftp/module.yaml line 22 uses `${VPN_IP}`. Rendering m1 (node-gw preset, sftp included) without `node-gw.vpn_ip` fails with `error: modules/sftp firewall: ${VPN_IP} is not set` (verified). The user never wrote anything about sftp or firewalls; the help text of vpn_ip calls it optional. Combined with the previous finding, the same omission is fatal on node-gw and silent on node-cloud.
- **Proposed fix**: Map the context key back to the fleet setting in `expand`'s error: if `k` is a stack setting (vpn_ip, pub_iface, docker_dir...) say `"%s: %s needs the stack setting %s (set it under %s:)" % (what, m.group(0), k, role)`; better, validate `vpn_ip` once in `resolve_settings` when any module has needs_vpn_dnat or a `${VPN_IP}` rule.

### R61. firewall rules with scope host or self are silently dropped although docs/modules.md promises they are applied

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:381
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `vpn_rules` is the only consumer of `Module.firewall_rules` (grep over lib/: lines 84-85, 381-384) and it skips every module whose `firewall.scope` is not 'vpn-client'. docs/modules.md documents `scope: host (gw.sh)` and `self (its own container)`. A module author who follows the docs and lists host rules (the gateway module's section even says 'applied by gw.sh') gets a stack without them and no warning.
- **Proposed fix**: Either render host-scope rules into a generated unit/script (e.g. append them to gw.sh or a kiwi-firewall.service) and self-scope rules into a per-module file, or raise KiwiError in `Module.__init__` when `rules` is non-empty with a scope other than vpn-client, and correct docs/modules.md.

### R62. Render-time ${VAR} errors for outputs, file settings, open_ports and urls name only the bare module

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:423
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `render()` passes `what=name` to `expand` on lines 420, 423, 437, 449 and 458, while the bind-mount path passes 'modules/%s storage'. An unset variable in a module's `outputs.path`, `open_ports` or `urls` yields `error: cloud: ${X} is not set` — unlike every other message, it does not say which module.yaml key is at fault, and 'cloud:' reads like a host name.
- **Proposed fix**: Use descriptive labels: "modules/%s outputs.%s" % (name, key), "modules/%s open_ports", "modules/%s urls", "modules/%s outputs.%s (file setting)".

### R63. dns_records() re-validates every host, duplicating the 'no disk' warning and silently skipping invalid hosts

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/cli.py`:128
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `h.validate(self.roles)` appends to `h.warnings` on every call; `check()` already called it once, and `dns_records()` calls it again for each host. Rendering a fleet where lab1 has no `disk:` prints `warning: lab1: no disk: set — configs render, but an ISO cannot be built` twice (verified). Also, when validate returns errors the host is dropped from the records with `continue` and no message, although the docstring says it is 'skipped with a warning' — e.g. `kiwi-server render fleet.yaml sh3` with a broken m1 produces a master start.sh without m1's mesh address and says nothing.
- **Proposed fix**: Make `Host.validate` idempotent (clear `self.warnings` at its start), and in `dns_records` do `errs = h.validate(self.roles); if errs: util.warn("dns records: skipping %s: %s" % (h.name, errs[0])); continue`.

### R64. Malformed nginx:/upstream entries in a module.yaml raise bare KeyError tracebacks instead of a KiwiError

- **Severity**: low · **lens**: renderer · **where**: `/home/user/kiwi-server/lib/kiwiserver/modules.py`:353
- **Verification**: unverified (verifier never ran — quota)
- **Scenario**: `nginx_blocks` indexes `u["name"]`, `u["server"]` (345-346), `e["server_name"]`, `e["proxy_pass"]` (353-354) and `x["path"]`, `x["config"]` (362-363) directly. A module whose `servers:` entry lacks `proxy_pass` (or uses the documented single-block form without `server_name` but with `proxy_pass`) makes `kiwi-server render`, `script`, and the GUI's dns-record pass crash with `KeyError: 'proxy_pass'` and a Python traceback; `main()` only catches KiwiError.
