- [medium] **JDownloader image swapped from headless jaymoulin/jdownloader to jlesage/jdownloader-2 (noVNC web UI)** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:28 image: jlesage/jdownloader-2 (untagged), env TZ/USER_ID/GROUP_ID/MYJDOWNLOADER_*, volumes /config and /output, web UI on 5800 (module.yaml:18) — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:115 image: jaymoulin/jdownloader (untagged), env MYJD_USER/MYJD_PASSWORD/MYJD_DEVICE_NAME, volumes $DOCKERDIR/knjdownloader/cfg:/opt/JDownloader/app/cfg, $DL_DIR/downloads:/opt/JDownloader/Downloads, /etc/localtime:/etc/localtime:ro; no web UI, MyJDownloader only — impact: Different container layout: existing live JD configuration (accounts, plugin settings, link grabber state) under knjdownloader/cfg is not picked up by the new image's /config; env names differ; a new 5800 UI (plus the 5900 VNC port the image also opens) appears on the shared gluetun namespace. The env names used in the template ARE correct for jlesage/jdownloader-2, so this is a deliberate behaviour change, not a typo. — fix: Either keep jaymoulin/jdownloader for parity (then drop the jd. nginx block and make MyJD settings required), or keep jlesage and document the migration (copy cfg into /config/... per jlesage docs) and pin an image tag in both cases.
- [medium] **JDownloader runs as uid 1000 instead of live uid 1001 / gid 100** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:34-35 USER_ID={{ puid | default('1000') }}, GROUP_ID={{ pgid | default('1000') }} — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:118 user: 1001:100; knsftp command '$SFTPUSER:$SFTPPASS:1001' (line 181) creates the SFTP user with the same uid 1001 — impact: Downloads written by JDownloader change owner from 1001:100 to 1000:1000; the (live) SFTP user uid 1001 loses write access to JD output, and an existing cfg dir owned by 1001 is unreadable by 1000. Transmission stays at 1000 live, so the live stack intentionally used two uids. — fix: Give JDownloader its own jdownloader_uid/jdownloader_gid settings (defaults 1001/100 for live parity) or make puid/pgid the single 'downloads owner' and document chown of existing data; keep the future sftp module's uid in sync.
- [medium] **Transmission config host path differs from live** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:24 {{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-transmission:/config -> /home/user/docker/kn-transmission — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:146 $DOCKERDIR/kntransmission/config:/config; module.yaml:49 says ${DOCKERDIR}/kntransmission — impact: Migrating a live node loses settings.json, torrent files and resume data unless the directory is moved; module.yaml and template disagree with each other as well. — fix: Pick one convention (kn-transmission/config is consistent with the kn- prefix rule and the live 'config' subdir), update module.yaml bind_mounts to match, and document a one-time move.
- [medium] **Downloads mount semantics: DL_DIR parent vs full download_dir** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:25 {{ download_dir }}:/downloads and :48 {{ download_dir }}:/output; module.yaml:46-48 bind_mount ${DL_DIR} -> /data — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:123,147,178 mount $DL_DIR/downloads (a subdirectory of DL_DIR) into JD, Transmission and knsftp — impact: A user who sets download_dir to the old DL_DIR value gets a different directory than live; module.yaml's /data container path matches no container at all, so any generator relying on storage.bind_mounts would create the wrong mounts. — fix: Define download_dir as the full downloads path (as examples/node-gw.yaml does) and rewrite module.yaml bind_mounts to {download_dir -> /downloads (transmission), /output (jdownloader)}.
- [high] **knsftp service absent from v2** — template: No sftp service anywhere in kiwi-v2/modules (module.yaml covers only transmission + jdownloader) — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:174-188 knsftp: image jmcombs/sftp, ports 2224:22, volume $DL_DIR/downloads:/home/user/Downloads, command $SFTPUSER:$SFTPPASS:1001, static IP 172.128.0.251 on knet-node, no restart policy; post-rules.txt.example:21 DNAT ${VPN_IP}:2224 -> 172.128.0.251:22; .env keys SFTPUSER/SFTPPASS — impact: Remote retrieval of downloaded files over SFTP (LAN port 2224 and VPN-IP DNAT) disappears, which is the live 'File Access' capability of the gw node (02_kiwi_node_gw.md:78-81). — fix: Add an sftp sub-service to this module (or a separate 'sftp' module depending on downloader) with sftp_user/sftp_password(secret)/sftp_port(2224)/uid(1001) settings, a static IP from the renderer ip_map, restart: unless-stopped, and an extra_dnat_rules entry for the vpn-client post-rules.
- [medium] **MyJDownloader direct-connect port 3129 not exposed** — template: module.yaml:19 exposed_ports: [] and vpn-client/docker-compose.yml.j2 publishes no ports; no FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS; no DNAT entry — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:25-26 knvpn-client ports 3129:3129; .env key FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS (line 25; the compose env line 19 is commented out); post-rules.txt.example:18 DNAT ${VPN_IP}:3129 -> 172.128.0.5:3129; nginx stream block for 3129 commented out (nginx.conf.example:159-163); Pi-hole overrides 192-168-2-20.mydns.jdownloader.org / 10-8-0-6.mydns.jdownloader.org and rebind-domain-ok=mydns.jdownloader.org (knpihole pihole.toml:117-118,1055) — impact: MyJDownloader apps cannot use LAN/VPN direct connection and fall back to the cloud relay. Note the live state is itself half-wired (DNAT points at nginx which has no 3129 listener, FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS commented), so treat as 'feature intended live'. — fix: Because the downloaders share gluetun's namespace, ports must be published on the vpn-client service: add a renderer mechanism (e.g. vpn_client_published_ports collected from modules) that emits '3129:3129' and FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS=3129 on kn-vpn-client, plus an extra_dnat_rules item {port: 3129, protocol: tcp, target: '{{ docker_subnet_base }}.2:3129'}; gate it behind an enable_myjd_direct_connect bool.
- [medium] **Example gw node config omits reverse-proxy although the live gw stack runs knnginx** — template: examples/node-gw.yaml:9-13 modules: vpn-client, dns, gateway, downloader (reverse-proxy is only an optional dependency, module.yaml:12-13) — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:89-111 knnginx on 172.128.0.5 with 80/443 published and DNAT from VPN_IP (post-rules.txt.example:13-14) — impact: Rendering the example yields a node where neither 9091 nor 5800 is reachable from anywhere (no nginx, no published ports on vpn-client). — fix: Add reverse-proxy to examples/node-gw.yaml, or make the module publish the UI ports on vpn-client when reverse-proxy is absent.
- [low] **MyJDownloader credentials optional in v2, mandatory live; device name default differs** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:36-40 MYJDOWNLOADER_* only when myjd_email set; device name default 'kiwi-jd' — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:125-128 MYJD_USER/MYJD_PASSWORD always set, MYJD_DEVICE_NAME=$JD_HOSTNAME — impact: Acceptable with the jlesage image (web UI exists), but if parity with the headless image is kept, an unset myjd_email leaves JD uncontrollable. Device shown in the MyJD app changes from the hostname to 'kiwi-jd'. — fix: Default myjd_device_name to the JD hostname; make myjd_email/myjd_password required when the headless image is used.
- [low] **depends_on ordering dropped** — template: No depends_on in docker-compose.yml.j2 — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:93-95 knnginx depends_on knvpn-client, kntransmission; :119-120 knjdownloader depends_on knnginx — impact: docker compose infers a dependency from network_mode: service:kn-vpn-client, so start order relative to gluetun is still correct; only nginx-before-JD and transmission-before-nginx ordering is lost (nginx then needs the upstream resolvable at start, which is satisfied by the vpn-client container name). — fix: Optionally add depends_on: [{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client] explicitly for readability; have reverse-proxy depend on vpn-client.
- [low] **Image tags unpinned / JD image untagged** — template: lscr.io/linuxserver/transmission:latest; jlesage/jdownloader-2 (no tag) — live: lscr.io/linuxserver/transmission:latest; jaymoulin/jdownloader (no tag); weekly cron does `up --pull always` (kiwi-node-gw.sh:29) — impact: Same floating behaviour as live; cosmetic for parity, but a generator should pin for reproducibility. — fix: Expose transmission_image_tag / jdownloader_image_tag settings defaulting to 'latest'.
- [low] **Health check target would always fail for Transmission** — template: module.yaml:56-59 health http://localhost:9091/ every 30s — live: No healthcheck defined for kntransmission/knjdownloader — impact: Transmission answers 401 when RPC auth is on (and 409/redirect otherwise), so an HTTP-200 probe marks the service unhealthy; with a shared network namespace it is also ambiguous which container executes it. — fix: Use type tcp target localhost:9091, or http with /transmission/web/ and credentials; run it in kn-transmission.

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **Wrong container name in nginx proxy_pass** (kiwi-v2/modules/downloader/module.yaml lines 64 and 66: proxy_pass: "http://knvpn-client:9091" / "http://knvpn-client:5800"): The prefix convention (module-schema.md:86-89, kiwi-render.py:214, vpn-client/docker-compose.yml.j2:8) gives kn-vpn-client; 'knvpn-client' is the v1 live name. nginx fails at startup with 'host not found in upstream'. — fix: proxy_pass: "http://{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client:9091" (and :5800), or an upstream block named {{ container_prefix }}-transmission-default.
- **templates.nginx points to a file that does not exist** (module.yaml line 40: nginx: nginx.server.conf.j2 (directory contains only docker-compose.yml.j2 and module.yaml)): Confirmed by running kiwi-render.py render examples/node-gw.yaml: 'Warning: Template not found: .../downloader/nginx.server.conf.j2'. Even if the file existed, kiwi-render.py never passes `servers`/`upstreams` into reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2 (lines 41-94 of that template loop over undefined names), so no server block is ever produced for this module. — fix: Add downloader/nginx.server.conf.j2 and implement aggregation in the renderer (collect each module's nginx.servers into ctx['servers'] before rendering reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2).
- **templates.env_example references a non-existent .env.example** (module.yaml line 41): No .env.example file exists; harmless today only because kiwi-render.py:403 skips the env_example key, but any generator that honours the schema will fail. — fix: Remove the key or add the file.
- **env.required names do not match the variables the template consumes** (module.yaml lines 28-29 (DL_DIR, PW_TRANS) vs docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 17, 25, 48 (transmission_password, download_dir)): kiwi-render.py validate() lowercases env.required and looks for 'dl_dir'/'pw_trans' in node vars, so examples/node-gw.yaml (which correctly sets download_dir and transmission_password) still triggers '⚠ Warning: Module downloader requires DL_DIR / PW_TRANS'. _generate_env_template()/_find_var_value() (lines 597-625) tries DL_DIR, dl_dir, downloader_dl_dir and finds nothing, so the generated .env.example contains 'DL_DIR=  # REQUIRED' and 'PW_TRANS=  # REQUIRED' with empty values (confirmed in render output) even though the values were provided. — fix: Rename env.required to DOWNLOAD_DIR and TRANSMISSION_PASSWORD (lowercase forms equal the template names), or derive required vars from the template.
- **Dead env.optional defaults (TZ, TRANS_USER, TRANS_PORT, JD_USER, JD_PORT)** (module.yaml lines 31-35): The renderer injects them lowercased (tz, trans_user, trans_port, jd_user, jd_port; kiwi-render.py:306-308) but the template reads timezone and transmission_user, has no port variables (9091/5800 are fixed by the images and hard-coded in module.yaml nginx), and JD_USER has no meaning for either JD image. Only MYJD_DEVICE_NAME is actually consumed. — fix: Drop TRANS_PORT/JD_PORT/JD_USER; rename TZ->TIMEZONE and TRANS_USER->TRANSMISSION_USER so the lowercased key matches the template, or move the defaults into the template filters only.
- **storage.bind_mounts container paths match neither the template nor the live stack** (module.yaml lines 45-54: ${DL_DIR}->/data, ${DOCKERDIR}/kntransmission->/config, ${DOCKERDIR}/knjdownloader->/jd/cfg): Template mounts {{ download_dir }} at /downloads and /output and uses host dirs kn-transmission / kn-jdownloader (with container_prefix); live uses kntransmission/config, knjdownloader/cfg and /opt/JDownloader/app/cfg. Container path /jd/cfg exists in no image. A generator that pre-creates/validates host dirs from this list would check the wrong paths. — fix: Rewrite bind_mounts to: {download_dir -> /downloads, required}, {download_dir -> /output, required}, {${DOCKERDIR}/kn-transmission -> /config}, {${DOCKERDIR}/kn-jdownloader -> /config}.
- **Non-schema top-level network_mode block hard-codes kn- prefix** (module.yaml lines 69-72: network_mode: transmission: "service:kn-vpn-client" / jdownloader: "service:kn-vpn-client"): Not part of module-schema.md and ignored by kiwi-render.py; if a port of the generator honoured it, the value would be wrong on a master node (km-vpn-client). The template already derives this correctly from container_prefix. — fix: Delete the block (or express it as a schema field with '{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client').
- **No enforcement of required variables; SilentUndefined turns missing secrets/paths into empty strings** (kiwi-render.py lines 25-37 (SilentUndefined) with docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 17, 24-25, 47-48): Missing transmission_password renders 'PASS=' -> linuxserver/transmission disables RPC auth (it requires both USER and PASS non-empty), exposing an unauthenticated UI behind nginx. Missing download_dir renders '- :/downloads' which docker compose rejects; missing docker_dir renders '/kn-transmission:/config' at filesystem root. — fix: Fail rendering when env.required (aligned to template names) is unset, or wrap the critical values in {% if not transmission_password %}{{ undefined_error }}{% endif %} guards.
- **myjd_password can render empty inside the MyJD block** (docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 36-40): The block is gated only on myjd_email; with myjd_email set and myjd_password unset the compose gets 'MYJDOWNLOADER_PASSWORD=' and JD logs MyJD auth failures. — fix: Gate on `{% if myjd_email and myjd_password %}` or validate the pair together.
- **UI ports unreachable when reverse-proxy is not selected** (module.yaml lines 12-13 (reverse-proxy optional), 16-19 (exposed_ports: []), examples/node-gw.yaml:9-13): Because both services use network_mode: service:kn-vpn-client, only the vpn-client service can publish 9091/5800, and nothing does. With reverse-proxy optional and absent from the gw example, the rendered node has no path to either web UI. — fix: Make reverse-proxy a required dependency, or add a renderer feature letting a module request ports to be published on vpn-client (also needed for 3129).

**Present live, missing in v2**

- knsftp service: jmcombs/sftp, host port 2224->22, $DL_DIR/downloads mounted at /home/user/Downloads, user created by command '$SFTPUSER:$SFTPPASS:1001', static IP 172.128.0.251, DNAT ${VPN_IP}:2224 -> 172.128.0.251:22 in knvpn-client/post-rules.txt.example:21, .env keys SFTPUSER/SFTPPASS (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:174-188)
- MyJDownloader direct-connect port 3129: '3129:3129' published on knvpn-client (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:26), FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS .env key (compose line 19 commented), DNAT ${VPN_IP}:3129 (post-rules.txt.example:18), commented nginx stream block (nginx.conf.example:159-163) and gluetun UNBLOCK=mydns.jdownloader.org hint (compose line 21)
- nginx server block for Transmission with websocket upgrade and a keepalive upstream (nginx.conf.example:39-43,105-125); no downloader/nginx.server.conf.j2 exists and kiwi-render.py has no server-block aggregation
- Per-service hostname settings TM_HOSTNAME / JD_HOSTNAME (.env:10-11) consumed by envsubst in kiwi-node-gw.sh:19 and as MYJD_DEVICE_NAME; v2 hard-codes dl./jd. prefixes and does not substitute ${HOSTNAME}
- Split-horizon DNS records for the download services: 'transmission.<host>', '<lan-ip-dashed>.mydns.jdownloader.org', '<vpn-ip-dashed>.mydns.jdownloader.org' and 'rebind-domain-ok=mydns.jdownloader.org' (knpihole/etc-pihole/hosts/custom.list:37-40, pihole.toml:115-118,1055) - the v2 dns module declares generates_local_overrides but nothing emits these
- JDownloader running as a dedicated uid/gid (1001:100) shared with the SFTP user, and the /etc/localtime:ro mount (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:118,124)
- Explicit depends_on ordering (knnginx -> kntransmission/knvpn-client, knjdownloader -> knnginx)
- A way for services in gluetun's namespace to publish host ports via the vpn-client service (needed for 3129 and for UI access without reverse-proxy)
- reverse-proxy module in the gw example node (live gw runs knnginx with 80/443 and VPN DNAT)

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `download_dir` | string | yes |  | Downloads directory — Absolute host path bind-mounted to /downloads (Transmission) and /output (JDownloader). Live stack used $DL_DIR/downloads, so enter the full downloads path (e.g. /mnt/data/downloads), not the parent. Must exist and be writable by the download uid/gid before compose up. |
| `transmission_user` | string |  | user | Transmission web UI username — RPC/web UI login (env USER). Live key TMUSER. Auth is only enabled when both username and password are non-empty. |
| `transmission_password` | secret | yes |  | Transmission web UI password — RPC/web UI password (env PASS). Live key TMPASS; module.yaml calls it PW_TRANS. Leaving it empty disables authentication on the UI. |
| `puid` | int |  | 1000 | Download owner UID — Applied as PUID (Transmission) and USER_ID (JDownloader). Live Transmission used 1000; live JDownloader ran as 1001:100 to share files with the SFTP user (uid 1001). |
| `pgid` | int |  | 1000 | Download owner GID — Applied as PGID (Transmission) and GROUP_ID (JDownloader). Live JDownloader gid was 100. |
| `timezone` | string |  | Europe/Berlin | Timezone — TZ for both containers. Normally inherited from the node-level variables.timezone; override here only if the downloaders need a different zone. |
| `docker_dir` | string | yes | /home/user/docker | Docker config directory — Node-level variable; config dirs are created at <docker_dir>/kn-transmission and <docker_dir>/kn-jdownloader (live: kntransmission/config and knjdownloader/cfg). |
| `myjd_email` | string |  |  | MyJDownloader account e-mail — Enables remote control via my.jdownloader.org (env MYJDOWNLOADER_EMAIL). Live key MYJD_USER. Optional with the jlesage web-UI image; mandatory if the headless jaymoulin image is used. |
| `myjd_password` | secret |  |  | MyJDownloader password — Required whenever myjd_email is set (env MYJDOWNLOADER_PASSWORD). Live key MYJD_PASSWORD. |
| `myjd_device_name` | string |  | kiwi-jd | MyJDownloader device name — Name shown in the MyJDownloader app (env MYJDOWNLOADER_DEVICE_NAME). Live used the JD hostname ($JD_HOSTNAME); consider defaulting to jdownloader.<hostname>. |
| `transmission_hostname` | string |  | transmission.<hostname> | Transmission hostname — server_name for the HTTPS reverse-proxy block (live TM_HOSTNAME, e.g. transmission.m1.kiwi). Also becomes a split-horizon DNS record. module.yaml currently hard-codes dl.<hostname>. |
| `jdownloader_hostname` | string |  | jdownloader.<hostname> | JDownloader hostname — server_name for the JDownloader web UI block (port 5800, jlesage image only). Live JD_HOSTNAME existed but had no nginx block; module.yaml hard-codes jd.<hostname>. |

**Reverse-proxy blocks**: transmission.<hostname>  (live: $TM_HOSTNAME; module.yaml: dl.<hostname>) → http://kn-vpn-client:9091  (preferably via `upstream kn-transmission-default { server kn-vpn-client:9091; keepalive 2; }`) (websocket); jdownloader.<hostname>  (module.yaml: jd.<hostname>; no live equivalent) → http://kn-vpn-client:5800 (websocket); (stream, optional) listen 3129 → kn-vpn-client:3129  (TCP stream proxy_pass, no http://)

**Host ports**: 443/tcp - HTTPS web UIs via reverse-proxy (LAN) and via DNAT ${VPN_IP}:443 -> nginx in vpn-client post-rules (port belongs to reverse-proxy but is the only access path for this module); 80/tcp - HTTP->HTTPS redirect via reverse-proxy (LAN + DNAT ${VPN_IP}:80); 2224/tcp - SFTP access to downloads if knsftp is ported (live: knsftp 2224:22 on LAN, DNAT ${VPN_IP}:2224 -> 172.128.0.251:22); 3129/tcp - optional MyJDownloader direct-connect port; must be published on the vpn-client container (live knvpn-client 3129:3129) with gluetun FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS=3129, plus DNAT ${VPN_IP}:3129 for VPN clients; Note: 9091 and 5800 are NOT opened on the host; they are reached only through nginx on the Docker network. Transmission peer port 51413 is not published live either (all peer traffic exits through the VPN tunnel).

**Host directories**: <download_dir> (e.g. /mnt/data/downloads; live $DL_DIR/downloads) - mounted at /downloads and /output; owner puid:pgid (live: writable by both 1000:1000 and 1001:100); <docker_dir>/kn-transmission (live $DOCKERDIR/kntransmission/config) - Transmission /config, owner puid:pgid; <docker_dir>/kn-jdownloader (live $DOCKERDIR/knjdownloader/cfg -> /opt/JDownloader/app/cfg) - JDownloader /config, owner of the JD uid (live 1001:100); (if knsftp is ported) no extra directory: it re-mounts <download_dir> at /home/<sftp_user>/Downloads; the SFTP uid must match the download owner; (dependency, not this module) <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/nginx.conf, fullchain.pem, privkey.pem must contain/accompany the server blocks above before reverse-proxy starts

<details><summary>Template variables (11)</summary>

- `container_prefix` — docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 7,9,11,24,27,29,31,47 (service keys, container_name, network_mode "service:{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client", config volume paths) — required — default (reserved: 'kn' for node, 'km' for master; kiwi-render.py:214) — Always provided by the renderer. Yields kn-transmission / kn-jdownloader / kn-vpn-client, which is why module.yaml's literal 'knvpn-client' and 'kn-vpn-client' strings are inconsistent with the template.
- `puid` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 13 (PUID for Transmission) and line 34 (USER_ID for JDownloader) — default '1000' (template filter) — Live kntransmission hard-codes PUID=1000; live knjdownloader runs as `user: 1001:100` (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:118), so one shared puid cannot reproduce live ownership for both services. Live .env also carries PUID/PGID keys that the live compose never references.
- `pgid` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 14 (PGID) and line 35 (GROUP_ID) — default '1000' (template filter) — See puid: live JDownloader gid is 100.
- `timezone` — docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 15 and 33 (TZ=) — default 'Europe/Berlin' (template filter) — Global node variable (node-config-schema.md variables.timezone). module.yaml env.optional TZ lowercases to 'tz', which the template never reads, so that default is dead.
- `transmission_user` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 16 (USER=) — default 'user' (template filter) — Live name is TMUSER (.env:39); module.yaml calls it TRANS_USER (-> 'trans_user', never consumed).
- `transmission_password` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 17 (PASS=) — required — default none (SilentUndefined renders PASS= empty) — Live name is TMPASS (.env:40); module.yaml env.required calls it PW_TRANS. examples/node-gw.yaml passes the literal string "${PW_TRANS}" so the secret is resolved by docker compose from .env at `up` time (confirmed in render: PASS=${PW_TRANS}). linuxserver/transmission only enables RPC auth when BOTH USER and PASS are non-empty, so an unset value silently yields an unauthenticated web UI.
- `docker_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24 and 47 (host side of /config mounts) — required — default none — Global node variable. Renders {{ docker_dir }}/kn-transmission and {{ docker_dir }}/kn-jdownloader; live paths are $DOCKERDIR/kntransmission/config and $DOCKERDIR/knjdownloader/cfg.
- `download_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 25 (:/downloads for Transmission) and line 48 (:/output for JDownloader) — required — default none (empty renders ':/downloads', which docker compose rejects as an invalid volume spec) — Semantics = full path of the downloads folder (examples/node-gw.yaml: /mnt/data/downloads). Live mounts $DL_DIR/downloads, i.e. DL_DIR is the parent. module.yaml env.required DL_DIR / bind_mount '${DL_DIR}:/data' matches neither.
- `myjd_email` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 36 ({% if myjd_email %}) and line 37 (MYJDOWNLOADER_EMAIL=) — default none (block omitted when unset) — Account identifier (PII) - treat as sensitive even though not a credential. Live equivalent MYJD_USER (.env:35) is always set because jaymoulin/jdownloader is headless and MyJDownloader is the only control path.
- `myjd_password` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 38 (MYJDOWNLOADER_PASSWORD=) — default none — Only emitted inside the myjd_email block; required whenever myjd_email is set, otherwise renders an empty value. Live equivalent MYJD_PASSWORD (.env:36).
- `myjd_device_name` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 39 (MYJDOWNLOADER_DEVICE_NAME=) — default 'kiwi-jd' (template filter; module.yaml env.optional MYJD_DEVICE_NAME has the same default and is the only env.optional key whose lowercased name the template actually consumes) — Live sets MYJD_DEVICE_NAME=$JD_HOSTNAME (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:128), i.e. the JD hostname such as jdownloader.<hostname>.

</details>

### Module `gateway`

**Discrepancies vs live**

- [high] **Missing `throw` route for the Docker subnet in table 128** — template: gw.sh.j2:28-29 only `ip rule add iif $PUB_IFACE table 128` + `ip route add table 128 default via $CONTAINER_IP` — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:42 and kn-service/gw.sh:25 add `ip route add table 128 throw "$DOCKER_SUBNET"` after the default route (docs 02_kiwi_node_gw.md:36 documents it) — impact: Any LAN packet destined for 172.128.0.0/24 (e.g. a client pointing DNS at 172.128.0.4 as per docs l.47, or the host reaching containers on behalf of LAN clients) is routed to the VPN container as next hop instead of the connected bridge route in the main table; routing loop / blackhole for LAN->container traffic. — fix: Add `ip route add table 128 throw "$DOCKER_SUBNET"` after line 29 (this also gives the dead DOCKER_SUBNET variable a purpose).
- [high] **FORWARD rules appended (-A) instead of inserted (-I): shadowed by Docker's own FORWARD chain** — template: gw.sh.j2:33-35 `iptables -A FORWARD ...` / `-t nat -A POSTROUTING ...` — live: The script that actually runs from systemd, kn-service/gw.sh:28-40, uses `iptables -I FORWARD ...` and `-t nat -I POSTROUTING ...` (inserted at position 1, ahead of Docker's `-j DOCKER-USER` / `-j DOCKER-FORWARD` jumps). Only the one-shot kiwi-node-gw.sh start() uses -A. — impact: Docker's FORWARD chain runs before appended rules. On Docker >= 28 (default bridge gateway_mode 'nat') the DOCKER chain drops traffic that enters a bridge from another interface unless it matches a published port, so `-A FORWARD -i eth0 -o knet-node -j ACCEPT` is never reached and LAN clients get no connectivity. Even on older Docker the rule ordering differs from live. — fix: Use `-I FORWARD` (or insert into DOCKER-USER: `iptables -I DOCKER-USER -i "$PUB_IFACE" -o "$NETWORK_NAME" -j ACCEPT`), and/or set `com.docker.network.bridge.gateway_mode_ipv4: nat-unprotected` on knet-node in templates/networks.yml.j2.
- [high] **Bridge interface name `knet-node` is not guaranteed in v2** — template: gw.sh.j2:33-35 and module.yaml:28-29 match on interface `knet-node` (`-o "$NETWORK_NAME"`) — live: docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:196 `com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node` names the Linux bridge; kiwi-v2/templates/networks.yml.j2:17-26 sets only enable_ipv6 and driver.mtu, not bridge.name — impact: The v2 bridge is `br-<12hex>`; iptables accepts the non-existent name so no error is raised, but FORWARD ACCEPT and MASQUERADE never match. LAN traffic reaches gluetun with LAN source IPs and is dropped by gluetun's firewall (`-s DOCKER_SUBNET -o tun0 ACCEPT` in post-rules only covers the Docker subnet). Gateway silently broken. — fix: Add `com.docker.network.bridge.name: {{ network_name }}` to the driver_opts in templates/networks.yml.j2 (shared template, but this module depends on it); optionally have gw.sh resolve the bridge via `docker network inspect -f '{{"{{"}}.Options{{"}}"}}'` as a fallback.
- [high] **No wait/retry before the VPN container is reachable (boot race)** — template: gw.sh.j2:21-24 pings once and `exit 1`; kn-gateway.service.j2 has `Type=oneshot`, `After=docker.service`, no `Restart=`, no `ExecStartPre` wait — live: kn-service/gw.sh:2 `sleep 15` before anything; gw.sh:41-43 on ping failure runs `systemctl restart kiwi-gw` (self-retry loop); kiwi-node-gw.sh:32-34 runs `docker compose up` then `sleep 5` before adding routes — impact: `After=docker.service` only orders after dockerd, not after the compose stack; on reboot the unit fires before kn-vpn-client has an IP, `ip route add ... via` fails (`set -e`) and the unit stays failed until someone restarts it, so the LAN loses its gateway after every reboot. — fix: Add a bounded wait loop in gw.sh (e.g. `for i in $(seq 1 30); do ping -c1 -W1 "$CONTAINER_IP" && break; sleep 2; done`) and/or `Restart=on-failure` + `RestartSec=10` in the unit (newer systemd accepts Restart= with Type=oneshot; otherwise switch to Type=simple with a wait loop), plus `Wants=network-online.target` `After=network-online.target docker.service`. module.yaml:63 already claims `restart: on-failure` but the unit template does not render it.
- [medium] **Missing LAN -> Docker-subnet DROP rule (container isolation from LAN)** — template: No rule restricting LAN access to the Docker subnet; gw.sh.j2:33 accepts everything `-i $PUB_IFACE -o $NETWORK_NAME` — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:45 / gw.sh:28 `iptables -A/-I FORWARD -i $PUB_IFACE -d $DOCKER_SUBNET -j DROP` ('Block LAN clients from accessing the Docker subnet') — impact: LAN clients can reach every container IP directly (Pi-hole admin on 172.128.0.4:80, nginx on .5, portainer on .250, gluetun control API on .2:8000) instead of only the published host ports. Security posture is weaker than live. (Note: in live gw.sh the -I ordering actually puts this DROP last, so it is partially ineffective there too; port it with correct ordering.) — fix: Add `iptables -I FORWARD -i "$PUB_IFACE" -d "$DOCKER_SUBNET" -j DROP` evaluated BEFORE the generic ACCEPT (insert it after the ACCEPTs when using -I, or use -A ordering DROP-first inside DOCKER-USER). Expose as setting `block_lan_to_docker` (default true).
- [low] **Missing explicit LAN -> VPN mesh (10.8.0.0/16) ACCEPT rule / VPN_SUBNET variable** — template: No VPN_SUBNET; only the generic `-o $NETWORK_NAME ACCEPT` — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:48 and gw.sh:7,31 `VPN_SUBNET=10.8.0.0/16`; `iptables -I FORWARD -i $PUB_IFACE -d $VPN_SUBNET -j ACCEPT` — impact: Functionally covered by the generic ACCEPT today, but once the DROP rule above is restored and if an admin narrows the generic ACCEPT, LAN access to the mesh (other kiwi nodes, master 10.8.0.1) is lost. Also no fleet setting for the mesh CIDR. — fix: Add `VPN_SUBNET="{{ vpn_subnet | default('10.8.0.0/16') }}"` and `iptables -I FORWARD -i "$PUB_IFACE" -d "$VPN_SUBNET" -j ACCEPT`.
- [medium] **Return-path rule differs: live uses INPUT, template uses FORWARD** — template: gw.sh.j2:34 `iptables -A FORWARD -i $NETWORK_NAME -o $PUB_IFACE -m state --state RELATED,ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT` — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:54 / gw.sh:37 `iptables -A/-I INPUT -i knet-node -m state --state RELATED,ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT` (no FORWARD return rule; relies on Docker's `-i knet-node ! -o knet-node -j ACCEPT`) — impact: Behaviour differs rather than breaks: the template's FORWARD rule is the more correct one for forwarded flows, but the live INPUT rule (host-terminated replies from containers when the host INPUT policy is DROP) is absent, so a hardened host firewall would block container->host replies (e.g. Pi-hole answering the host's own resolver queries). — fix: Keep the FORWARD rule and also add the live INPUT rule: `iptables -I INPUT -i "$NETWORK_NAME" -m state --state RELATED,ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT`.
- [medium] **Not idempotent: iptables rules accumulate on every restart; ExecStop only clears routes** — template: gw.sh.j2:17-18 deletes only `ip rule`/`ip route` state, then `-A` appends iptables rules; kn-gateway.service.j2:10 ExecStop clears only table 128 — live: kn-service/gw.sh:12-16 explicitly `iptables -D ...` every rule (`|| true`) before re-adding; kiwi-node-gw.sh stop():78-86 removes all rules; live cron restarts the unit daily (kiwi-node-gw.sh:28) — impact: Each `systemctl restart kn-gateway` (or re-run) adds another copy of the three FORWARD/POSTROUTING rules; after weeks of daily restarts the chains contain hundreds of duplicates and `systemctl stop` leaves MASQUERADE/FORWARD rules in place (LAN still forwarded while routing is torn down). — fix: Mirror live: `iptables -D ... 2>/dev/null || true` for each rule before adding (or use `iptables -C ... || iptables -I ...`), and make ExecStop run the same script with a `stop` argument that removes the iptables rules too. Also loop `while ip rule del table 128 2>/dev/null; do :; done` so stacked rules are fully cleared.
- [medium] **Cron maintenance (daily routing refresh, weekly stack update) absent** — template: Nothing; module.yaml has no cron/host_integration entry for it — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:26-30 writes /etc/cron.d/kn-docker: `01 07 * * * root systemctl restart kiwi-gw` and `0 0 * * 0 root docker compose ... down && ... up --detach --pull always && docker image prune -f && docker volume prune -f && systemctl restart kiwi-gw`; removed in stop():100 — impact: No automatic recovery of the routing table after gluetun reconnects/restarts and no weekly image refresh; the live operator relies on these. — fix: Add a `host_integration.cron` entry (or a systemd timer template) rendering the same two jobs with the new unit name; note the live update() at kiwi-node-gw.sh:109 references the wrong compose file (`docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml`), do not port that.
- [low] **PUB_IFACE source: static fleet value vs runtime auto-detect** — template: gw.sh.j2:9 static `PUB_IFACE="{{ pub_iface }}"` — live: kn-service/gw.sh:3 `PUB_IFACE=$(ip route get 8.8.8.8 | grep -oP '(?<=dev )[^ ]+')` (auto-detect the default-route interface), while .env:15 and kiwi-node-gw.sh use static `PUB_IFACE=eth0` — impact: Static is more predictable (auto-detect picks the WAN interface, which only equals the LAN interface on single-NIC boxes). Behaviour differs only when pub_iface is left empty: live falls back to detection, template renders `iif ""` and fails. — fix: Keep static but fall back: `PUB_IFACE="{{ pub_iface }}"; [ -n "$PUB_IFACE" ] || PUB_IFACE=$(ip route get 8.8.8.8 | sed -n 's/.* dev \([^ ]*\).*/\1/p')`.
- [low] **Unit name, unit path and script path renamed** — template: kn-gateway.service (module.yaml:60), installed per compose comment l.9 at /etc/systemd/system/, ExecStart=/opt/kiwi/gw.sh (kn-gateway.service.j2:9), Requires=docker.service, RemainAfterExit=yes, ExecStop cleanup — live: kiwi-node-gw.sh:61-73 writes /lib/systemd/system/kiwi-gw.service with ExecStart=/home/user/docker/kn-service/gw.sh, Type=oneshot, only After=docker.service, no ExecStop; cron and gw.sh:42 call `systemctl restart kiwi-gw` — impact: Cosmetic rename plus harmless improvements (Requires, RemainAfterExit, ExecStop), but anything ported from live that calls `kiwi-gw` (cron, the self-restart in gw.sh) must be updated. — fix: Keep kn-gateway.service, make the script path a variable (`{{ gw_script_path | default(docker_dir ~ '/kn-service/gw.sh') }}`) and update every reference consistently (see bugs).
- [low] **Live gw.sh throws/blocks a /16 while compose and .env use /24** — template: DOCKER_SUBNET from node network.docker_subnet = 172.128.0.0/24 — live: kn-service/gw.sh:6 hard-codes `DOCKER_SUBNET=172.128.0.0/16` whereas .env:16 has `172.128.0.0/24` and compose l.201 `subnet: 172.128.0.0/24` — impact: Live inconsistency, not a v2 regression; the /24 the template uses is the correct value. Mentioned so the porter does not copy the /16. — fix: Keep /24 from the renderer context.

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **container_ip resolves to the gateway module's placeholder IP (.10), not the VPN container** (modules/gateway/gw.sh.j2:10 with scripts/kiwi-render/kiwi-render.py:264 (`"gateway": f"{base}.10"`) and :416 (`module_ctx["container_ip"] = container_ips.get(module_name)`)): The script needs the vpn-client container IP as next hop. The renderer injects container_ips['gateway'] = 172.128.0.10 (no container lives there). Verified by rendering examples/node-gw.yaml with `module_config.gateway.container_ip` removed: output is `CONTAINER_IP="172.128.0.10"`. The example only works because node-gw.yaml:50 hard-codes 172.128.0.2. — fix: Change gw.sh.j2:10 to `CONTAINER_IP="{{ vpn_container_ip | default(container_ips['vpn-client']) }}"` and drop the 'gateway' entry from the IP map (the module has no container).
- **Three contradictory locations for gw.sh, including a bind mount into a container that does not exist** (module.yaml:48-49 (`host: "${DOCKERDIR}/kn-service/gw.sh"` -> `container: "/kiwi/gw.sh"`), module.yaml:62 and kn-gateway.service.j2:9 (`/opt/kiwi/gw.sh`), docker-compose.yml.j2:8 (`/opt/kiwi/gw.sh`)): storage.bind_mounts declares a container mount, but docker-compose.yml.j2 spawns no service, so nothing consumes it; the systemd unit expects /opt/kiwi/gw.sh while the renderer writes the file to `<output>/gateway/gw.sh` and no installer copies it anywhere. Live uses `$DOCKERDIR/kn-service/gw.sh`. — fix: Remove storage.bind_mounts; add `host_integration.files: [{src: gw.sh, dest: "{{ gw_script_path }}", mode: '0755'}]` and template the same path into ExecStart.
- **firewall.rules mixes `ip rule`/`ip route` shell commands and `|| true` into an 'iptables format' list and uses undocumented variables** (module.yaml:22-29): module-schema.md:36-38 says rules are 'iptables format with ${VAR} substitution' and documents only ${VPN_IP}, ${DOCKER_SUBNET}, ${DOCKER_SUBNET2}, ${LAN_IFACE}. Lines 25-26 are `ip` commands with a shell `|| true`, lines 25/26/28 use ${PUB_IFACE}/${CONTAINER_IP}. A consumer that prefixes `iptables` to each entry would produce `iptables ip rule add ...`. The list also diverges from gw.sh.j2 (no RELATED,ESTABLISHED rule, no throw, no DROP) so there are two sources of truth. kiwi-render.py never reads firewall.rules. — fix: Either delete firewall.rules for this module (gw.sh is the single source of truth) or keep only true iptables rules there, rename ${PUB_IFACE} -> ${LAN_IFACE}, and move the `ip rule/route` lines to a `routing:` key.
- **DOCKER_SUBNET listed as env.required although always renderer-provided; variable is dead in the script** (module.yaml:35; gw.sh.j2:11): `kiwi-render.py validate examples/node-gw.yaml` prints `Warning: Module 'gateway' requires 'DOCKER_SUBNET'` and the generated .env.example contains `DOCKER_SUBNET=  # REQUIRED` (verified), because validate/_find_var_value only search module_config and variables, not `network:`. Meanwhile gw.sh.j2 assigns DOCKER_SUBNET and never uses it. — fix: Drop DOCKER_SUBNET (and NETWORK_NAME) from env; use the variable for the `throw` route and DROP rule (see discrepancies).
- **host_integration.restart: on-failure is not rendered into the unit; Type=oneshot has no restart path** (module.yaml:63 vs kn-gateway.service.j2:6-10): The unit template has no `Restart=` line, so the metadata is a lie; combined with `exit 1` on ping failure the service just stays failed. — fix: Render `Restart={{ restart | default('on-failure') }}` and `RestartSec=10` (verify the target systemd accepts Restart= on oneshot; otherwise use Type=simple + wait loop).
- **Health check can never fail and has no container to run in** (module.yaml:52-55 `type: command`, `target: "ip route show table 128"`): `ip route show table 128` exits 0 even when the table is empty, so the check is always green; the schema's health block is per-container and this module has none, so the renderer/installer has nowhere to attach it. — fix: Use `ip route show table 128 | grep -q '^default via'` and mark it as a host-level check (new key, e.g. `host_integration.health`).
- **dependencies.optional references a module that does not exist** (module.yaml:14 `- dhcp`): kiwi-v2/modules/ contains no `dhcp` directory (verified with find). Listing it under `modules:` in a fleet file makes ModuleLoader.load raise `Module 'dhcp' not found`. The live function is kndhcphelper (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:73-86). — fix: Create a `dhcp-relay` module (homeall/dhcphelper, network_mode host, env IP=<dns container ip>, cap NET_ADMIN) or drop the entry.
- **templates.env_example points at a file that does not exist** (module.yaml:43 `env_example: .env.example`; modules/gateway/ has only 4 files): Harmless today because render_module_configs skips the env_example key (kiwi-render.py:403), but any stricter loader will fail. — fix: Remove the key or add the file.
- **Compose template is comment-only but still registered as `templates.compose`; comment paths are wrong** (module.yaml:40, docker-compose.yml.j2:17 (`See templates/gw.sh.j2 and templates/kn-gateway.service.j2`)): Merged output parses (verified with yaml.safe_load: gateway contributes zero services), so no YAML breakage, but the module needlessly participates in compose generation, and the referenced paths are modules/gateway/*.j2, not templates/*.j2. — fix: Delete docker-compose.yml.j2 and the `compose:` key (renderer already `continue`s when absent, kiwi-render.py:283-284); fix the comment if kept.
- **Systemd unit template contains no Jinja variables, so docker_dir / script path cannot vary per node** (kn-gateway.service.j2:9): Every other module parameterises paths with `{{ docker_dir }}`; this unit hard-codes /opt/kiwi/gw.sh which contradicts module.yaml:48. Also lacks `Wants=network-online.target`. — fix: `ExecStart={{ gw_script_path | default(docker_dir ~ '/kn-service/gw.sh') }}`, `ExecStop=... stop`, add `After=network-online.target docker.service`, `Wants=network-online.target`.

**Present live, missing in v2**

- kndhcphelper DHCP relay (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:73-86: image homeall/dhcphelper:latest, network_mode host, env IP='172.128.0.4' (Pi-hole), TZ, cap_add NET_ADMIN, json-file 10m) - no v2 module; gateway module.yaml points to a non-existent 'dhcp' module
- `ip route add table 128 throw $DOCKER_SUBNET` (kiwi-node-gw.sh:42, gw.sh:25)
- LAN->Docker-subnet DROP rule and LAN->10.8.0.0/16 ACCEPT rule (kiwi-node-gw.sh:45-48, gw.sh:28-31) and the VPN_SUBNET variable
- INPUT `-i knet-node RELATED,ESTABLISHED ACCEPT` rule (kiwi-node-gw.sh:54, gw.sh:37)
- Startup delay / self-retry (gw.sh:2 `sleep 15`, gw.sh:41-43 restart on ping failure) and ordering after `docker compose up` (kiwi-node-gw.sh:32-34)
- Rule cleanup before re-adding (gw.sh:12-20) and full teardown on stop (kiwi-node-gw.sh:77-102 stop(): iptables -D, ip route flush, ip rule delete, compose down, disable unit, remove cron)
- /etc/cron.d/kn-docker: daily 07:01 `systemctl restart kiwi-gw`, weekly Sunday 00:00 compose down/up --pull always + image/volume prune + restart (kiwi-node-gw.sh:26-30)
- Lifecycle wrapper with start/stop/restart/update/del_conf (kiwi-node-gw.sh:118-120) including envsubst generation of nginx.conf and post-rules.txt from *.example and the `touch .$HOSTNAME` marker
- `com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node` on the Docker network (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:196) - required for the module's interface-based iptables rules; absent from templates/networks.yml.j2
- Other services the live gw node runs that no v2 module/example covers (other modules' area, listed for completeness): knportainer (portainer/portainer-ce, 127.0.0.1:9443, static .250, docker.sock, named volume portainer_data), knsftp (jmcombs/sftp, 2224:22, static .251, `command: $SFTPUSER:$SFTPPASS:1001`, DL_DIR mount), knnginx reverse proxy on the gw node (80/443, .5, plus nginx `stream { listen 2222 -> 192.168.2.20:2222 }` TCP passthrough) - examples/node-gw.yaml does not include reverse-proxy although downloader's nginx blocks need the aggregator
- vpn-client specifics on the gw node (vpn-client module area): `ports: 3129:3129`, `sysctls net.ipv4.tcp_mtu_probing=1 / net.ipv4.tcp_base_mss=1024`, WIREGUARD_MTU=1412, post-rules DNATs `${VPN_IP}:2224 -> 172.128.0.251:22` (sftp), `:3129 -> 172.128.0.5:3129`, `:2222 -> 172.128.0.5:2222`, `:80/:443 -> 172.128.0.5` (note v2 post-rules.txt.j2:21-22 DNATs to `{{ docker_subnet_base }}:443` = `172.128.0:443`, an incomplete address)

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pub_iface` | string | yes |  | LAN interface — Interface facing the LAN clients that use this node as their default gateway (e.g. eth0, enp1s0). Consumed by gw.sh.j2:9 as `ip rule add iif` / `iptables -i`. Leave empty to auto-detect the default-route interface (only correct on single-NIC hosts). |
| `vpn_container_ip` | string |  | <docker_subnet_base>.2 (container_ips['vpn-client']) | VPN next-hop IP — IP of the vpn-client (gluetun) container that LAN traffic is routed through. Normally derived automatically from container_ips['vpn-client']; override only if the VPN container has a non-standard address. Replaces the ambiguous `container_ip` the template currently consumes. |
| `docker_subnet` | string |  | 172.128.0.0/24 | Docker subnet (read-only, node level) — Provided by the renderer from network.docker_subnet; used for the table-128 `throw` route and the LAN->Docker DROP rule once those are restored. Should not be exposed as a module setting, listed here because gw.sh.j2:11 consumes it. |
| `network_name` | string |  | knet-node | Docker bridge name (read-only, node level) — Renderer-provided (knet-node). Used as the Linux interface name in iptables; requires `com.docker.network.bridge.name` to be set to the same value on the network. |
| `vpn_subnet` | string |  | 10.8.0.0/16 | VPN mesh subnet — CIDR of the WireGuard mesh that LAN clients may reach through the gateway (live hard-codes 10.8.0.0/16 in gw.sh:7). Needed once the live ACCEPT rule is restored. |
| `block_lan_to_docker` | bool |  | true | Isolate containers from LAN — Adds the live `FORWARD -i <pub_iface> -d <docker_subnet> -j DROP` rule so LAN clients can only reach published host ports, not container IPs directly. |
| `gw_script_path` | string |  | <docker_dir>/kn-service/gw.sh | Gateway script install path — Where gw.sh is installed on the host and what the systemd unit's ExecStart points to. Live used /home/user/docker/kn-service/gw.sh; the v2 unit hard-codes /opt/kiwi/gw.sh. |
| `startup_wait_seconds` | int |  | 60 | Max wait for VPN container at boot — Upper bound for the ping-wait loop before the unit fails (live: fixed `sleep 15` + restart loop). |
| `daily_restart_cron` | string |  | 01 07 * * * | Daily routing refresh schedule — Cron expression for `systemctl restart kn-gateway` (live: `01 07 * * *`). Empty disables. |
| `weekly_update_cron` | string |  | 0 0 * * 0 | Weekly stack update schedule — Cron expression for compose down/up --pull always + prune + gateway restart (live: `0 0 * * 0`). Empty disables. |

**Host ports**: No INPUT ports are opened by the gateway module itself; it needs FORWARD/NAT rules on the host: `FORWARD -i <pub_iface> -o knet-node ACCEPT`, `FORWARD -i knet-node -o <pub_iface> RELATED,ESTABLISHED ACCEPT`, `nat POSTROUTING -o knet-node MASQUERADE` (inserted ahead of Docker's chains, or via DOCKER-USER / gateway_mode_ipv4=nat-unprotected); sysctl net.ipv4.ip_forward=1 (Docker normally sets it; make it explicit in the unit or /etc/sysctl.d); 53/tcp and 53/udp on <pub_iface> (dns module; LAN clients in 'DNS resolution mode' or full gateway mode resolve via the node, live compose publishes 53 on all interfaces); 67/udp (and 68/udp replies) on <pub_iface> if the kndhcphelper DHCP relay (network_mode host) is restored; ICMP echo from the host to <vpn_container_ip> inside knet-node (gw.sh reachability check; gluetun's default firewall accepts traffic from its own Docker subnet, so no extra rule is needed unless FIREWALL_OUTBOUND_SUBNETS is tightened)

**Host directories**: <docker_dir>/kn-service/ (live location of gw.sh and what module.yaml:48 references) - or /opt/kiwi/ if the kn-gateway.service.j2:9 path is kept; the two must be reconciled, only one is needed; /etc/systemd/system/ (install target for kn-gateway.service; exists on any systemd host); /etc/cron.d/ (for the kn-docker maintenance file, if the live cron jobs are ported; exists on Debian); No bind-mount directories: the module spawns no container, so module.yaml storage.bind_mounts (`${DOCKERDIR}/kn-service/gw.sh -> /kiwi/gw.sh`) should be removed rather than created

<details><summary>Template variables (6)</summary>

- `pub_iface` — gw.sh.j2:9 `PUB_IFACE="{{ pub_iface }}"` (then `ip rule add iif "$PUB_IFACE"` l.28, `iptables -A FORWARD -i "$PUB_IFACE"` l.33-34) — required — default (none; Jinja SilentUndefined renders empty string -> `PUB_IFACE=""` and `ip rule add iif ""` fails) — Not provided by the renderer. Must come from `variables.pub_iface` or `module_config.gateway.pub_iface` (examples/node-gw.yaml sets BOTH, l.22 and l.49). module.yaml env.required spells it PUB_IFACE; docs/module-schema.md:43 documents the equivalent firewall variable as ${LAN_IFACE}, so three spellings exist.
- `container_ip` — gw.sh.j2:10 `CONTAINER_IP="{{ container_ip }}"` (next hop in `ip route add table 128 default via` l.29; ping target l.21) — required — default Renderer auto-assigns `{{docker_subnet_base}}.10` because kiwi-render.py:264 maps module 'gateway' -> `.10` (render_module_configs l.416 sets module_ctx['container_ip']=container_ips['gateway']) — SEMANTICALLY WRONG DEFAULT. The script needs the vpn-client container's IP (live: 172.128.0.2). Verified: rendering examples/node-gw.yaml gives 172.128.0.2 only because module_config.gateway.container_ip overrides it (node-gw.yaml:50); with that line removed the rendered script says `CONTAINER_IP="172.128.0.10"` (no container there). Template should use `{{ container_ips['vpn-client'] }}`.
- `docker_subnet` — gw.sh.j2:11 `DOCKER_SUBNET="{{ docker_subnet }}"` — default Always provided by renderer from node `network.docker_subnet`, fallback 172.128.0.0/24 for nodes (kiwi-render.py:228) — Assigned but NEVER used in the script body (the live `throw` route and LAN->docker DROP rule that consume it were dropped). Listed as env.required in module.yaml:35, so `kiwi-render.py validate` prints a false `Warning: Module 'gateway' requires 'DOCKER_SUBNET'` and `.env.example` emits `DOCKER_SUBNET=  # REQUIRED` (verified), because _find_var_value only looks at module_config/variables, not `network:`.
- `network_name` — gw.sh.j2:12 `NETWORK_NAME="{{ network_name | default('knet-node') }}"`, used as iptables INTERFACE name `-o "$NETWORK_NAME"` l.33-35 — default 'knet-node' (Jinja default; also env.optional NETWORK_NAME lowercased by renderer l.426; also renderer base ctx l.215 = knet-node for nodes / knet-master for master) — Used as a Linux interface name, which only works if the Docker bridge is named knet-node via `com.docker.network.bridge.name` (live compose l.196). v2 templates/networks.yml.j2 does not set that option, so the interface would be `br-<hash>` and every rule silently matches nothing (iptables does not validate interface existence).
- `${PUB_IFACE}, ${CONTAINER_IP} (shell-style, module.yaml firewall.rules l.25-28)` — module.yaml:25 `ip rule add iif ${PUB_IFACE} table 128 || true`, :26 `ip route add table 128 default via ${CONTAINER_IP}`, :28 `-A FORWARD -i ${PUB_IFACE} -o knet-node -j ACCEPT` — required — default (none) — Not Jinja and not in the documented substitution set (module-schema.md:39-43 lists only VPN_IP, DOCKER_SUBNET, DOCKER_SUBNET2, LAN_IFACE). No code in kiwi-render.py consumes firewall.rules at all.
- `(none)` — kn-gateway.service.j2 (whole file) and docker-compose.yml.j2 (whole file, comments only) — default n/a — The systemd unit hard-codes `ExecStart=/opt/kiwi/gw.sh` with no variable; the compose template renders to comments only (verified: merged compose parses, gateway contributes zero services).

</details>

### Module `reverse-proxy (kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy). Relative paths below are under /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/ ; render outputs cited are under /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/render-out/`

**Discrepancies vs live**

- [high] **No HTTPS server blocks or upstreams are rendered** — template: nginx.conf.j2:41-45 and :54-94 loop over 'upstreams' and 'servers', which kiwi-render.py never provides; rendered render-out/node-cloud/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf contains only the port-80 redirect and no 'listen 443' at all. — live: kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf.example:39-48 upstreams vaultwarden-default (knvault:80), portainer-default (knport:9000); servers for $NC_HOSTNAME (:64-120), $NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME (:123-137), vault.$HOSTNAME (:140-159), portainer.$HOSTNAME (:162-181). kiwi-node-gw/knnginx/nginx.conf.example: upstreams kntransmission-default (knvpn-client:9091), knportainer-default (knportainer:9000); servers $PIHOLE_HOSTNAME (:66-103), $TM_HOSTNAME (:106-125), $PT_HOSTNAME (:128-147). — impact: Nothing is served over HTTPS; module.yaml health check tcp localhost:443 can never pass; every dependent module (cloud, vault) is unreachable. — fix: In the renderer, collect the nginx: section of every enabled module (normalise cloud/vault single-server form and downloader servers[] list into one list), Jinja-render their strings with the module context, and pass 'upstreams' and 'servers' into the reverse-proxy context; also generate the live-equivalent blocks listed in nginx_blocks.
- [high] **nginx is not attached to the nextcloud-aio network** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:9-14 joins {{network_name}} only; the nextcloud-aio membership is behind {% if nextcloud_network %} which nothing sets. — live: kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:58-61 knnginx joins knet-node (172.129.0.3) AND nextcloud-aio. — impact: nextcloud-aio-apache and the AIO containers only exist on the nextcloud-aio network, so proxy_pass http://nextcloud-aio-apache:11000 cannot resolve: Nextcloud cannot be proxied at all. — fix: Have kiwi-render.py pass has_nextcloud (already computed at :277) into each module context and change the template to '{% if has_nextcloud %}      nextcloud-aio:{% endif %}'; keep nextcloud_network only as an override.
- [high] **nextcloud-aio network declared external (first boot fails)** — template: templates/networks.yml.j2:29-33 declares 'nextcloud-aio: external: true' when the cloud module is enabled. — live: kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:321-323 declares 'nextcloud-aio: name: nextcloud-aio' (external: true commented out) so compose creates it and AIO reuses it. — impact: On a fresh node 'docker compose up' aborts with 'network nextcloud-aio declared as external, but could not be found' because AIO only creates that network after the mastercontainer has been configured; kn-nginx (and the whole stack) never starts. — fix: Declare it like live: 'nextcloud-aio:\n    name: nextcloud-aio' (non-external), or create it in a pre-up step before compose.
- [high] **Static proxy_pass hostnames vs live resolver + variable pattern** — template: nginx.conf.j2:68 'proxy_pass {{ server.proxy_pass }};' resolved by nginx at startup. — live: Hand-edited kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf:72-73 'resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=10s; resolver_timeout 5s;' and :105-106 'set $nc_aio_apache_hn nextcloud-aio-apache; proxy_pass http://$nc_aio_apache_hn:11000;' (not in the .example). — impact: With a static hostname nginx exits with '[emerg] host not found in upstream "nextcloud-aio-apache"' whenever that container does not exist (first boot before AIO setup, AIO updates/backups), taking vault/portainer down with it. The live operator fixed this by hand; the template inherits the broken .example. — fix: Emit 'resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=10s ipv6=off;' in http{} and render hostname targets through a variable ('set $up_<name> <host>; proxy_pass http://$up_<name>:<port>;').
- [medium] **Static IP differs from live cloud node** — template: kiwi-render.py:259 assigns reverse-proxy {base}.5 -> 172.129.0.5 on the cloud example (rendered compose :50). — live: kiwi-cloud compose:60 knnginx 172.129.0.3; kiwi-node-gw compose:107 knnginx 172.128.0.5 (gw matches, cloud does not). — impact: examples/node-cloud.yaml:48 trusted_proxies '172.129.0.3' and the live DNAT target (kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:21-23 -> 172.129.0.3) still point at .3: Nextcloud will not trust the proxy (wrong client IPs, brute-force throttling of the proxy) and VPN ingress targets the wrong container. vault also moves .5 -> .7. — fix: Derive trusted_proxies and the DNAT target from container_ips['reverse-proxy'] instead of literals; or allow per-role ip maps so the cloud role keeps .3.
- [medium] **No depends_on** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 has no depends_on. — live: kiwi-cloud compose:46-49 depends_on knvpn-client, nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer, knvault; kiwi-node-gw compose:93-95 depends_on knvpn-client, kntransmission. — impact: nginx may start before its backends; combined with static proxy_pass hostnames this produces a crash loop until every backend container exists. — fix: Generate depends_on from the modules that contribute server blocks (kn-vpn-client, kn-vault, nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer, ...) and adopt the resolver pattern so missing backends are non-fatal.
- [medium] **Nextcloud-specific directives cannot be expressed** — template: Generic location / with Host, X-Real-IP, X-Forwarded-For, X-Forwarded-Proto and optional hsts/client_max_body_size/proxy_read_timeout (nginx.conf.j2:67-86); cloud/module.yaml:69-72 'extra_config' is never consumed. — live: kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf.example:64-120: http2 on; HSTS; fastcgi_buffers 64 4K; client_body_buffer_size 512k; tuned gzip/gzip_types; Referrer-Policy no-referrer; X-Permitted-Cross-Domain-Policies none; X-Robots-Tag noindex; fastcgi_hide_header X-Powered-By; proxy_read_timeout 86400s; client_body_timeout 86400s; client_max_body_size 0; types { text/javascript mjs; }; proxy_redirect off; X-Forwarded-Host $server_name; Upgrade/Connection $http_connection; four .well-known rewrites (carddav, caldav, webfinger, nodeinfo). — impact: CalDAV/CardDAV/webfinger autodiscovery breaks, Nextcloud admin security checks warn, large uploads time out at nginx default 60s, .mjs served with wrong MIME type. — fix: Add servers[].extra_config (raw text at server{} level) and servers[].location_extra (raw text inside location /) to the aggregator and let the cloud module ship the live block verbatim.
- [medium] **WebSocket Connection header hardcoded** — template: nginx.conf.j2:75-76 'proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade; proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade";' when websocket. — live: Both examples define 'map $http_upgrade $connection_upgrade { default upgrade; "" close; }' (:34-37) and use 'Connection $connection_upgrade' (cloud :150,173; gw :116,138). — impact: Every proxied request on websocket-enabled servers carries 'Connection: upgrade' even without an Upgrade header; keepalive semantics differ from live. — fix: Emit the map in http{} and use 'proxy_set_header Connection $connection_upgrade;'.
- [medium] **Per-service hostname variables not modelled** — template: module.yaml:26 declares HOSTNAME required but neither template references it; no NC_HOSTNAME/PIHOLE_HOSTNAME/TM_HOSTNAME/PT_HOSTNAME concept in reverse-proxy. — live: kiwi-cloud/kiwi-node.sh:20 envsubst '${HOSTNAME},${NC_HOSTNAME},${NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME}'; kiwi-node-gw/kiwi-node-gw.sh:19 envsubst of HOSTNAME, PIHOLE_HOSTNAME, JD_HOSTNAME, TM_HOSTNAME, PT_HOSTNAME, OT_HOSTNAME, FFS_HOSTNAME; values in the live .env (e.g. NC_HOSTNAME=cloud.<hostname>, PIHOLE_HOSTNAME=pihole.<hostname>, TM_HOSTNAME=transmission.<hostname>, PT_HOSTNAME=portainer.<hostname>). — impact: No server_name reaches nginx; rendered .env.example duplicates HOSTNAME under reverse-proxy. — fix: Contributor modules provide server_name from their own settings (nc_hostname, pihole_hostname, ...) defaulting to '<svc>.{{ hostname }}'; drop HOSTNAME from reverse-proxy env.required or consume it for the port-80 server_name.
- [medium] **stream{} TCP forwarding block missing** — template: nginx.conf.j2 has only an http{} block. — live: kiwi-node-gw/knnginx/nginx.conf.example:150-164 'stream { server { listen 2222; proxy_pass 192.168.2.20:2222; } }' (plus commented 3129 -> knjdownloader); hand-edited kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf:189-195 forwards 2222 -> 192.168.2.5:2222; paired DNAT vpn_ip:2222 -> nginx ip (kiwi-node-gw/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt.example:15, kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:23). — impact: SSH to a LAN host via <vpn_ip>:2222 over the mesh is lost. — fix: Add an optional stream_forwards list ({listen, target}) rendered into a stream{} block and generate matching extra_dnat_rules for vpn-client.
- [low] **Image tag** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:7 image: nginx:alpine — live: kiwi-cloud compose:43 and kiwi-node-gw compose:90 image: nginx:latest — impact: Different base (musl, no bash); stream/http2/ssl modules are present in both official images so functionality is equivalent. — fix: Keep alpine but expose an nginx_image setting (default nginx:alpine) so the live tag can be chosen; consider pinning a major (nginx:1-alpine).
- [low] **server_tokens not disabled** — template: No server_tokens directive. — live: 'server_tokens off;' at http level and inside server blocks (cloud example :24,57,70; gw example :24,57). — impact: nginx version disclosed in headers/error pages. — fix: Add 'server_tokens off;' in http{}.
- [low] **http-level defaults differ (headers, gzip, body size)** — template: nginx.conf.j2:27 'gzip on', :36-39 four add_header at http level, :81 client_max_body_size 0 for every server, :25 tcp_nopush on. — live: No http-level add_header/gzip; headers, gzip and client_max_body_size 0 only inside the Nextcloud server; vault/portainer/pihole keep nginx default 1m; tcp_nopush commented. — impact: Unlimited request bodies to vault/portainer/pihole; http-level headers silently vanish on any server that sets HSTS (nginx add_header inheritance). — fix: Default client_max_body_size to nginx default unless the server sets it; emit the security headers inside every server block rather than at http level.
- [low] **Port-80 redirect scope** — template: nginx.conf.j2:48-52 'listen 80 default_server; server_name _;' — live: 'listen 80; server_name *.$HOSTNAME $HOSTNAME;' (cloud :52-61, gw :52-61). — impact: Template redirects any Host header (superset of live behaviour); harmless. — fix: None required; optionally use '*.{{ hostname }} {{ hostname }}' for parity.
- [low] **Upstream options** — template: nginx.conf.j2:42-44 'upstream { server X; }' — live: 'zone <name> 64k; keepalive 2;' on every upstream (cloud :39-48, gw :39-48). — impact: No backend keepalive. — fix: Add 'keepalive 2;' (and zone if desired) to the upstream template.
- [low] **Config generation semantics (generate-once vs always regenerate)** — template: kiwi-render.py:390-446 rewrites output/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf on every run and never copies it to {{docker_dir}}/kn-nginx/. — live: kiwi-node.sh:19-21 / kiwi-node-gw.sh:18-20 only envsubst nginx.conf.example -> nginx.conf if nginx.conf is absent, so hand edits (resolver, stream block in kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf) survive. Note the gw call 'envsubst "${HOSTNAME},..."' expands the values inside double quotes, so its substitution list is wrong; do not inherit that. — impact: Hand edits are lost on re-render; no deploy step places the file where the compose template mounts it. — fix: Render directly to <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/nginx.conf with a managed-file header and provide an escape hatch (extra server blocks / working conf.d include) instead of relying on hand edits.
- [low] **gluetun FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS for VPN ingress** — template: modules/vpn-client/docker-compose.yml.j2 sets no FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS. — live: kiwi-cloud compose:16 knvpn-client FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS="80,443" alongside the nginx DNAT. — impact: Parity only: DNAT'd packets traverse FORWARD (accepted by post-rules), so this mainly matters if nginx ever runs inside the gluetun netns; live sets it regardless. — fix: Have vpn-client emit FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS from the exposed_ports of modules with needs_vpn_dnat (80,443).

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **Aggregator inputs 'servers'/'upstreams' are never produced by the renderer** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2:41,54 and /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/scripts/kiwi-render/kiwi-render.py:390-446): render_module_configs builds module_ctx from node variables, module_config and env.optional only; no code reads other modules' nginx: sections. SilentUndefined makes the for-loops iterate nothing, so the rendered file (render-out/node-cloud/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf) has no 'listen 443' server at all, despite module.yaml:55-56 'nginx: aggregator: true'. — fix: Add an aggregation step: for each enabled module with a nginx: key, Jinja-render its strings with that module's context (so {{ hostname }}, {{ container_prefix }} work), normalise to {upstreams: [...], servers: [...]} and inject into the reverse-proxy context; fail validation if reverse-proxy is enabled with zero servers.
- **Contributor nginx definitions use a different schema, missing files, shell placeholders and wrong container names** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/cloud/module.yaml:46,63-72; .../modules/vault/module.yaml:35,50-56; .../modules/downloader/module.yaml:40,61-67): cloud/vault declare nginx.upstream/server_name/proxy_pass/extra_config/websocket (single server), downloader declares nginx.servers[]; the aggregator expects upstreams[] and servers[] with hsts/extra_locations keys. All three reference templates nginx.server.conf.j2 that do not exist (renderer prints 'Warning: Template not found' for cloud, vault, downloader). Values use shell placeholders '${NC_HOSTNAME}', 'vault.${HOSTNAME}', 'dl.${HOSTNAME}' that Jinja never substitutes, and upstream targets 'knvault:80', 'knvpn-client:9091', 'knvpn-client:5800' violate the kn- prefix convention (v2 containers are kn-vault, kn-vpn-client). The cloud module defines no nc-admin server although live has one. — fix: Standardise on nginx.upstreams[] / nginx.servers[] in every module, write hostnames as '{{ nc_hostname }}' / 'vault.{{ hostname }}', targets as '{{ container_prefix }}-vault:80', add the NC admin block to cloud, and either create the nginx.server.conf.j2 files or drop templates.nginx from those module.yaml files.
- **VPN DNAT to nginx targets an incomplete address** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2:21-22 (and cloud/module.yaml:27-28 firewall.rules)): '--to-destination {{ docker_subnet_base }}:443' renders as '172.129.0:443' (render-out/node-cloud/vpn-client/post-rules.txt:20-21). The live example it was copied from is itself wrong ('${DOCKER_SUBNET2}:443' with DOCKER_SUBNET2=172.129.0.0, i.e. the network address, kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt.example:18-19); the live operator hand-fixed the deployed file to the nginx IP 172.129.0.3 (kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:21-23). The docs also disagree on what DOCKER_SUBNET2 is (module-schema.md:42 '172.129.0.0' vs node-config-schema.md:37 docker_subnet_base '172.129.0'). This is the only path by which mesh clients reach this module. — fix: Use '--to-destination {{ container_ips["reverse-proxy"] }}:443' (and :80, plus 2222 if the stream block is ported) and delete the DOCKER_SUBNET2 variants from cloud/module.yaml.
- **Host bind-mount paths in module.yaml disagree with the compose template (knnginx vs kn-nginx)** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/module.yaml:38-46,57-58 vs docker-compose.yml.j2:24-28): module.yaml storage.bind_mounts and nginx.ssl_cert/ssl_key say '${DOCKERDIR}/knnginx/...' (live layout) while the compose template mounts '{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-nginx/...' which renders '/home/user/docker/kn-nginx/...'. A generator that pre-creates directories from module.yaml will create knnginx/, the container will bind-mount non-existent kn-nginx/nginx.conf and Docker will create a directory there, so nginx fails with 'nginx.conf is a directory'. node-config-schema.md:90 and examples/node-cloud.yaml:35 also say knnginx. — fix: Use one spelling in both places (e.g. '${DOCKERDIR}/${CONTAINER_PREFIX}-nginx/...' or literal kn-nginx) and make the pre-flight step create the files, not just the directory.
- **nextcloud_network and has_nextcloud are decided independently** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/docker-compose.yml.j2:12-14 and kiwi-render.py:277,325-327): The service-level network key comes from an undocumented module_config value 'nextcloud_network' while the top-level declaration comes from has_nextcloud (cloud in modules). Setting nextcloud_network on a node without the cloud module (render-out/rp-only) yields 'nextcloud-aio: null' under kn-nginx with no networks: declaration -> compose error 'service kn-nginx refers to undefined network nextcloud-aio'; not setting it on a cloud node leaves nginx unable to reach the AIO containers. — fix: Pass has_nextcloud (or the enabled module list) into every module context and gate both places on it.
- **extra_conf_dir mount is inert** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/docker-compose.yml.j2:27-29 and nginx.conf.j2 (no include)): The flag mounts <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/conf.d to /etc/nginx/conf.d:ro but nginx.conf.j2 contains no 'include /etc/nginx/conf.d/*.conf;' so nothing in it is ever loaded. — fix: Emit '{% if extra_conf_dir %}    include /etc/nginx/conf.d/*.conf;{% endif %}' inside http{} (and create the directory in pre-flight).
- **http-level security headers are dropped on servers that enable HSTS** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2:36-39,63-65): nginx inherits add_header from the parent level only if the current level defines no add_header. Any server with hsts: true adds Strict-Transport-Security at server level and therefore loses X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, X-XSS-Protection and Referrer-Policy. — fix: Render the full header set inside every server block (or an included snippet) instead of at http level.
- **Deprecated 'listen 443 ssl http2' form** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2:57): The http2 parameter of listen is deprecated since nginx 1.25.1; nginx:alpine (1.27+) logs 'the "listen ... http2" directive is deprecated' on every start. The live Nextcloud block already uses 'listen 443 ssl; http2 on;' (cloud example :65-66). — fix: Use 'listen 443 ssl;' plus 'http2 on;'.
- **Static proxy_pass hostnames make nginx refuse to start when a backend is absent** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2:68): nginx resolves literal upstream hostnames at config load; nextcloud-aio-apache does not exist until AIO has been set up (and disappears during AIO updates/backups), so the whole proxy (vault, portainer, pihole too) goes down. The live operator worked around this by hand (kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf:72-73,105-106). — fix: Add 'resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=10s ipv6=off;' and render hostname targets via 'set $up <host>; proxy_pass http://$up:<port>;'.
- **Merged compose file does not parse once the cloud module is included** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/cloud/docker-compose.yml.j2:35-37 with kiwi-render.py:348-374 (_build_compose_file)): Cross-module but it blocks deploying kn-nginx: the cloud template carries a top-level 'volumes:' which the merger indents by two spaces under services (rendered node-cloud docker-compose.yml:94-96) -> yaml.safe_load fails ('expected <block end>, but found <block mapping start>' at line 94). A vpn-client + reverse-proxy-only render parses cleanly, so the reverse-proxy template itself is valid YAML after merging. — fix: Remove top-level volumes:/networks: from module compose templates (storage.volumes in module.yaml already feeds the merger) or make _build_compose_file split service and non-service sections.
- **module.yaml declares unused/unread keys and a missing file** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/reverse-proxy/module.yaml:26,28,33,55-58): env.required HOSTNAME and env.optional TZ are consumed by neither template (tz is injected into the context by kiwi-render.py:306-308 and ignored; .env.example gets a duplicate HOSTNAME line). templates.env_example '.env.example' points to a file that does not exist in the module directory (the renderer skips the key at :403, but module-schema.md:56 defines it as a path). nginx.aggregator/ssl_cert/ssl_key are outside module-schema.md and nothing reads them. — fix: Drop HOSTNAME/TZ from env (or consume hostname for the port-80 server_name), remove or add the .env.example file, and either document the nginx: aggregator keys in module-schema.md or delete them.

**Present live, missing in v2**

- Aggregation of per-service HTTPS server blocks: Nextcloud ($NC_HOSTNAME), Nextcloud AIO admin ($NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME -> https://nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer:8080; no v2 definition at all), Vaultwarden (vault.$HOSTNAME), Portainer (portainer.$HOSTNAME / $PT_HOSTNAME; Portainer is not a v2 module), Pi-hole admin ($PIHOLE_HOSTNAME -> knpihole:80/admin/ with /admin/ and /api/ locations and proxy_hide_header X-Frame-Options; the dns module has no nginx: section), Transmission ($TM_HOSTNAME -> knvpn-client:9091)
- nginx stream{} TCP forwarding (live: listen 2222 -> LAN host 192.168.2.x:2222; commented 3129 -> knjdownloader for MyJDownloader direct connect) and the matching DNAT rules vpn_ip:2222/3129 -> nginx container IP (kiwi-node-gw/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt.example:13-18, kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:21-23)
- nginx joining the nextcloud-aio Docker network, and compose creating that network (non-external) so first boot works
- depends_on ordering on backends (knvpn-client, nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer, knvault / kntransmission)
- resolver 127.0.0.11 + variable proxy_pass so nginx survives absent AIO containers (hand edit in the deployed kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf)
- Nextcloud reverse-proxy block details: .well-known carddav/caldav/webfinger/nodeinfo rewrites, X-Forwarded-Host, proxy_redirect off, Connection $http_connection, 86400s proxy_read_timeout and client_body_timeout, gzip types, 'types { text/javascript mjs; }', extra headers, fastcgi_hide_header
- server_tokens off and the $http_upgrade -> $connection_upgrade map
- Per-service hostname variables (NC_HOSTNAME, NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME, PIHOLE_HOSTNAME, TM_HOSTNAME, PT_HOSTNAME, JD_HOSTNAME; OT_HOSTNAME and FFS_HOSTNAME reserved in the gw .env for olivetin/ff-sync with no containers yet) and the envsubst 'generate nginx.conf only if absent' flow
- Certificate provisioning: ksslgen.sh new_ca / new_cert <domain> produces a wildcard *.<domain> cert signed by kiwiCA (ksslgen/ksslgen.sh:15-31); the live knnginx expects fullchain.pem (= <domain>.pem + kiwiCA.pem) and privkey.pem (= <domain>.key) copied into $DOCKERDIR/knnginx/. v2 has no step that generates, chains or places these files (TODO.md:81 'Certificate distribution')
- gluetun FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS="80,443" on the cloud vpn-client for the nginx DNAT path
- Deploy glue: copying the rendered nginx.conf into <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/ before compose up (renderer writes to output/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf only)
- Daily/weekly cron maintenance that restarts the stack (kiwi-node.sh:30-35, kiwi-node-gw.sh:26-30), which also restarts nginx; not reverse-proxy specific but absent in v2

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hostname` | string | yes |  | Node base domain — Base domain used for every server_name (live HOSTNAME, e.g. sh3.kiwi or m1.kiwi); contributed blocks default to <service>.<hostname>, so the certificate must cover *.<hostname>. |
| `ssl_fullchain` | file | yes |  | TLS certificate chain (fullchain.pem) — PEM chain mounted read-only at /etc/nginx/fullchain.pem. With ksslgen: concatenate <domain>.pem and kiwiCA.pem. |
| `ssl_privkey` | secret | yes |  | TLS private key (privkey.pem) — PEM private key mounted read-only at /etc/nginx/privkey.pem (ksslgen <domain>.key). Store encrypted; never commit. |
| `docker_dir` | string |  | /home/user/docker | Docker config directory — Host directory holding kn-nginx/{nginx.conf,fullchain.pem,privkey.pem}; node-level variable consumed by the compose template bind mounts. |
| `nginx_image` | string |  | nginx:alpine | nginx image — Image for the kn-nginx container; the live stacks run nginx:latest. Both official variants include stream, http2 and ssl. |
| `join_nextcloud_network` | bool |  | auto (true when the cloud module is enabled) | Attach to nextcloud-aio network — Maps to the template's nextcloud_network; required for proxy_pass to nextcloud-aio-apache / nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer. |
| `extra_conf_dir` | bool |  | false | Mount conf.d for hand-written snippets — Mounts <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/conf.d into /etc/nginx/conf.d (only useful once nginx.conf.j2 includes it). |
| `default_client_max_body_size` | string |  | 0 | Default upload size limit — Applied to every server block that does not set its own client_max_body_size (template currently defaults to 0 = unlimited; live only unlimits Nextcloud). |
| `extra_servers` | list |  | [] | Additional HTTPS server blocks — Appended to the aggregated 'servers' list; each item: {server_name, proxy_pass, websocket, hsts, client_max_body_size, proxy_read_timeout, extra_locations:[{path, config}]}. Use for Portainer, LAN hosts or anything without a module. |
| `extra_upstreams` | list |  | [] | Additional upstream groups — Appended to 'upstreams'; each item: {name, server}. |
| `stream_forwards` | list |  | [] | Raw TCP forwards (stream block) — Each item {listen, target} renders a stream{} server (live: 2222 -> <lan-host>:2222) and should also produce a DNAT from the node VPN IP to the nginx container for that port. |
