| `hsts` | bool |  | false | Enable HSTS on Nextcloud-style blocks — Per-server flag in the aggregator (servers[].hsts); live enables it only on the Nextcloud block. Header set must be re-emitted inside the block (see add_header bug). |

**Reverse-proxy blocks**: _ (port 80 catch-all; live: *.<hostname> <hostname>) → none - return 301 https://$host$request_uri; <nc_hostname> (live NC_HOSTNAME = cloud.<hostname>) → http://nextcloud-aio-apache:11000 (via resolver + variable; container lives only on the nextcloud-aio network) (websocket); <nc_admin_hostname> (live NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME = nc-admin.<hostname>) → https://nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer:8080; vault.<hostname> → http://kn-vault:80 (upstream vaultwarden-default; live knvault:80) (websocket); portainer.<hostname> (live PT_HOSTNAME) → http://kn-portainer:9000 (live knport:9000 on cloud, knportainer:9000 on gw) (websocket); pihole.<hostname> (live PIHOLE_HOSTNAME) → http://kn-pihole:80/admin/ (live knpihole:80/admin/); transmission.<hostname> (live TM_HOSTNAME; v2 downloader says dl.<hostname>) → http://kn-vpn-client:9091 (transmission runs in network_mode service:kn-vpn-client; live upstream kntransmission-default -> knvpn-client:9091) (websocket); jdownloader.<hostname> (live JD_HOSTNAME reserved; v2 jd.<hostname>) → http://kn-vpn-client:5800 (as declared by downloader/module.yaml:65-67) (websocket); (stream block, listen 2222 - TCP, not HTTP) → <lan_ssh_host>:2222 (live 192.168.2.20 in gw example :150-157; 192.168.2.5 in deployed cloud nginx.conf:189-195)

**Host ports**: 80/tcp (LAN interface; nginx HTTP redirect; compose publishes 80:80 on all interfaces like live); 443/tcp (LAN interface; nginx HTTPS; compose publishes 443:443); (VPN side) 80/tcp and 443/tcp arrive on <vpn_ip> and are DNAT'd inside the gluetun container to the nginx container IP - no host firewall rule, but the post-rules DNAT must target container_ips['reverse-proxy'] and gluetun should set FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS=80,443 for parity; 2222/tcp only if the live stream forward is ported - reached via VPN DNAT to the container IP, not host-published; No ports on the master: the master stack has no nginx (kiwi-master compose exposes Pi-hole UI on 127.0.0.1:8080 directly)

**Host directories**: <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/ (path the compose template mounts; module.yaml and the live layout say knnginx/ - pick one) containing REGULAR FILES nginx.conf, fullchain.pem and privkey.pem before 'docker compose up'; if any is missing Docker creates a directory at the mount source and nginx fails to start; <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/conf.d/ only when extra_conf_dir is set (and only useful once nginx.conf.j2 includes it); Copy the rendered output/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf into <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/nginx.conf (no deploy step exists in v2); Certificates: run ksslgen new_cert <hostname> (wildcard *.<hostname>), then fullchain.pem = <hostname>.pem + kiwiCA.pem and privkey.pem = <hostname>.key, placed in <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/ with the key mode 0600; Docker network nextcloud-aio must exist (or be declared non-external) before up when the cloud module is enabled, otherwise kn-nginx's second network attachment fails

<details><summary>Template variables (18)</summary>

- `container_prefix` — docker-compose.yml.j2:6,8 (service key and container_name -> kn-nginx) and :24-28 (host dir {{docker_dir}}/{{container_prefix}}-nginx/) — required — default renderer-provided: 'km' on master, 'kn' on node (kiwi-render.py:214) — Live name is knnginx; the kn- form matches docs/module-schema.md:89 but module.yaml bind_mount/ssl paths still say knnginx (see bugs).
- `network_name` — docker-compose.yml.j2:10 (networks: key) — required — default renderer-provided: knet-master / knet-node (kiwi-render.py:215) — Live: knet-node on both nodes.
- `container_ip` — docker-compose.yml.j2:11 (ipv4_address) — required — default renderer-provided: {docker_subnet_base}.5 (kiwi-render.py:259) — Live knnginx is 172.129.0.3 on kiwi-cloud and 172.128.0.5 on kiwi-node-gw; only the gw value matches.
- `docker_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2:24,25,26,28 (host side of the nginx.conf/fullchain.pem/privkey.pem/conf.d bind mounts) — required — default none in renderer (SilentUndefined renders '' so paths become /kn-nginx/...); examples set /home/user/docker; live DOCKERDIR=/home/user/docker — Comes from node variables.docker_dir (node-config-schema.md:30).
- `nextcloud_network` — docker-compose.yml.j2:12-14 ({% if nextcloud_network %} ... {{ nextcloud_network }}: ) adds a second network to the service — default unset -> block omitted — Never set by the renderer, examples or any module; must be the literal 'nextcloud-aio'. Live cloud knnginx joins nextcloud-aio (kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:61). Only declared in templates/networks.yml.j2 when has_nextcloud (cloud module enabled).
- `extra_conf_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2:27-29 (truthy flag -> mounts {{docker_dir}}/kn-nginx/conf.d:/etc/nginx/conf.d:ro) — default unset -> no mount — Inert: nginx.conf.j2 contains no 'include /etc/nginx/conf.d/*.conf;' (grep confirmed).
- `upstreams` — nginx.conf.j2:41-45 ({% for upstream in upstreams %} upstream {{upstream.name}} { server {{upstream.server}}; }) — default never provided -> no upstream blocks (SilentUndefined iterates empty) — Expected shape: list of {name, server}. Live equivalents: vaultwarden-default->knvault:80, portainer-default->knport:9000 (cloud); kntransmission-default->knvpn-client:9091, knportainer-default->knportainer:9000 (gw), each with 'zone ... 64k; keepalive 2;'.
- `servers` — nginx.conf.j2:54-94 ({% for server in servers %} one 'listen 443 ssl http2' server per entry) — required — default never provided -> zero HTTPS server blocks (verified: render-out/node-cloud/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf has only the port-80 redirect) — Required for the module to do anything; kiwi-render.py:390-446 builds the context from node vars + module_config + env.optional only and never aggregates other modules' nginx: sections.
- `servers[].server_name` — nginx.conf.j2:58 and :55 (fallback comment) — required — default none — Live values come from envsubst of $NC_HOSTNAME, $NC_ADMIN_HOSTNAME, vault.$HOSTNAME, portainer.$HOSTNAME (cloud) and $PIHOLE_HOSTNAME, $TM_HOSTNAME, $PT_HOSTNAME (gw).
- `servers[].proxy_pass` — nginx.conf.j2:68 (static proxy_pass inside location /) — required — default none — Static hostname targets make nginx fail at startup if the container is absent (live hand-edit uses resolver + variable, kiwi-cloud/knnginx/nginx.conf:72-73,105-106).
- `servers[].comment` — nginx.conf.j2:55 — default server.server_name — Cosmetic comment line.
- `servers[].hsts` — nginx.conf.j2:63-65 (adds Strict-Transport-Security add_header at server level) — default false — Live sets HSTS only on the Nextcloud block. Setting it drops the http-level security headers because of nginx add_header inheritance (see bugs).
- `servers[].websocket` — nginx.conf.j2:74-77 (Upgrade $http_upgrade / Connection "upgrade") — default false — Live uses a map $http_upgrade $connection_upgrade and Connection $connection_upgrade (vault, portainer, transmission) or $http_connection (Nextcloud).
- `servers[].client_max_body_size` — nginx.conf.j2:78-82 — default 0 (unlimited) when unset — Live only sets client_max_body_size 0 on the Nextcloud block; other live servers keep nginx default 1m.
- `servers[].proxy_read_timeout` — nginx.conf.j2:83-85 — default unset -> nginx default 60s — Live Nextcloud block uses 86400s (also client_body_timeout 86400s, not expressible).
- `servers[].extra_locations[].path / .config` — nginx.conf.j2:87-92 (additional location blocks, raw config text) — default [] — Only way to add Pi-hole style /admin/ and /api/ locations; cannot alter location / or the server{} level (cloud/module.yaml extra_config is not consumed).
- `hostname (declared env.required HOSTNAME, module.yaml:26)` — NOT referenced in either template (grep confirmed) — default renderer always provides hostname = variables.hostname or node.name (kiwi-render.py:213) — Declared required but unused; .env.example duplicates HOSTNAME under the module (render-out/node-cloud/.env.example:17). Live nginx consumes $HOSTNAME for the port-80 server_name and vault./portainer. subdomains.
- `tz (from env.optional TZ, module.yaml:28, lowercased by kiwi-render.py:306-308)` — NOT referenced in either template; compose template has no environment: section — default Europe/Berlin — Live knnginx also sets no TZ; dead config.

</details>

### Module `tor`

**Discrepancies vs live**

- [high] **torrc is not templated at all - module.yaml references a torrc.j2 that does not exist** — template: module.yaml line 34: `torrc: torrc.j2`. Directory /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/tor/ contains only module.yaml and docker-compose.yml.j2. Rendering examples/master.yaml prints `Warning: Template not found: .../modules/tor/torrc.j2` and produces no torrc. TODO.md line 76 confirms it was never written. — live: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-master/kmtor/etc/torrc, active lines: 20 `SOCKSPort 10.8.0.1:9050`; 31 `SOCKSPolicy accept 10.8.0.0/16`; 32 `SOCKSPolicy reject *`; 33 `EntryNodes {de},{ch},{at},{nl},{fr}`; 34 `ExitNodes {de},{ch},{at},{nl},{fr}`; 36 `ExcludeNodes {ac},{af},...` (~240 country codes, every country except the five above); 37 `StrictNodes 1`; 38 `CircuitBuildTimeout 5`; 39 `KeepalivePeriod 60`; 40 `NewCircuitPeriod 15`; 41 `NumEntryGuards 8`. Mounted via compose line 103 `$DOCKERDIR/kmtor/etc:/etc/tor`. — impact: A fresh v2 deployment mounts an empty /etc/tor, so Tor runs on whatever the dockurr/tor image generates by default: the SOCKS listener is not pinned to the WireGuard gateway address 10.8.0.1, there is no SOCKSPolicy (any container sharing or reaching the namespace can use the proxy), no de/ch/at/nl/fr pinning, and default circuit timings. The entire user-visible behaviour of the live module (WireGuard clients using socks5://10.8.0.1:9050 with circuits restricted to five countries) is lost. — fix: Write modules/tor/torrc.j2 that reproduces the live file from variables: `SOCKSPort {{ socks_bind_address | default(wg_default_dns | default('10.8.0.1')) }}:{{ socks_port | default(9050) }}` when network_mode is set, else `SOCKSPort 0.0.0.0:{{ socks_port }}` (the container has its own IP in standalone mode); `SOCKSPolicy accept {{ cidr }}` for each entry of socks_policy_accept (default ['10.8.0.0/16']) then `SOCKSPolicy reject *`; EntryNodes/ExitNodes from tor_countries (default de,ch,at,nl,fr) and ExcludeNodes computed as all ISO codes minus tor_countries (drop the duplicate `{uk}` and obsolete `{tp},{an},{fx},{su}` from the live list); `StrictNodes {{ 1 if strict_nodes else 0 }}`; CircuitBuildTimeout/KeepalivePeriod/NewCircuitPeriod/NumEntryGuards from settings with the live values as defaults. The renderer must write it to `<docker_dir>/km-tor/etc/torrc` before `docker compose up`.
- [high] **Silent fallback to standalone networking when network_mode is omitted; live is unconditionally in the VPN server's namespace** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 9-17: `{% if network_mode %} network_mode: "service:{{ network_mode }}" {% else %} networks: {{ network_name }}: ipv4_address: {{ container_ip }} / ports: - "127.0.0.1:9050:9050/tcp" {% endif %}`. The value comes only from module_config.tor.network_mode, hardcoded as "km-vpn-server" in examples/master.yaml line 53 (not derived from container_prefix). — live: docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml line 101: `network_mode: "service:kmvpn-server"`, no `networks:` and no `ports:` for kmtor. Tor is reachable only at 10.8.0.1:9050 on the wg0 interface inside kmvpn-server (torrc line 20), and kmvpn-server/start.sh lines 28-29 explicitly allow the no-c2c subnet 10.8.1.0/24 to reach 10.8.0.1 for "pihole, dns, tor". — impact: If a fleet file omits the key, Tor is rendered as a separate container at 172.64.0.8 with SOCKS published only on the host loopback: WireGuard clients can no longer reach socks5://10.8.0.1:9050, and with the live torrc's `SOCKSPort 10.8.0.1:9050` the bind fails inside that container (address does not exist there) so Tor crash-loops under restart: unless-stopped. The hardcoded km- prefix in the example also breaks if the prefix convention changes. — fix: Default it in the template: `{% set tor_netmode = network_mode | default(container_prefix ~ '-vpn-server' if node_type == 'master' and 'vpn-server' in modules else '') %}` (or have the generator compute it), and make torrc.j2 choose the bind address from the same variable so the two can never disagree. Document that standalone mode is a different topology (local-only SOCKS).
- [medium] **Host directory renamed kmtor -> km-tor; existing data and torrc are not migrated** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24-25 render `/home/user/docker/km-tor/etc:/etc/tor` and `/home/user/docker/km-tor/var:/var/lib/tor` (verified in the rendered output). — live: docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml lines 103-104: `'$DOCKERDIR/kmtor/etc:/etc/tor'` and `'$DOCKERDIR/kmtor/var:/var/lib/tor'`; the snapshot contains kmtor/etc/torrc and an (empty in the zip) kmtor/var. — impact: Porting an existing master to v2 starts Tor with an empty /etc/tor (no torrc, see above) and an empty DataDirectory, so the eight persisted entry guards (NumEntryGuards 8) and any cached consensus are discarded and re-chosen. — fix: Either keep a migration step in the installer (`mv $DOCKERDIR/kmtor $DOCKERDIR/km-tor` if the old dir exists) or expose `tor_dir` with default `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-tor` so an operator can point it at the old location. Fix module.yaml storage.bind_mounts to match (see bugs).
- [medium] **vpn-server listed as an optional dependency although the working master configuration cannot start without it** — template: module.yaml lines 10-13: `dependencies: required: [] optional: - vpn-server`. The renderer's DependencyResolver only honours `required` (kiwi-render.py lines 100-115). — live: kmtor exists only as `network_mode: "service:kmvpn-server"` (line 101); there is no standalone Tor anywhere in the live stack. — impact: A master fleet file that enables tor but not vpn-server renders cleanly, then `docker compose up` fails with an undefined-service error for km-vpn-server (or, if network_mode is also omitted, silently produces the loopback-only standalone topology). — fix: Make vpn-server `required` for the master/service-mode case (or have the generator validate that `network_mode` names a service that is actually in `modules`).
- [low] **Environment variables: template passes none (matches live), but module.yaml advertises TZ and TOR_LOCATION that nothing consumes** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 has no `environment:` block. module.yaml lines 28-30 declare env.optional `TZ: "Europe/Berlin"` and `TOR_LOCATION: "DE"`; the generated .env.example shows `# TZ=Europe/Berlin` and `# TOR_LOCATION=DE` under the tor section. — live: docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml lines 97-99: `#    environment:` / `#      - LOCATION=$TLOCATION` / `#      - TZ=$TZ` (all commented out). .env line 26 sets `TLOCATION` (value redacted) which is therefore unused. Geo pinning is done in torrc instead. — impact: Effective behaviour is identical (no env either side), but operators are shown a TOR_LOCATION knob that does nothing, under a third name (TOR_LOCATION vs live TLOCATION vs image LOCATION). If someone later wires `LOCATION=` through, it would fight the torrc's ExitNodes/StrictNodes. — fix: Remove TZ and TOR_LOCATION from env.optional (Tor does not need TZ; location belongs in torrc.j2 as `tor_countries`). If an image-level LOCATION override is ever wanted, add it conditionally and document the conflict with StrictNodes.
- [low] **Container name kmtor -> km-tor** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 6 and 8: `{{ container_prefix }}-tor` -> `km-tor`. — live: docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml lines 94-96: service and container_name `kmtor`. — impact: Intentional per docs/module-schema.md lines 86-89 (`km-{service}`). No live script references the container name (kmvpn-server/start.sh, kiwi-master.sh and cron.d/km-docker-up reference only IPs and kmvpn-client), so nothing breaks; only `docker logs kmtor` muscle memory and the host directory (above) change. — fix: None needed beyond the directory migration; keep the convention.
- [low] **No automatic image refresh schedule in v2 (image is untagged on both sides)** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 line 7: `image: dockurr/tor` (no tag), identical to live line 95. v2 ships no cron equivalent. — live: cron.d/km-docker-up line 2: weekly `docker compose ... down && ... up --detach --pull always && docker image prune -f`, and kiwi-master.sh line 9 also uses `--pull always`; so live Tor silently tracks `latest` every Sunday. — impact: Behaviour differs over time: a v2 host keeps whatever dockurr/tor digest it first pulled until someone pulls manually. Not Tor-specific, but it is how the live Tor stays current. — fix: Either add an `image_tag` setting (default `latest`) plus the weekly pull/restart job to the master preset, or pin a digest deliberately and document it.

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **module.yaml references templates that do not exist (torrc.j2, .env.example)** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/tor/module.yaml lines 32-35 (`templates: compose: docker-compose.yml.j2 / torrc: torrc.j2 / env_example: .env.example`)): Only docker-compose.yml.j2 and module.yaml exist in modules/tor/. kiwi-render.py render_module_configs (lines 406-409) prints `Warning: Template not found` for torrc.j2 and continues, so the render exits successfully with no torrc; env_example is skipped by design (line 403) so that reference is merely dead. Confirmed by actually rendering examples/master.yaml. — fix: Create torrc.j2 (contents per the first discrepancy) and either add a real .env.example or drop the key. The port should treat a missing referenced template as a hard error, not a warning.
- **storage.bind_mounts disagrees with the compose template (v1 directory name, wrong path, data dir missing)** (module.yaml lines 37-42: `bind_mounts: - host: "${DOCKERDIR}/kmtor" container: "/etc/tor" required: true` versus docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24-25): The template mounts `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-tor/etc` -> /etc/tor and `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-tor/var` -> /var/lib/tor (two mounts, km-tor name, /etc and /var suffixes). module.yaml lists one mount, named kmtor (v1), without the /etc suffix, and omits /var/lib/tor entirely. Any tooling that pre-creates directories from module.yaml would create the wrong directory and skip the DataDirectory. — fix: Replace with: `- host: "${DOCKERDIR}/km-tor/etc" container: "/etc/tor" required: true` and `- host: "${DOCKERDIR}/km-tor/var" container: "/var/lib/tor" required: true` (or express them with the same container_prefix variable the template uses).
- **Health check targets localhost:9050 but Tor only listens on 10.8.0.1:9050** (module.yaml lines 44-47: `health: type: tcp target: "localhost:9050"`): The live torrc line 20 is `SOCKSPort 10.8.0.1:9050` with no loopback listener. Inside km-vpn-server's namespace (which km-tor shares) nothing accepts on 127.0.0.1:9050, so a TCP probe to localhost:9050 fails permanently and would mark a healthy Tor as unhealthy. Additionally, Tor needs wg0 to carry 10.8.0.1 before it can bind, so an early probe during vpn-server start-up is expected to fail for a few seconds. — fix: Target the templated SOCKS bind address (`{{ socks_bind_address }}:{{ socks_port }}`, i.e. 10.8.0.1:9050 in service mode, container_ip:9050 standalone) and give it a start_period, or probe via the Tor process (`pgrep tor`).
- **internal_ports advertises 9051/tcp control port that is not configured anywhere** (module.yaml lines 16-18: `internal_ports: - "9050/tcp" - "9051/tcp"    # Control port`): The live torrc has no `ControlPort` line (only lines 20, 31-41 are active), so nothing listens on 9051. Also 9050 is not reachable on the Docker network in service mode; it is only on the WireGuard interface 10.8.0.1, which the metadata does not say. — fix: Remove 9051 (or add a `control_port` setting that emits `ControlPort` + `CookieAuthentication 1` in torrc.j2 when non-zero) and annotate 9050 as "on wg0 10.8.0.1 inside the vpn-server namespace".
- **Top-level network_mode block in module.yaml is outside the schema, ignored by the renderer, and uses a format the template would double-prefix** (module.yaml lines 49-51: `network_mode: tor: "service:km-vpn-server"`): docs/module-schema.md defines no `network_mode` key and kiwi-render.py never reads one (it reads dependencies, templates, env, storage only). The template expects the bare service name (`service:{{ network_mode }}`, docker-compose.yml.j2 line 10); anyone copying the module.yaml value into module_config gets `network_mode: "service:service:km-vpn-server"`, which Docker Compose rejects. The hardcoded `km-` prefix also bypasses the container_prefix convention. — fix: Delete the block (and the same pattern in modules/downloader/module.yaml line 70) or formalise it in the schema as a default that the renderer injects as the bare name `{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-server`.
- **docker_dir has no default and an undefined value renders a root-level bind mount instead of failing** (docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24-25 combined with kiwi-render.py SilentUndefined (lines 25-37)): If a fleet file omits variables.docker_dir, `{{ docker_dir }}` becomes an empty string and the volume line becomes `/km-tor/etc:/etc/tor`, which Docker happily creates at the filesystem root. The rendered compose otherwise parses: rendering examples/master.yaml and loading the result with PyYAML gives services [km-vpn-server, km-tor, km-vpn-client, km-pihole] with km-tor carrying `network_mode: service:km-vpn-server` and the two volumes, so the `services:` merge itself is sound. — fix: Use `{{ docker_dir | default('/home/user/docker') }}` in the template or, better, have the generator fail on undefined variables (StrictUndefined) for required keys like docker_dir.
- **module_config keys are flattened into one shared context, so tor and dns share the `network_mode` key** (kiwi-render.py build_context lines 236-241 (`for key, value in module_vars.items(): ctx[key] = value`), consumed by modules/tor/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 9 and modules/dns/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 17): Both templates read the same unprefixed `network_mode`. Per-module overrides are applied afterwards (line 300), but a value set only under module_config.dns silently becomes tor's network_mode too (and vice versa). Harmless in examples/master.yaml because both say km-vpn-server, but it is an unintended coupling. — fix: Namespace module_config per module in the generator (e.g. expose `tor.network_mode` or only merge that module's own keys when rendering its template).
- **master_only flag is not part of the schema and is never enforced** (module.yaml line 8: `master_only: true`): docs/module-schema.md has no such key and kiwi-render.py never checks it. Listing tor in a node fleet file renders a standalone kn-tor on knet-node with SOCKS on 127.0.0.1 only, and the (future) torrc would have to switch bind address accordingly. — fix: Either enforce master_only in the generator's validation, or support node mode deliberately (bind SOCKSPort to container_ip and restrict SOCKSPolicy to the node's docker subnet / VPN subnet).

**Present live, missing in v2**

- torrc.j2: the complete Tor configuration (SOCKSPort bound to the WireGuard gateway address, SOCKSPolicy, geo pinning, circuit tuning) - the live kmtor/etc/torrc is hand-maintained and v2 has nothing that produces it.
- SOCKS listener on the WireGuard interface: live `SOCKSPort 10.8.0.1:9050` (torrc line 20) makes Tor reachable to all WG clients as socks5://10.8.0.1:9050; v2 metadata never captures that the service lives on wg0 inside km-vpn-server rather than on the Docker network.
- SOCKSPolicy restriction: live `SOCKSPolicy accept 10.8.0.0/16` + `reject *` (torrc lines 31-32) limits proxy use to the main (10.8.0.0/24) and no-c2c (10.8.1.0/24) WireGuard client subnets.
- Geo pinning of the whole circuit: live `EntryNodes`/`ExitNodes {de},{ch},{at},{nl},{fr}`, `ExcludeNodes <every other country>`, `StrictNodes 1` (torrc lines 33-37). Note the ExcludeNodes list also constrains middle relays, which is a deliberate (anonymity-reducing) live choice that the template should preserve as a setting rather than drop.
- Circuit tuning: `CircuitBuildTimeout 5`, `KeepalivePeriod 60`, `NewCircuitPeriod 15`, `NumEntryGuards 8` (torrc lines 38-41).
- Host directory bootstrap: nothing in v2 creates <docker_dir>/km-tor/etc (with torrc inside) and <docker_dir>/km-tor/var before `docker compose up`; live relies on the pre-existing kmtor/etc and kmtor/var directories.
- Migration of the existing live data directory $DOCKERDIR/kmtor (torrc + Tor state/guards) to the new km-tor name.
- A health check that can succeed (probe 10.8.0.1:9050, not localhost:9050).
- Operational schedule from cron.d/km-docker-up: weekly `docker compose down && up --pull always && docker image prune -f` (line 2) and the daily 07:00 `docker restart kmvpn-client` (line 1). Tor shares kmvpn-server's namespace and kmvpn-server's default route goes via kmvpn-client (start.sh line 37), so these jobs are what keep the live Tor path current and un-stuck; v2 has no equivalent.
- User-facing documentation that clients use the proxy at socks5://10.8.0.1:9050 over WireGuard (there is no HTTP endpoint, no reverse-proxy block, and no host port).

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `network_mode` | string |  | km-vpn-server | Share network namespace with — Bare Compose service name whose network namespace Tor joins (template prefixes `service:`). Default on master is `<container_prefix>-vpn-server`, which reproduces the live stack (SOCKS reachable by WireGuard clients at 10.8.0.1:9050). Leave empty only for a standalone Tor with its own IP and SOCKS published on 127.0.0.1:9050. |
| `docker_dir` | string | yes | /home/user/docker | Docker config directory — Global node variable consumed by docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24-25; Tor's config and data live in <docker_dir>/km-tor/etc and <docker_dir>/km-tor/var. |
| `socks_bind_address` | string |  | 10.8.0.1 | SOCKS listen address — For torrc.j2 `SOCKSPort <addr>:<port>`. In service mode this must be an address that exists inside the vpn-server container: the wg0 gateway address (same as vpn-server's wg_default_dns, live 10.8.0.1). In standalone mode use 0.0.0.0 or the container IP. |
| `socks_port` | int |  | 9050 | SOCKS port — For torrc.j2 SOCKSPort and the health check; also the host-published port in standalone mode (docker-compose.yml.j2 line 16). |
| `socks_policy_accept` | list |  | ["10.8.0.0/16"] | Allowed SOCKS client networks — Each entry becomes `SOCKSPolicy accept <cidr>`, followed by `SOCKSPolicy reject *`. Live value 10.8.0.0/16 covers both the main 10.8.0.0/24 and the no-c2c 10.8.1.0/24 WireGuard subnets. |
| `tor_countries` | list |  | ["de","ch","at","nl","fr"] | Allowed relay countries — ISO 3166 codes used for EntryNodes and ExitNodes; ExcludeNodes is generated as every other country so middle relays are restricted too, as in the live torrc (lines 33-36). Replaces the dead TOR_LOCATION/TLOCATION/LOCATION env knob. |
| `strict_nodes` | bool |  | true | Strict node selection — Emits `StrictNodes 1` (live) so Tor fails rather than falling back to excluded countries. |
| `circuit_build_timeout` | int |  | 5 | Circuit build timeout (s) — torrc `CircuitBuildTimeout`; live 5. |
| `keepalive_period` | int |  | 60 | Keepalive period (s) — torrc `KeepalivePeriod`; live 60. |
| `new_circuit_period` | int |  | 15 | New circuit period (s) — torrc `NewCircuitPeriod`; live 15. |
| `num_entry_guards` | int |  | 8 | Number of entry guards — torrc `NumEntryGuards`; live 8. Guard state persists in <docker_dir>/km-tor/var, so changing the directory resets guards. |

**Host directories**: <docker_dir>/km-tor/etc  (bind-mounted to /etc/tor; must already contain the rendered torrc before the first `docker compose up`; live equivalent $DOCKERDIR/kmtor/etc); <docker_dir>/km-tor/var  (bind-mounted to /var/lib/tor, Tor's DataDirectory holding guard state; must be writable by the uid the dockurr/tor image runs Tor as - Tor refuses a DataDirectory it cannot own/0700; live equivalent $DOCKERDIR/kmtor/var)

<details><summary>Template variables (8)</summary>

- `container_prefix` — /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/tor/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 6 (service key `{{ container_prefix }}-tor`), line 8 (container_name), lines 24-25 (volume host paths `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-tor/etc|var`) — required — default (renderer) km on master, kn on node — Provided by kiwi-render.py build_context line 214. Renders `km-tor` on master, which replaces the live name `kmtor` (docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml lines 94-96).
- `network_mode` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 9 `{% if network_mode %}` and line 10 `network_mode: "service:{{ network_mode }}"` — default none in template; examples/master.yaml line 53 sets module_config.tor.network_mode: "km-vpn-server" — Value must be the BARE service name; the template prepends `service:`. When unset the template falls into the standalone branch (own IP on knet-master + `127.0.0.1:9050:9050/tcp`), which is NOT what the live stack does (live is unconditionally `network_mode: "service:kmvpn-server"`, line 101). Also: kiwi-render.py lines 237-241 flatten every module's module_config into one shared context, so a `network_mode` set under module_config.dns leaks into tor's render and vice versa.
- `network_name` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 13 (standalone branch only: `networks: {{ network_name }}:`) — default (renderer) knet-master / knet-node — Only consumed when network_mode is unset. Provided by kiwi-render.py line 215.
- `container_ip` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 14 (standalone branch only: `ipv4_address: {{ container_ip }}`) — default (renderer) {docker_subnet_base}.8 -> 172.64.0.8 on master (kiwi-render.py line 262) — Reserved even in service mode where it is unused. In the live stack kmtor has no IP of its own; it lives in kmvpn-server's namespace (172.64.0.3 / wg0 10.8.0.1).
- `docker_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 24-25 (`{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-tor/etc:/etc/tor` and `.../var:/var/lib/tor`) — required — default none in template (examples/master.yaml line 18 sets variables.docker_dir: /home/user/docker; live .env key DOCKERDIR) — No `| default(...)`. The renderer uses SilentUndefined (kiwi-render.py lines 25-37), so a fleet file that omits docker_dir silently renders `/km-tor/etc:/etc/tor` (a path at filesystem root) instead of failing.
- `tz` — NOT consumed by any tor template. Declared only in module.yaml line 29 (env.optional TZ: "Europe/Berlin"); the renderer lowercases it into the context (kiwi-render.py lines 303-308) and the generated .env.example emits `# TZ=Europe/Berlin` under `# === Module: tor ===` — default Europe/Berlin — Live compose has `TZ=$TZ` commented out for kmtor (line 99), so not passing it matches live. Dead declaration.
- `tor_location` — NOT consumed by any tor template. Declared only in module.yaml line 30 (env.optional TOR_LOCATION: "DE"); appears in generated .env.example as `# TOR_LOCATION=DE` — default DE — Three different names exist for the same unused knob: module.yaml TOR_LOCATION, live .env TLOCATION (line 26, set to a value but unused), and the image's env LOCATION (live compose line 98, commented out). Live geo-pinning is done in torrc (EntryNodes/ExitNodes/ExcludeNodes/StrictNodes), not via this env var. Upstream semantics of dockurr/tor LOCATION could not be verified from this sandbox (GitHub unreachable).
- `${DOCKERDIR}` — module.yaml line 40 storage.bind_mounts[0].host: "${DOCKERDIR}/kmtor" (v1 shell-style variable, not Jinja) — default none — The renderer never reads storage.bind_mounts (it only collects storage.volumes, kiwi-render.py lines 314-318), so this is informational metadata only, and it disagrees with the template (see bugs).

</details>

### Module `vault`

**Discrepancies vs live**

- [high] **No nginx server block or upstream is generated for vault** — template: module.yaml lines 31-34 declare `templates.nginx: nginx.server.conf.j2` (file does not exist) and lines 44-50 an `nginx:` section (upstream vaultwarden-default -> knvault:80, server_name vault.${HOSTNAME}, websocket: true). reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2 lines 41-45 and 54-94 loop over `upstreams` / `servers`. — live: knnginx/nginx.conf.example lines 39-43 define `upstream vaultwarden-default { zone vaultwarden-default 64k; server knvault:80; keepalive 2; }` and lines 139-159 a `server { listen 443 ssl; server_name vault.$HOSTNAME; ... proxy_pass http://vaultwarden-default; }` with Upgrade/Connection headers; kiwi-node.sh line 20 envsubst's $HOSTNAME into it. — impact: kiwi-render.py never reads any module's `nginx:` section and never sets `upstreams`/`servers`; the rendered reverse-proxy/nginx.conf for examples/node-cloud.yaml is 48 lines containing only the port-80 redirect (verified). The renderer also prints 'Warning: Template not found: modules/vault/nginx.server.conf.j2'. Vaultwarden is unreachable through the proxy. — fix: Implement the aggregator: in render_module_configs collect each enabled module's `nginx:` block into `upstreams` (name, server) and `servers` (server_name, proxy_pass, websocket, extra config), expanding `${HOSTNAME}` from `hostname` and emitting the upstream server as `{{ container_prefix }}-vault:80`; or ship vault/nginx.server.conf.j2 and have the reverse-proxy mount `conf.d/`. Either way delete the dangling `templates.nginx`/`templates.env_example` entries or create the files.
- [high] **VPN-side DNAT points at a truncated address instead of the nginx container** — template: vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2 lines 21-22: `--to-destination {{ docker_subnet_base }}:443` / `:80`, rendering `172.129.0:443` (verified in rendered output line 20). vault/module.yaml line 14 `needs_vpn_dnat: true` is never consumed by the renderer; DNAT is only emitted when module_config.vpn-client.enable_vpn_dnat is true. cloud/module.yaml lines 27-28 carry the same `${DOCKER_SUBNET2}:443` pattern. — live: knvpn-client/post-rules.txt lines 21-22 DNAT ${VPN_IP}:443/80 to `172.129.0.3:443` / `:80` - the knnginx container IP. (The live .example lines 18-19 also used `${DOCKER_SUBNET2}:443`, i.e. 172.129.0.0:443, and had to be hand-corrected on the node.) — impact: inet_pton rejects `172.129.0` (iptables: 'Bad IP address'); lenient inet_aton parsing yields 172.129.0.0, the network address. Either way https://vault.<hostname> is unreachable from other VPN peers, which is the primary access path for this node. — fix: Render `--to-destination {{ container_ips['reverse-proxy'] }}:443` (and :80), and have the renderer set `enable_vpn_dnat` automatically when any enabled module declares `needs_vpn_dnat: true`. Align docs/module-schema.md (`DOCKER_SUBNET2` = 172.129.0.0) with the renderer's `docker_subnet_base` (172.129.0).
- [high] **Upstream container name uses v1 spelling** — template: module.yaml line 46 `server: "knvault:80"`; the compose template renders the service as `kn-vault` (lines 6, 8). — live: Live container is `knvault` (live compose line 267) and nginx.conf upstream says `server knvault:80` (line 41). — impact: Once the nginx aggregator exists, nginx would fail config load with 'host not found in upstream knvault'. — fix: Use `{{ container_prefix }}-vault:80` (or a renderer-expanded `${CONTAINER_PREFIX}-vault:80`) in module.yaml, consistent with docs/module-schema.md lines 86-89.
- [medium] **Reverse proxy no longer depends_on vault** — template: reverse-proxy/docker-compose.yml.j2 has no `depends_on` (lines 5-29). — live: Live `knnginx` has `depends_on: [knvpn-client, nextcloud-aio-mastercontainer, knvault]` (live compose lines 46-49). — impact: With a static `upstream { server kn-vault:80; }`, nginx refuses to start until the name resolves; on `compose up` start order is unordered, so kn-nginx can crash-loop until kn-vault is running (restart policy eventually recovers, but the 80->443 redirect and all other vhosts are down meanwhile). — fix: Have the reverse-proxy template emit `depends_on:` for every module that contributes an nginx block (renderer can pass `proxied_services`), or use `resolver 127.0.0.11 valid=30s;` with a variable proxy_pass so startup does not require resolution.
- [medium] **Open signups default flipped** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 line 11 and module.yaml line 29 default `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED=false`. — live: Live knvault runs `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED: "true"` (live compose line 271). — impact: Behaviour differs: on a fresh v2 node nobody can create the first account unless admin_token is set and the admin invites users, or signups are temporarily enabled. On the live node anyone reaching vault.<hostname> can register. — fix: Keep `false` as the hardened default but expose it as a bool setting, and document the bootstrap path (enable for first user then disable, or set admin_token and use /admin invitations).
- [medium] **Data directory path differs three ways** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 line 28 mounts `{{ docker_dir }}/kn-vault/data:/data`; module.yaml line 36 declares bind mount `${DOCKERDIR}/knvault/data`. — live: Live mounts `$DOCKERDIR/knvault/:/data/` (live compose line 273) - the knvault directory itself holds db.sqlite3, rsa keys, attachments. — impact: A node migrated from live to v2 would start with an empty vault unless data is moved; module.yaml's own bind_mount declaration does not match its template. — fix: Standardise on `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-vault/data` in both files and document the one-time migration `mv $DOCKERDIR/knvault $DOCKERDIR/kn-vault/data` (preserving ownership).
- [low] **Static IP moved from .5 to .7 (and nginx from .3 to .5)** — template: kiwi-render.py ip_map line 261 assigns vault `{base}.7`; reverse-proxy gets `.5`. — live: knvault is `172.129.0.5` (live compose line 278); knnginx is `.3`, AIO is `.4`. — impact: Nothing in vault's path references the IP by number, but anything hand-written against the live plan (firewall rules, Nextcloud trusted_proxies - examples/node-cloud.yaml line 48 still says 172.129.0.3) silently points at the wrong container. — fix: Keep the renderer's ip_map authoritative, document the v1->v2 IP plan, and derive trusted_proxies / DNAT targets from `container_ips[...]` rather than literals.
- [low] **Image tag differs** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2 line 7: `vaultwarden/server:latest`. — live: Live: `vaultwarden/server:latest-alpine` (live compose line 266). — impact: Functionally equivalent; alpine image is smaller. Weekly `--pull always` on the live node tracks a different tag. — fix: `image: vaultwarden/server:{{ vault_image_tag | default('latest-alpine') }}`.
- [low] **Extra environment added versus live** — template: Template sets INVITATIONS_ALLOWED, SHOW_PASSWORD_HINT, LOG_LEVEL=warn, TZ and optional ADMIN_TOKEN (lines 12-18). — live: Live knvault sets only DOMAIN and SIGNUPS_ALLOWED (live compose lines 269-271); Vaultwarden defaults are INVITATIONS_ALLOWED=true, SHOW_PASSWORD_HINT=false, LOG_LEVEL=info, no TZ. — impact: Only LOG_LEVEL actually changes behaviour (less logging than live). Others match upstream defaults. — fix: Keep; optionally default log_level to 'info' to mirror live.
- [low] **WebSocket Connection header and listen syntax differ from live nginx** — template: reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2 line 76 sets `proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade"` unconditionally when websocket is true and has no `map $http_upgrade` block; line 57 uses `listen 443 ssl http2`. — live: nginx.conf.example lines 34-37 define `map $http_upgrade $connection_upgrade` and the vault block uses `Connection $connection_upgrade` (line 150); vault block uses plain `listen 443 ssl` (line 141). — impact: Every proxied request carries `Connection: upgrade`; Vaultwarden tolerates it but it is incorrect for non-WS requests. The `http2` listen parameter is deprecated since nginx 1.25.1 (warning only). — fix: Add the map to nginx.conf.j2 and emit `Connection $connection_upgrade`; use `listen 443 ssl;` plus `http2 on;`.
- [low] **Docker network loses its fixed name and bridge name** — template: templates/networks.yml.j2 lines 17-26 define knet-node without `name:` or `com.docker.network.bridge.name`. — live: Live network has `name: knet-node` and `com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node` (live compose lines 309-314). — impact: Compose would create `<project>_knet-node` with a random br-xxxx host bridge; host-side scripts/firewall rules that reference the bridge name break. Shared concern, not vault-specific. — fix: Add `name: {{ network_name }}` and `com.docker.network.bridge.name: {{ network_name }}` to networks.yml.j2.

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **Referenced template files do not exist** (modules/vault/module.yaml lines 31-34 (`templates.nginx: nginx.server.conf.j2`, `templates.env_example: .env.example`); directory only contains docker-compose.yml.j2 and module.yaml): render_module_configs() prints 'Warning: Template not found: .../modules/vault/nginx.server.conf.j2' (reproduced); env_example is skipped silently. TODO.md line 74 acknowledges the nginx block is missing. — fix: Create vault/nginx.server.conf.j2 (upstream + server block as in nginx_blocks) and an .env.example, or remove the entries and rely on the aggregator.
- **Wrong container name in nginx upstream** (modules/vault/module.yaml line 46: `server: "knvault:80"`): Prefix convention (docs/module-schema.md lines 86-89, docker-compose.yml.j2 line 8) renders `kn-vault`; `knvault` would never resolve inside knet-node. — fix: `server: "${CONTAINER_PREFIX}-vault:80"` with renderer expansion, or `kn-vault:80` if master is never a vault host.
- **env.required name does not match the variable the template consumes** (modules/vault/module.yaml line 27 (`DOMAIN`) vs docker-compose.yml.j2 line 10 (`{{ vault_domain }}`)): kiwi-render.py validate lowercases DOMAIN to `domain` and warns 'Module vault requires DOMAIN' even for the shipped example that sets vault_domain (reproduced). The generated .env.example emits `DOMAIN=https://vault.office.kiwi  # REQUIRED` (only found via the `vault_` prefix heuristic at kiwi-render.py line 602) although compose already has the value baked in. — fix: Rename env.required to `VAULT_DOMAIN` (lowercases to vault_domain), or make the template read `{{ domain }}`.
- **YAML booleans render as Python `True`/`False`** (modules/vault/docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 11-13): Rendering with `signups_allowed: false` (unquoted) yields `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED=False` (reproduced). Vaultwarden parses booleans with Rust's case-sensitive `str::parse::<bool>` and fails to start on `False`. The examples only work because every value is quoted. — fix: `{{ (signups_allowed | default('false')) | string | lower }}` for all three bool vars, or coerce bools in build_context.
- **ADMIN_TOKEN is not escaped for compose interpolation** (modules/vault/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 17: `- ADMIN_TOKEN={{ admin_token }}`): Vaultwarden recommends an argon2 PHC hash (`$argon2id$v=19$...`) for ADMIN_TOKEN. Rendered verbatim (reproduced), docker compose treats each `$name` as a variable reference, warns, and substitutes empty strings, so the admin page token is silently corrupted. The example's suggested `"${VAULTWARDEN_ADMIN_TOKEN}"` placeholder is written literally into compose but the renderer never adds that key to .env.example. — fix: `{{ admin_token | replace('$', '$$') }}`, or write secrets to a generated env_file / docker secret instead of the compose environment list; declare admin_token as an optional secret in module.yaml so it reaches .env.example.
- **docker_dir has no default - volume lands at filesystem root** (modules/vault/docker-compose.yml.j2 line 28): With `variables.docker_dir` absent the SilentUndefined renders `- /kn-vault/data:/data` (reproduced). Docker would create /kn-vault/data as root on the host. — fix: `{{ docker_dir | default('/home/user/docker') }}` or make the renderer fail validation when docker_dir is missing.
- **Merged docker-compose.yml for the cloud node does not parse** (kiwi-render.py _build_compose_file lines 348-374 combined with modules/cloud/docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 13-15 (`volumes:` top-level key)): The renderer re-indents every non-comment line of each module template by two spaces, so cloud's top-level `volumes:` becomes `  volumes:` inside `services:` (rendered line 94) and yaml.safe_load fails with 'expected <block end>, but found <block mapping start>' (reproduced). Vault's own block is valid in isolation (verified), but every node that runs vault alongside cloud gets an unusable compose file. — fix: Strip top-level `volumes:`/`networks:` sections from module templates (the renderer already collects `storage.volumes`), or parse each rendered module as YAML and merge dicts instead of line-splicing.
- **needs_vpn_dnat and firewall.rules are declared but never consumed** (modules/vault/module.yaml lines 14, 16-17; kiwi-render.py has no reference to `needs_vpn_dnat`, `firewall`, `storage.bind_mounts`, `health` or `nginx`): DNAT only appears when module_config.vpn-client.enable_vpn_dnat is set by hand (examples/node-cloud.yaml line 33), and even then targets `172.129.0:443` (vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2 line 21). — fix: Renderer: set enable_vpn_dnat when any module has needs_vpn_dnat; DNAT to `container_ips['reverse-proxy']`.
- **Health check target is the web root instead of Vaultwarden's liveness endpoint** (modules/vault/module.yaml lines 38-41: `target: "http://localhost:80/"`): Vaultwarden exposes `/alive` for health probes; `/` returns the web vault HTML and is heavier. Section is unused by the renderer anyway and neither live nor v2 compose defines a healthcheck. — fix: `target: "http://localhost:80/alive"` and emit a compose `healthcheck` from it.

**Present live, missing in v2**

- Generated nginx upstream `vaultwarden` + `server_name vault.<hostname>` block (live nginx.conf.example lines 39-43, 139-159); the v2 aggregator path (`upstreams`/`servers` in reverse-proxy/nginx.conf.j2) is never populated by kiwi-render.py.
- `depends_on` from the reverse proxy on vault (live compose lines 46-49).
- `.env`/`.env.example` for the module: live uses DOMAINVAULT (.env line 26); v2 declares env_example but ships no file and never lists admin_token.
- Portainer on the same node: live `knport` (portainer/portainer-ce:latest, 127.0.0.1:9443:9443, docker.sock, 172.129.0.250, live compose lines 285-301) plus nginx `upstream portainer-default { server knport:9000; }` and `server_name portainer.$HOSTNAME` (nginx.conf.example lines 44-48, 161-181). No v2 module.
- Node lifecycle glue from kiwi-node.sh: envsubst bootstrap of nginx.conf and post-rules.txt from .example files (lines 19-25), cron daily 07:01 restart and weekly Sunday `down && up --pull always && image/volume prune` (lines 30-35). v2 has no install/deploy script.
- Network definition details: `name: knet-node` and `com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node` (live compose lines 309-314) absent from templates/networks.yml.j2.
- Live-only nginx `stream {}` TCP forward on port 2222 to 192.168.2.5:2222 (live nginx.conf lines 192-194) with matching DNAT (live post-rules.txt line 23) - node-level, not vault, but part of the live stack.
- Correct VPN DNAT target (live post-rules.txt lines 21-22 DNAT to the nginx container IP); v2 renders a truncated `172.129.0:443`.

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vault_domain` | string | yes | https://vault.<hostname> | Vaultwarden public URL — Written to DOMAIN. Must be the exact https URL users open (scheme + host, no trailing slash) and must match the nginx server_name `vault.<hostname>`; used for WebAuthn/TOTP, attachment and invitation links. Live node sets it via DOMAINVAULT. |
| `signups_allowed` | bool |  | false | Allow open registration — SIGNUPS_ALLOWED. The live node runs with true. With false, the first account must be invited from /admin (requires admin_token) or you temporarily enable this for bootstrap. Rendered lowercase. |
| `invitations_allowed` | bool |  | true | Allow invitations — INVITATIONS_ALLOWED - lets existing organisation owners invite users even when signups are closed. |
| `show_password_hint` | bool |  | false | Show password hints — SHOW_PASSWORD_HINT - displays hints on the login page without email verification; keep false on a shared network. |
| `log_level` | enum |  | warn | Log level — LOG_LEVEL - one of trace, debug, info, warn, error, off. Vaultwarden's own default (and the live node) is info. |
| `admin_token` | secret |  |  | Admin panel token — ADMIN_TOKEN - enables https://vault.<hostname>/admin. Leave empty to disable the admin page. Prefer an argon2 PHC hash (`vaultwarden hash`); the generator must escape `$` as `$$` in docker-compose or write it to an env_file. |
| `vault_image_tag` | string |  | latest-alpine | Image tag — Tag of vaultwarden/server. The live node tracks latest-alpine; the v2 template hard-codes latest. |
| `timezone` | string |  | Europe/Berlin | Timezone — Global node variable passed as TZ. Live knvault sets none. |
| `docker_dir` | string | yes | /home/user/docker | Docker config directory — Global node variable. Vault data (db.sqlite3, rsa_key*, attachments, sends) lives in <docker_dir>/kn-vault/data; migrate the live <docker_dir>/knvault/ contents there. |

**Reverse-proxy blocks**: vault.<hostname> → http://kn-vault:80 (upstream `vaultwarden` -> <container_prefix>-vault:80; live name vaultwarden-default -> knvault:80) (websocket)

**Host ports**: none for kn-vault itself - it only listens on 80/tcp inside knet-node (module.yaml internal_ports; the live `8081:80` publish is commented out, live compose lines 274-275); 443/tcp and 80/tcp on the host LAN interface - published by the reverse-proxy module (kn-nginx `ports: 80:80, 443:443`), which is the only path to vault; 443/tcp and 80/tcp on ${VPN_IP} (10.8.0.x) via the vpn-client DNAT to the kn-nginx container IP (live post-rules.txt lines 21-22) - must target the nginx IP, not vault and not the truncated subnet base

**Host directories**: <docker_dir>/kn-vault/data  (e.g. /home/user/docker/kn-vault/data) - bind-mounted to /data; holds db.sqlite3, rsa_key.*, attachments/, sends/, icon_cache/. Live equivalent is <docker_dir>/knvault/ (live compose line 273); copy or move its contents here when migrating. Docker will auto-create it root-owned if missing, which Vaultwarden (runs as root by default) tolerates.; Dependency directories (not vault's own, but required before `compose up` on a vault node): <docker_dir>/kn-nginx/ with nginx.conf, fullchain.pem, privkey.pem (reverse-proxy, read-only mounts) and <docker_dir>/kn-vpn-client/ with post-rules.txt and the WireGuard config named by wireguard_config_file (vpn-client).

<details><summary>Template variables (11)</summary>

- `container_prefix` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 6 (service key), line 8 (container_name), line 28 (host bind path `{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-vault/data`) — required — default renderer: 'kn' for node, 'km' for master (kiwi-render.py line 214) — Renders service/container `kn-vault`. module.yaml nginx.upstream.server still says `knvault:80` (line 46) and storage.bind_mounts says `${DOCKERDIR}/knvault/data` (line 36) - both contradict the template.
- `vault_domain` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 10: `- DOMAIN={{ vault_domain }}` — required — default none (SilentUndefined renders `DOMAIN=` empty) — Comes from module_config.vault.vault_domain (examples/node-cloud.yaml line 52). module.yaml env.required lists `DOMAIN` (line 27), which the renderer lowercases to `domain`, NOT `vault_domain`; `kiwi-render.py validate` therefore warns 'Module vault requires DOMAIN' even when vault_domain is set (confirmed). Live equivalent: `.env` key DOMAINVAULT -> compose `DOMAIN: $DOMAINVAULT` (live compose line 270). Must equal `https://vault.<hostname>` to match the nginx server_name.
- `signups_allowed` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 11: `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED={{ signups_allowed | default('false') }}` — default 'false' (template filter and module.yaml env.optional SIGNUPS_ALLOWED, line 29) — Live runs `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED: "true"` (live compose line 271). A YAML boolean `false` renders as Python `False` (verified) which Vaultwarden's case-sensitive bool parser rejects - value must be quoted or the template must apply `| string | lower`.
- `invitations_allowed` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 12 — default 'true' (template and module.yaml line 30) — Not set live; matches Vaultwarden's built-in default. Same boolean-capitalisation hazard as signups_allowed.
- `show_password_hint` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 13 — default 'false' (template and module.yaml line 31) — Not set live; matches Vaultwarden's built-in default. Same boolean-capitalisation hazard.
- `log_level` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 14 — default 'warn' (template and module.yaml line 32) — Not set live (Vaultwarden default is 'info'), so v2 logs less than the live node. Valid values: trace, debug, info, warn, error, off.
- `timezone` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 15: `TZ={{ timezone | default('Europe/Berlin') }}` — default 'Europe/Berlin' — Global node variable (`variables.timezone`). Live knvault sets no TZ at all (only knvpn-client does).
- `admin_token` — docker-compose.yml.j2 lines 16-18: `{% if admin_token %} - ADMIN_TOKEN={{ admin_token }} {% endif %}` — default none (block omitted when unset; SilentUndefined is falsy) — Not declared anywhere in module.yaml env (neither required nor optional), so the generated .env.example never mentions it. Example config suggests `admin_token: "${VAULTWARDEN_ADMIN_TOKEN}"` (node-cloud.yaml line 57) which would be written literally into compose and then compose-interpolated from an .env key that v2 never emits. Vaultwarden recommends an argon2 PHC hash here; PHC strings contain `$` which docker compose interpolates unless escaped as `$$` (template does not escape - verified).
- `network_name` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 20 (networks key) — required — default renderer: 'knet-node' / 'knet-master' (kiwi-render.py line 215) — Matches live network name `knet-node` (live compose line 309), but v2 templates/networks.yml.j2 omits `name: knet-node` and `com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node` that the live file sets (lines 310-314).
- `container_ip` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 21: `ipv4_address: {{ container_ip }}` — required — default renderer ip_map vault -> `{docker_subnet_base}.7` (kiwi-render.py line 261); overridable via module_config.vault.container_ip — Live knvault is 172.129.0.5 (live compose line 278); in v2 .5 belongs to reverse-proxy.
- `docker_dir` — docker-compose.yml.j2 line 28: `- {{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-vault/data:/data` — required — default none - when unset the volume renders as `/kn-vault/data:/data` at filesystem root (verified) — Global node variable (`variables.docker_dir`). Live mounts `$DOCKERDIR/knvault/:/data/` (live compose line 273), i.e. one directory level up and a different name.

</details>

### Module `vpn-client`

**Discrepancies vs live**

- [medium] **WIREGUARD_MTU never emitted** — template: No WIREGUARD_MTU line in docker-compose.yml.j2; module.yaml env.optional WIREGUARD_MTU: "1412" is injected into the context as wireguard_mtu but unused. — live: kiwi-node-gw/docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:16 and kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:15: '- WIREGUARD_MTU=$WIREGUARD_MTU' with WIREGUARD_MTU="1412" in both .env files. — impact: Gluetun uses its built-in MTU default instead of the 1412 the live mesh is tuned for (bridge MTU 1412, wg-easy WG_MTU=1412); the live post-rules comment notes TLS breakage on some sites when the MTU is wrong. — fix: Add '- WIREGUARD_MTU={{ wireguard_mtu | default('1412') }}' to the environment list.
- [medium] **FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS missing (cloud node)** — template: No FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS / FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS variable or line. — live: kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:16: '- FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS="80,443"' (node-gw has it commented out, line 17-19). — impact: Inbound 80/443 arriving on tun0 from the VPN mesh is not whitelisted in gluetun's own firewall; the cloud node relies on it together with the DNAT rules to serve nginx over the VPN IP. — fix: Add an optional 'firewall_vpn_input_ports' (and 'firewall_input_ports') setting: '{% if firewall_vpn_input_ports %}- FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS={{ firewall_vpn_input_ports }}{% endif %}'. Emit it unquoted: in compose list syntax the live double quotes become part of the value.
- [medium] **No way to publish host ports on the vpn-client (gw node needs 3129)** — template: Service has no 'ports:' key and no variable for it. — live: kiwi-node-gw/docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:25-26: 'ports: - 3129:3129' on knvpn-client (JDownloader direct-connect port; jdownloader/transmission share this netns via network_mode: service:knvpn-client). — impact: Containers that use network_mode: service:kn-vpn-client cannot declare ports themselves (Docker rejects it), so any host-published port for the downloader stack must live on the vpn-client service; the gw stack loses 3129. — fix: Add '{% if published_ports %}ports:{% for p in published_ports %} - "{{ p }}"{% endfor %}{% endif %}' with a list setting (gw example: ["3129:3129"]).
- [medium] **sysctls missing (gw node)** — template: No sysctls block. — live: kiwi-node-gw/docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:36-38: 'sysctls: - net.ipv4.tcp_mtu_probing=1 - net.ipv4.tcp_base_mss=1024'. — impact: PMTU-blackhole mitigation that the gateway node relies on (LAN clients routed through this container) is lost. — fix: Add an optional 'sysctls' list setting rendered as a sysctls: block; default it on for nodes that run the gateway module.
- [low] **/etc/hosts bind mount missing (cloud node)** — template: Volumes: only post-rules.txt and the wg conf. — live: kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:38: '- /etc/hosts:/etc/hosts:ro'. — impact: Hostname overrides from the host's /etc/hosts are not visible inside gluetun (and therefore to any container sharing its netns). — fix: Add an 'extra_volumes' list setting (cloud example: ["/etc/hosts:/etc/hosts:ro"]).
- [medium] **Master post-rules diverge: TCPMSS clamp dropped, NEW added, DNAT-masquerade added** — template: post-rules.txt.j2:11 '-A FORWARD -m conntrack --ctstate ESTABLISHED,RELATED,NEW -j ACCEPT', no mangle/TCPMSS rule, line 31 '-t nat -A POSTROUTING -m conntrack --ctstate DNAT -j MASQUERADE' always emitted. — live: kiwi-master/kmvpn-client/post-rules.txt:1-6: 'iptables -t mangle -A FORWARD -p tcp -m tcp --tcp-flags SYN,RST SYN -j TCPMSS --clamp-mss-to-pmtu', then '--ctstate ESTABLISHED,RELATED -j ACCEPT' (no NEW), '-i eth0 -o eth0 -j REJECT', '-s 172.64.0.0/24 -o tun0 -j ACCEPT', MASQUERADE -o tun0, INPUT tun0 accept. No DNAT masquerade. — impact: On the master the MSS clamp (which protects all mesh traffic exiting via Mullvad) disappears, and accepting NEW before the eth0->eth0 REJECT makes that REJECT unreachable, re-enabling container-to-container forwarding through gluetun that the live master blocks. The DNAT masquerade is harmless there. — fix: Add 'tcp_mss_clamp' bool (default true on master, false on nodes) emitting the mangle rule first; emit NEW only when the node needs DNAT/forwarding (or move the eth0->eth0 REJECT before the ESTABLISHED,RELATED,NEW accept); wrap the ctstate-DNAT masquerade in the same condition as the DNAT rules.
- [medium] **Host directory renamed knvpn-client -> kn-vpn-client (and kmvpn-client -> km-vpn-client)** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:37,39 mount from '{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client/'; module.yaml storage.bind_mounts still say '${DOCKERDIR}/knvpn-client/${WG_CONFIG}'. — live: '$DOCKERDIR/knvpn-client/$WG_CONFIG' and '$DOCKERDIR/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt' (both node composes), '$DOCKERDIR/kmvpn-client/post-rules.txt' (master). kiwi-node.sh/kiwi-node-gw.sh envsubst into ${DOCKERDIR}/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt; kiwi-server roles/node-cloud/apply.sh:30,89,103 also create/use knvpn-client/. — impact: Existing hosts (and the current kiwi-server node-cloud role) have files under knvpn-client/; a v2 render points at a non-existent kn-vpn-client/ directory, and Docker then creates empty directories at the file mount paths so gluetun fails to start. — fix: Keep the new convention but make the generator create <docker_dir>/<prefix>-vpn-client/ and migrate/symlink from the old name; update module.yaml bind_mounts to the same expression as the template.
- [medium] **VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT emitted unconditionally on nodes** — template: docker-compose.yml.j2:22 '- VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT={{ vpn_endpoint_port | default('51820') }}' always. — live: Only the master sets VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT=$VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT (kiwi-master/docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml:16); nodes (custom provider) take the Endpoint from the mounted wg0.conf. — impact: Gluetun gives env precedence over the conf file, so if the master's WireGuard port is ever not 51820 (vpn-server wg_port setting) every node silently connects to the wrong port. — fix: Emit VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT (and SERVER_*) only when vpn_provider != 'custom', or only when vpn_endpoint_port is explicitly set.
- [low] **Empty WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY= / WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES= on nodes; BLOCK_MALICIOUS/UPDATER_PERIOD added to nodes** — template: Lines 14-15, 24, 26 emitted for every node type. — live: Nodes set neither WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY/ADDRESSES nor BLOCK_MALICIOUS=off / UPDATER_PERIOD=24h (kiwi-node-gw/docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:10-16, kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:9-16); only the master does. — impact: Mostly cosmetic: gluetun treats empty values as unset and BLOCK_MALICIOUS=off is the default; UPDATER_PERIOD=24h triggers pointless server-list updates for provider=custom. — fix: Wrap lines 14-15, 22, 24, 26 in '{% if vpn_provider != 'custom' %}'.
- [high] **Shared network lacks fixed name and bridge name** — template: templates/networks.yml.j2:17-26 defines knet-node without 'name: knet-node' and without 'com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node'. — live: kiwi-node-gw/docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:191-197 and kiwi-cloud/docker-compose-kiwi-node-cloud.yml:309-314: 'name: knet-node' plus driver_opts 'com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node'. — impact: The host bridge gets a random br-xxxx name and compose prefixes the network with the project name; the gateway host rules ('-o knet-node', '-i knet-node' in kiwi-node-gw.sh:51-57 and kn-service/gw.sh:28-40) then match nothing, so LAN clients lose VPN routing via the vpn-client, and nginx/Portainer DNS names tied to knet-node break. — fix: Add 'name: {{ network_name }}' and 'com.docker.network.bridge.name: {{ network_name }}' to both branches of networks.yml.j2.
- [low] **Bridge MTU forced to 1412 on cloud node** — template: networks.yml.j2 always sets com.docker.network.driver.mtu: {{ mtu | default('1412') }}. — live: kiwi-cloud compose has the mtu driver_opt commented out (line 315) -> 1500; gw and master use 1412. — impact: Minor throughput difference on the cloud node; not a breakage. — fix: Expose network.mtu per node (already supported) and set 1500 in the cloud example if the live value is intentional.
- [low] **Health check declares a legacy control-server path and has no live counterpart** — template: module.yaml health: http http://localhost:8000/v1/openvpn/status every 30s (renderer ignores it). — live: No healthcheck in any live compose; the gluetun image ships its own HEALTHCHECK. — impact: If a generator starts honouring health:, it would need a client inside the container (gluetun has no curl) and /v1/openvpn/status is the deprecated name for /v1/vpn/status. — fix: Either drop health: or declare type: command with the image's built-in healthcheck.
- [low] **Image tag** — template: image: qmcgaw/gluetun (untagged). — live: Same, untagged, but live cron re-pulls weekly (kiwi-master/cron.d/km-docker-up:2, kiwi-node.sh:33, kiwi-node-gw.sh:29). — impact: Identical today; v2 has no update cron so nodes would never pick up new images. — fix: Pin 'qmcgaw/gluetun:v3' and port the weekly 'compose up --pull always' cron.

**Bugs in the template / module.yaml**

- **DNAT to an incomplete address '172.129.0:443'** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2:21-22): '--to-destination {{ docker_subnet_base }}:443' renders (verified with examples/node-cloud.yaml) as 'iptables -t nat -A PREROUTING -d 10.8.0.25 -p tcp --dport 443 -j DNAT --to-destination 172.129.0:443'. docker_subnet_base is a 3-octet prefix (node-config-schema.md:37), so iptables rejects the rule and VPN-side access to nginx never works. The live example it was copied from targets ${DOCKER_SUBNET2}:443 = 172.129.0.0 (the network address, also wrong); the deployed kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:21-23 was hand-fixed to 172.129.0.3 (knnginx). — fix: Target the reverse-proxy container: '--to-destination {{ container_ips['reverse-proxy'] }}:443' (renderer ip_map gives <base>.5; note live cloud nginx is .3 and examples/node-cloud.yaml trusted_proxies says 172.129.0.3, so align the ip_map or the example).
- **module.yaml bind_mounts contradict the template (old dir name, undefined ${WG_CONFIG})** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/module.yaml:44-50): storage.bind_mounts declare '${DOCKERDIR}/knvpn-client/${WG_CONFIG}' and '${DOCKERDIR}/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt', while docker-compose.yml.j2:37-39 mounts '{{ docker_dir }}/{{ container_prefix }}-vpn-client/{{ wireguard_config_file }}'. ${WG_CONFIG} is not a renderer variable (the template uses wireguard_config_file) and the renderer never reads bind_mounts at all. — fix: Rewrite bind_mounts to '${DOCKERDIR}/${CONTAINER_PREFIX}-vpn-client/${WIREGUARD_CONFIG_FILE}' (or Jinja), mark the wg conf mount required only for provider=custom, and make the generator use them to pre-create host paths.
- **env.required / env.optional do not match what the template consumes; generated .env.example is incomplete** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/module.yaml:29-36): required lists only VPN_PROVIDER and VPN_TYPE (VPN_TYPE has a template default). The truly required inputs (wireguard_private_key + wireguard_addresses for non-custom providers, wireguard_config_file for custom, docker_dir, vpn_ip when DNAT is on) are not declared, so 'kiwi-render validate' never warns and the rendered .env.example for examples/master.yaml contains no WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY/WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES/SERVER_*/VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT even though the compose references ${WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY} (verified). TZ in optional is dead (template reads 'timezone'); WIREGUARD_MTU in optional is never emitted. — fix: Declare required: [vpn_provider, docker_dir] plus conditional requirements, add WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY/WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES/SERVER_CITIES/SERVER_COUNTRIES/VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT/WIREGUARD_MTU/FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS to the env schema, and emit WIREGUARD_MTU in the template.
- **docker_dir has no default; silently renders root-level paths** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/docker-compose.yml.j2:37,39): With SilentUndefined, a node config without variables.docker_dir renders '- /kn-vpn-client/post-rules.txt:/iptables/post-rules.txt' with no warning. — fix: Use '{{ docker_dir | default('/home/user/docker') }}' or make docker_dir a validated required variable.
- **extra_dnat_rules loop relies on SilentUndefined** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2:25): '{% for rule in extra_dnat_rules %}' only works when the variable is undefined because the renderer's SilentUndefined.__iter__ returns []. Any port to a strict renderer (StrictUndefined, or a different engine) fails for every node that does not define the list. — fix: '{% for rule in extra_dnat_rules | default([]) %}'.
- **firewall.rules, network.needs_vpn_dnat and health are dead duplicates** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/module.yaml:12-27,52-55): kiwi-render.py never reads firewall.rules, network.needs_vpn_dnat, storage.bind_mounts or health (grep confirms no reference). firewall.rules repeats post-rules.txt.j2 in ${VAR} syntax and already differs from it (no NEW/DNAT handling), so the two will drift. — fix: Keep post-rules.txt.j2 as the single source and delete firewall.rules, or make the generator render firewall.rules and delete the template.
- **templates.env_example points to a file that does not exist** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/module.yaml:41): The module directory contains only docker-compose.yml.j2, module.yaml and post-rules.txt.j2; there is no .env.example (the renderer skips the key, so no error surfaces). — fix: Add the file or drop the key.
- **vpn_ip renders empty on the master without warning** (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kiwi-server/121dd16b-65a0-5d03-883f-f5c82b3badd1/scratchpad/upload/kiwi-network-docker/kiwi-v2/modules/vpn-client/post-rules.txt.j2:8,21-22,27): examples/master.yaml defines no vpn_ip; the header renders '# VPN_IP='. Harmless now, but if enable_vpn_dnat or extra_dnat_rules is ever set on such a node the rule renders '-d  -p tcp' and iptables fails. — fix: Guard the DNAT blocks with '{% if enable_vpn_dnat and vpn_ip %}' and have validate() require vpn_ip when either is set.

**Present live, missing in v2**

- Daily restart of the VPN client / stack: master cron '00 07 * * * user docker restart kmvpn-client' (kiwi-master/cron.d/km-docker-up:1); nodes '01 07 * * * ... kiwi-node.sh restart' / 'systemctl restart kiwi-gw' (kiwi-cloud/kiwi-node.sh:32, kiwi-node-gw/kiwi-node-gw.sh:28).
- Weekly 'docker compose down && up --pull always && docker image prune -f' update cron on master and nodes (same files, line 2 / :33 / :29).
- First-run generation of post-rules.txt from post-rules.txt.example via envsubst (DOCKER_SUBNET, DOCKER_SUBNET2, VPN_IP) in kiwi-node.sh:23-25 and kiwi-node-gw.sh:22-24; v2 renders at generate time but ships no deploy step that copies the rendered file to <docker_dir>/<prefix>-vpn-client/post-rules.txt.
- Provisioning of the WireGuard client config file ($WG_CONFIG, e.g. sh3.kiwi.conf) into the vpn-client directory with 0600 permissions (done today by kiwi-server roles/node-cloud/apply.sh:89); v2 only references the file name.
- Gluetun firewall env knobs used live: FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS="80,443" (cloud) and the commented FIREWALL_INPUT_PORTS / FIREWALL_OUTBOUND_SUBNETS / DOT_PRIVATE_ADDRESS / UNBLOCK lines on the gw node (docker-compose-kiwi-node.yml:17-21).
- Host-published ports on the vpn-client for netns-sharing containers (gw: 3129:3129 for JDownloader).
- sysctls net.ipv4.tcp_mtu_probing=1 / net.ipv4.tcp_base_mss=1024 on the gw vpn-client.
- /etc/hosts:/etc/hosts:ro mount on the cloud vpn-client.
- TCPMSS --clamp-mss-to-pmtu mangle rule active on the master (kiwi-master/kmvpn-client/post-rules.txt:1).
- Extra DNATs present live but absent from the v2 examples: gw 2222 -> nginx stream proxy (192.168.2.20:2222), 3129 -> nginx :3129 (JDownloader), 2224 -> knsftp 172.128.0.251:22 (kiwi-node-gw/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt.example:13-21); deployed cloud node adds 2222 -> nginx and game-server DNATs 7777 tcp/udp, 8888, 25565, 25567, 27016 to 172.129.0.100-.103 (kiwi-cloud/knvpn-client/post-rules.txt:23,33-43). extra_dnat_rules can express them but no example/preset carries them.
- knsftp service (jmcombs/sftp, 2224:22, 172.128.0.251) reachable through the vpn-client DNAT on the gw node; no v2 module.
- Named network with fixed host bridge name (name: knet-node, com.docker.network.bridge.name: knet-node) that the gateway iptables/ip-rule logic depends on.
- Routing of the wg-server's default route through the vpn-client (ip route add default via 172.64.0.2 in kiwi-master/kmvpn-server/start.sh:35-37) is only a comment-level rule in vpn-server/module.yaml (${VPN_CLIENT_IP}) with no start.sh.j2 template (renderer warns 'Template not found').
- Container names kmvpn-client/knvpn-client are referenced by the live nginx upstream 'server knvpn-client:9091' (kiwi-node-gw/knnginx/nginx.conf.example:41) and by v2's own downloader/module.yaml:64,66 proxy_pass 'http://knvpn-client:9091' — the rename to kn-vpn-client is not applied consistently.

**Suggested settings**

| key | type | required | default | label / help |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vpn_provider` | string | yes | custom | VPN provider — Gluetun VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER. 'custom' connects to the kiwi-master with a WireGuard config file (all nodes); 'mullvad' etc. uses a commercial provider with key/addresses (master). |
| `vpn_type` | enum |  | wireguard | VPN type — wireguard \| openvpn (only wireguard is used live). |
| `wireguard_config_file` | file |  |  | WireGuard client config — Required when vpn_provider is custom. The wg0.conf from your master; the generator writes it to <docker_dir>/<prefix>-vpn-client/<name>.conf (0600) and mounts it at /gluetun/wireguard/wg0.conf. Contains the private key: treat as secret. |
| `wireguard_private_key` | secret |  |  | WireGuard private key — Required when vpn_provider is not custom (master/Mullvad). Written to .env as WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY and referenced as ${WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY} in compose; never inlined. |
| `wireguard_addresses` | string |  |  | WireGuard address — Required when vpn_provider is not custom, e.g. 10.66.x.x/32 (WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES). |
| `server_countries` | string |  |  | Server countries — Comma-separated SERVER_COUNTRIES filter for commercial providers. Ignored for custom. |
| `server_cities` | string |  |  | Server cities — Comma-separated SERVER_CITIES filter (live master uses this one). |
| `vpn_endpoint_port` | int |  | 51820 | VPN endpoint port — VPN_ENDPOINT_PORT for commercial providers. Leave empty for custom so the port in the config file is used. |
| `wireguard_mtu` | int |  | 1412 | WireGuard MTU — WIREGUARD_MTU; must match the knet bridge MTU and the master's WG_MTU (1412 live). |
| `dot_providers` | string |  | google | DNS-over-TLS providers — Gluetun DOT_PROVIDERS; Pi-hole on the same node uses this container (<base>.2) as upstream. |
| `timezone` | string |  | Europe/Berlin | Timezone — Global node variable (TZ). |
| `docker_dir` | string | yes | /home/user/docker | Docker directory — Global node variable; host root for <prefix>-vpn-client/ (post-rules.txt and the WireGuard config). |
| `vpn_ip` | string |  |  | VPN mesh address — This node's 10.8.0.x address; required when enable_vpn_dnat is true or extra_dnat_rules is set (global node variable, unset on the master). |
| `enable_vpn_dnat` | bool |  | false | Expose nginx on the VPN address — DNAT tcp/80 and tcp/443 arriving on vpn_ip to the reverse-proxy container (requires the reverse-proxy module). Live: cloud node on, gw node on, master off. |
| `extra_dnat_rules` | list |  | [] | Extra DNAT rules — Items {comment, protocol: tcp\|udp, port, target: ip:port} forwarded from vpn_ip through the tunnel, e.g. 2224 -> sftp, 3129 -> jdownloader, game servers. |
| `firewall_vpn_input_ports` | string |  |  | Gluetun VPN input ports — FIREWALL_VPN_INPUT_PORTS, comma-separated; live cloud node uses 80,443 together with enable_vpn_dnat. |
| `published_ports` | list |  | [] | Host ports published on the VPN client — host:container entries, needed for containers that share this netns (gw node: 3129:3129 for JDownloader). |
| `sysctls` | list |  | [] | Container sysctls — gw node live: net.ipv4.tcp_mtu_probing=1, net.ipv4.tcp_base_mss=1024. |
| `extra_volumes` | list |  | [] | Extra bind mounts — cloud node live: /etc/hosts:/etc/hosts:ro. |
| `tcp_mss_clamp` | bool |  | false | Clamp TCP MSS to PMTU — Adds the mangle TCPMSS --clamp-mss-to-pmtu rule first in post-rules. Live master: on; nodes: off (commented). |

**Reverse-proxy blocks**: transmission.<hostname> → http://kn-vpn-client:9091 (websocket); jdownloader.<hostname> → http://kn-vpn-client:5800 (websocket)

**Host ports**: none inbound for the vpn-client itself (master and cloud node publish nothing on this service); 3129/tcp inbound only on gw nodes that run JDownloader in the vpn-client netns (live 'ports: - 3129:3129'); egress udp/<vpn_endpoint_port> (51820 live) from the host to the WireGuard endpoint: the master's public IP for nodes, the Mullvad server for the master; plus egress 853/tcp for DNS-over-TLS (DOT_PROVIDERS) through the tunnel; inside the VPN mesh only (not host firewall): tcp/80 and tcp/443 to vpn_ip when enable_vpn_dnat is on, and each extra_dnat_rules port (2222, 2224, 3129, game ports live)

**Host directories**: <docker_dir>/<container_prefix>-vpn-client/  (e.g. /home/user/docker/kn-vpn-client or /home/user/docker/km-vpn-client; live names are knvpn-client / kmvpn-client, so migrate or symlink); <docker_dir>/<container_prefix>-vpn-client/post-rules.txt  (must exist as a FILE before 'compose up'; Docker otherwise creates a directory at that path and gluetun fails); <docker_dir>/<container_prefix>-vpn-client/<wireguard_config_file>  (nodes only, 0600, contains the WireGuard private key; same file-vs-directory caveat)

<details><summary>Template variables (21)</summary>

- `container_prefix` — docker-compose.yml.j2:6,8,37,39 (service key, container_name, host dir name) — required — Renderer-provided (km|kn). Produces km-vpn-client / kn-vpn-client; live containers are kmvpn-client / knvpn-client.
- `vpn_provider` — docker-compose.yml.j2:12 (VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER) — required — No default; renders VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER= (empty) if unset, gluetun then fails. Live: mullvad on master, custom on nodes. module.yaml declares it as env.required VPN_PROVIDER; validate() matches the lowercase name.
- `vpn_type` — docker-compose.yml.j2:13 (VPN_TYPE) — default wireguard — Declared env.required VPN_TYPE in module.yaml but the template has a default, so it is effectively optional.
- `wireguard_private_key` — docker-compose.yml.j2:14 (WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY) — Required only when vpn_provider != custom (master/Mullvad). Emitted unconditionally; on nodes it renders WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY= (empty). examples/master.yaml passes the literal ${WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY} so compose reads it from .env, but the generated .env.example never lists it.
- `wireguard_addresses` — docker-compose.yml.j2:15 (WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES) — Required only when vpn_provider != custom. Emitted unconditionally (empty on nodes).
- `server_cities` — docker-compose.yml.j2:16-18 ({% if server_cities %} SERVER_CITIES) — Live master uses SERVER_CITIES=$SERVER_CITIES (kiwi-master/docker-compose-kiwi-master.yml:15).
